least labels, its dominance growth along a row, and the drift
identity; the \emph{self-dual $\theta$}, with the character's
two-sided form cap, its square's channel list at multiplicities,
dimensions and Casimirs, the base $c_1$, and $\theta$'s own
content list; the \emph{class data}, the vertex law's classes
over the root folds with the winding floors per class; the
\emph{evaluation}, the unit-coefficient read with orthonormal
characters and the coefficient carrier's evaluation identity and
integration by parts; and
the \emph{presentation}, the evaluation's computation at stated
factor lists. A \emph{series} enters with one further tier: the
declared residue with every field a polynomial-pair read in it at
stated reach clearances, the banded families the fusion-closed
interiors with the $\theta$ content list among their data, and the
generic lift's discipline; a fixed member
enters at the member fields alone, its reads displayed finite
evaluations. The label calculus above instantiates every field,
each instantiating statement the field's own derivation, and a
further member's or series' instantiation is its own construction
of the field data, the chain reading the fields alone: the
classification's members each enter with every field a derived
read at their weight tables, their positive lists, alternants,
assembly, character identity, fusion rows, pairing spans and
defining tables the fields' own instantiations, the count and
involution fields at the member constructions
(Lemma~\ref{lem:memberdata}), and every chain derivation reads
the fusion data through the fields, at every member at once.
\end{construction}

\begin{construction}[The lattice interface]\label{con:lattice}
The carrier's region data are one interface, and every derivation
reads the lattice through its fields: a set of \emph{links} and a set
of \emph{vertices}, each link with one tail and one head, the
orientation the labels' duality read; the
\emph{plaquettes}, each boundary a cyclic word of four distinct
oriented links; \emph{simplicity}, two vertices sharing at most one link; the
\emph{even-cycle read}, every cycle's length even, a two-coloring of
the vertices its witness datum; the $d$ \emph{directions}, a
partition of the links with a transverse-cut family per direction,
each cut crossed by its own direction's links alone; the
\emph{translation action} on a
torus window, permuting links, vertices and plaquettes and preserving
directions, two shifts composing to the keys' one composite with
the directions' composites one map at either order; and the signed coordinate
permutations $B_d$, permuting the link and plaquette sets with
orientation reversal dualizing labels. The fields read at two
instantiation families. The region fields, the links, the vertices,
the plaquettes, simplicity, and the even-cycle read with its
two-coloring witness, are every carrier window's, the regions of
Definition~\ref{def:carrier}'s directed family; the direction data
with their transverse cuts read at both families. The translation
action and the signed coordinate permutations are the torus
family's, the windows of the momentum fibering: there a
contractible cycle's wrap count is even, so its length reads even
at the coordinate parity, while a winding cycle reads the side's
parity and is odd at odd side, so a torus is a carrier of the
interface's fields off the even-cycle read, its counts reaching the
directed windows at the decimation's window-free reads
(Lemma~\ref{lem:stableentries}) over the momentum fibering's stated
family, the odd tori at side five or beyond. A \emph{label-graph isomorphism}
between two supports is a bijection of their links and vertices
preserving incidence, orientation and labels. The
dimension enters through the direction count alone, its reads (the
neighbor count $2d$, the per-link and per-vertex plaquette
counts, the per-direction momentum products, the
charged direction sums) polynomial in it.
\end{construction}

\begin{definition}[The carrier]\label{def:carrier}
The sector is an
indexed family with multiplicities, built from the fusion
interface's fields alone (Construction~\ref{con:fusion}). A
\emph{configuration} $a$ assigns a nonunit label to each link of its
\emph{support}, finitely many links at a natural count
(Construction~\ref{con:labels}); its \emph{content} is
$\sum_{\ell\in\mathrm{supp}\,a}C_2(a_\ell)$, positive at every
configuration; a vertex of $\mathrm{touched}(a)$, the support's
endpoints, is \emph{occupied} where the incident labels'
product, dualized on incoming links, reads an invariant count of at
least one, and
$a$ is \emph{occupied} where every touched vertex is. The window
index is the occupied configurations at the region and the cutoff,
and the window is the unit's line with their fibers:
\[
\begin{gathered}
\mathrm{Idx}(\Lambda,C)=\Big\{a\ \text{occupied}\ \Big|\
\mathrm{supp}\,a\subseteq\Lambda,\
\sum_{\ell\in\mathrm{supp}\,a}C_2(a_\ell)\le C\Big\},\\
\mathrm{Sect}(\Lambda,C)=\mathbb P\,\mathbf 1\ \oplus
\bigoplus_{a\in\mathrm{Idx}(\Lambda,C)}\mathrm{Fib}(a),
\qquad
\mathrm{Fib}(a)=\bigotimes_{v\in\mathrm{touched}(a)}\mathrm{inv}(v,a),
\end{gathered}
\]
with
$\mathrm{inv}(v,a)$ the vertex's intertwiner space, the incident
labels' product's invariants at a stated independent spanning
list, incoming links dualized (Lemma~\ref{lem:lowerspan}'s
collected reads at Definition~\ref{def:blockcount}'s count), and
$\mathrm{vmult}(v,a)$ its count, the vertex intertwiner
multiplicity, a natural at every occupied vertex. The
pairing is definitional as well: $\mathrm{gram}$ is the orthogonal direct sum
of the unit line's pairing at one and the fibers' own pairings,
per configuration the invariant lists' power pairing at the links'
cleared block scale, one factor $[\,1:d_{a_\ell}\,]$ per support
link: positive definiteness is by construction, the power pairing
a coordinate fold at positive scales. The windows are directed under
$(\Lambda,C)\le(\Lambda',C')$, the inclusions
$\mathrm{Sect}(\Lambda,C)\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Sect}(\Lambda',C')$ are the
summand injections mapping the unit line and each fiber to
themselves, compatible under
composition, and the carrier is
their colimit
\[
\mathrm{Sect}_\infty\;=\;\varinjlim_{(\Lambda,C)}\ \mathrm{Sect}(\Lambda,C).
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[A window is finite-dimensional, and its dimension is a
count]\label{prop:windowfinite}
For a region $\Lambda$ of finitely many links,
$\mathrm{Idx}(\Lambda,C)$ is finite, each $\mathrm{Fib}(a)$ is
finite-dimensional, the dimension is one at the unit line alone,
and at an occupied index
\[
\dim\mathrm{Sect}(\Lambda,C)\;=\;1+\sum_{a\in\mathrm{Idx}(\Lambda,C)}
\ \prod_{v\in\mathrm{touched}(a)}\mathrm{vmult}(v,a)\ ,
\]
\emph{a count computed from the structure constants $N^c_{ab}$}, the vertex
multiplicity being $m\{a,b,c\}=N^{\bar c}_{ab}$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:repring}).
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
For dominant $\lambda$ the read $\langle\lambda,2\rho\rangle$ is a fold
of natural products by its shape (Lemma~\ref{lem:casimir}'s display), so
$C_2(\lambda)\ge\langle\lambda,\lambda\rangle$ and the labels of content at most
$C$ satisfy $\langle\lambda,\lambda\rangle\le C$: finitely many. At a
reduced shape, at most $r$ rows deep, the fold
$\langle\lambda,2\rho\rangle$ reads at or beyond the degree, the last
place's pairs the rows' own reads, so $d_f\,Q(\lambda)$ clears
$d_f\,k$ (Definition~\ref{def:c2hat}) and an enumeration at a stated
cutoff closes on the degrees at or below it. A
configuration of $\mathrm{Idx}(\Lambda,C)$ assigns to the links of its
support, within $\Lambda$'s finitely many, labels from that finite
set, so
$\mathrm{Idx}(\Lambda,C)$ is finite; and the enumeration reads the
cutoff per link: a configuration's content is the fold over its
links' reads, each a natural, so a partial assignment's content
sits at or below every completion's and the enumeration refuses a
partial beyond the cutoff with every completion refused beside it,
the pruned walk and the whole product's filter one list. Each $\mathrm{vmult}(v,a)$ is a
natural at an occupied configuration
and the product defining $\mathrm{Fib}(a)$ has finitely many factors, so
$\mathrm{Fib}(a)$ is finite-dimensional and the displayed sum is a finite sum of
finite products of structure-constant reads. Both indices are needed: the
content bound alone leaves a loop free to sit at every translate, all at one
content, so the region is what makes the count finite.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Fusion rows lie in content balls]\label{prop:fusionfinite}
Every constituent of $a\otimes b$ has
highest weight below the Cartan product $a+b$ in the dominance order (a
constituent's top content is a content of the product, contents of a
tensor product add over the factors, and a block's contents sit at or
below its top (Lemma~\ref{lem:lowerspan})), and
$\hat c_2$ (Definition~\ref{def:c2hat}) is increasing there (at
$\mu\preceq\lambda$ dominant,
$C_2(\mu)+\sum_k\delta_k\,h_k=C_2(\lambda)$ at the dominance order's
prefix gaps $\delta_k$ and the gaps $h_k$ of the sorted list
$\lambda+\mu+2u$ at $u$ the unit set's sorted display
(Construction~\ref{con:places}), every summand two naturals' product), so
\[
\hat c_2(c)\ \le\ \hat c_2(a+b)\ =\ \hat c_2(a)+\hat c_2(b)
+\big[\,2d_f\,\langle a,b\rangle:2d_f^{\,2}\,\big]
\qquad\text{for every nonunit member }c\text{ of the row},
\]
the unit member, one exactly at $b=\bar a$
(Proposition~\ref{prop:repring}), the Casimir's kernel point, inside
every content ball outright;
the cross term twice the bilinear read
($C_2(a+b)=C_2(a)+C_2(b)+2\langle a,b\rangle$),
$d_f\,\langle a,b\rangle$ the weight lattice's integer read, every term
one form read against itself, the pair at one value across the
form's scales
(Definition~\ref{def:ground}; Proposition~\ref{prop:form}).
So a fusion row lies in a content ball, and content balls are finite, which is
what makes $\mathrm{fuse}$, $\Phi$ and the one-link step
finite objects. This bound and $\Phi$ do different jobs: the bound
makes the row a finite set, while $\Phi$ is the \emph{attained} maximum over it,
and the window index uses $\Phi$, since a bound composed from the two
contents' root sum forces a rounding whose composition depends on the order
taken.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[Every element has a least window, read off its index
support]\label{prop:leastwindow}
An
$A\in\mathrm{Sect}_\infty$ is represented by some $x\in\mathrm{Sect}(\Lambda,C)$,
a unit coordinate with its family of fiber components over the
index. The \emph{index support}
$\mathrm{ix}(A)$, the member configurations, is representative-free,
because the
inclusions are summand injections and map the unit line and each
component to themselves; the unit coordinate is at home in every
window, multiplying as the scalar it is, every window its own. An
element whose index support has members reads its window data over
them:
\[
\Lambda_A=\bigcup_{a\in\mathrm{ix}(A)}\mathrm{supp}\,a ,
\qquad
C_A=\max_{a\in\mathrm{ix}(A)}\mathrm{content}(a)
\]
are one value at every representative, and
$\mathrm{supp}(A)=(\Lambda_A,C_A)$ is the least window whose
image contains $A$'s components. Both are \emph{counts off the index}: the links the element
occupies and its members' largest content. Multiplication therefore has a
window bound stated on the index alone,
\[
A\cdot\mathrm{Sect}(\Lambda,C)\ \subseteq\
\mathrm{Sect}\big(\Lambda_A\cup\Lambda,\ \Phi(C_A,C)\big).
\]
A probe is an element of the
carrier, and its window is this one.
\end{proposition}

\begin{definition}[The product and the involution]\label{def:algebra}
$\mathrm{Sect}_\infty$ is a unital $\ast$-algebra. A factor of the
unit coordinate alone multiplies as its scalar, every window its
own (Proposition~\ref{prop:leastwindow}); at $A$ and $u$ whose
index supports have members, at the least windows
$\mathrm{supp}(A)=(\Lambda_A,C_A)$ and
$\mathrm{supp}(u)=(\Lambda_u,C_u)$
(Proposition~\ref{prop:leastwindow}), the \emph{target window} is
$\mathrm{Sect}_0:=\mathrm{Sect}\big(\Lambda_A\cup\Lambda_u,\
\Phi(C_A,C_u)\big)$, its \emph{window list} the unit line's vector
with the fibers' stated lists, the pairing's Gram of
unequal-membered determinant at the lists' independence
(Definition~\ref{def:carrier}; Lemma~\ref{lem:lowerspan}), and the
product $A\cdot u$ is the
target window's element at the reads
\[
\mathrm{gram}\big(A\cdot u,\;v\big)\;=\;\mathrm{Eval}\big(\bar A\,\bar u\,v\big)
\qquad\text{at every window-list member }v ,
\]
the element the reads' solve, the adjugate column against the
Gram's determinant (Definition~\ref{def:elim}), one finite ground
computation per read, the right side the \emph{unit read}: $\mathrm{Eval}$ reads its
argument's unit coordinate in the label index
(Definition~\ref{def:carrier}), the invariant count's own read
(Proposition~\ref{prop:repring}), a finite ground computation in the
structure constants alone. Its matrix elements between
summands, $\mathrm{gram}(x,A\cdot y)$ for $x,y$ window-list members,
are the label-index reads
of Proposition~\ref{prop:repring} on the changed edge alone, and the
magnetic form is built from them. The involution is contour reversal, descending from the words' own
involution, $\mathrm{Eval}$ one value under it.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[The product reads the pairing identity, and the algebra laws
are structure-constant identities]\label{prop:algebra}
The product of Definition~\ref{def:algebra} reads the pairing
identity against every element,
$\mathrm{gram}_\infty(A\cdot u,\,v)=\mathrm{Eval}(\bar A\,\bar u\,v)$
at every $v$, at the colimit pairing $\mathrm{gram}_\infty$, two
elements' read at a shared window, one value at every window; it is
the target window's one element at that identity, and every window
at or beyond the target window solves the identity to it; the product of two window elements
sits in a window, and in the colimit commutativity, associativity and the unit
are the identities
\[
N^c_{ab}=N^c_{ba},\qquad
\sum_eN^e_{ab}N^d_{ec}=\sum_fN^f_{bc}N^d_{af},\qquad
N^c_{a\mathbf 1}=\delta_{ac} .
\]
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Take $A$ and $u$ whose index supports have members, with
$\mathrm{Sect}_0=\mathrm{Sect}\big(\Lambda_A\cup\Lambda_u,\ \Phi(C_A,C_u)\big)$
the target window of Definition~\ref{def:algebra} at the least
windows (Proposition~\ref{prop:leastwindow}); a factor of the unit
coordinate alone multiplies as its scalar, its identity the
scalar's read.

\emph{The functional reads equal members off $\mathrm{Sect}_0$.} $\mathrm{Eval}$ reads the unit
coordinate, so
$\mathrm{Eval}(\bar A\bar uv)$ is of equal members unless the labels of $v$ occur among the
constituents of $\bar A$'s against $\bar u$'s, dual labels at one
link set and one content each (Definition~\ref{def:c2hat}). Those constituents sit on the links
of $\Lambda_A\cup\Lambda_u$ and, by Proposition~\ref{prop:fusionfinite}, at
content at most $\Phi(C_A,C_u)$: they are summands of $\mathrm{Sect}_0$. So
$v\mapsto\mathrm{Eval}(\bar A\bar uv)$ is of equal members on every summand beyond $\mathrm{Sect}_0$.

\emph{On $\mathrm{Sect}_0$ the identity is the definition's solved read.} $\mathrm{Sect}_0$ is
finite-dimensional (Proposition~\ref{prop:windowfinite}) and its window list is
independent with its pairing Gram of unequal-membered determinant
(Definition~\ref{def:carrier}; Lemma~\ref{lem:lowerspan}), so
the identity's reads against the list solve the one
$x\in \mathrm{Sect}_0$ with
$\mathrm{gram}(x,v)=\mathrm{Eval}(\bar A\bar uv)$ at every $v\in \mathrm{Sect}_0$, the adjugate
column against the determinant
(Lemma~\ref{lem:genericlift}(ii); Definition~\ref{def:elim}); two
stated lists spanning $\mathrm{Sect}_0$ solve one element, each
solution reading the identity against the other's members, the
solutions' gap at equal members against every member with its
self-pairing that unit at the positive pairing, so
a presentation's wiring list reads the determination at its own
Gram. For $v$ in any larger window, $\mathrm{gram}(x,v)$ reads
only $v$'s $\mathrm{Sect}_0$-summands, $\mathrm{gram}$ being the orthogonal direct sum, and
both sides are of equal members off $\mathrm{Sect}_0$ as above. So the product reads the
identity against every $v$; a window at or beyond the target reads
the identity at its own list to one further solved element, each
reading the identity against every $v$, the two agreeing against
every element, one element at the positive pairing on the larger
window; and the product sits in $\mathrm{Sect}_0$ and descends to
the colimit.

\emph{The laws.} $\mathrm{Sect}_\infty$'s elements are character polynomials
and $\mathrm{Eval}$ reads their product's unit coefficient, so the product
just constructed is the character polynomials' own, commutative and
associative with the unit label as its unit. Read in the label index those three statements are
the displayed identities on $N$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:repring}). The pairing and the link Laplacians are
compatible with the summand injections, so they descend to $\mathrm{Sect}_\infty$
and restrict to every window: a pairing of two colimit elements is read at any
common window, one value at every window.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Matrix elements are reads at the support's reach]
\label{lem:stableentries}
Let $x,y$ be elements of a window with joint support
$S:=\Lambda_x\cup\Lambda_y$. Then $\mathrm{gram}(x,y)$ and
$\langle x,Ey\rangle$ read the index data on $S$ alone, and the magnetic
row reads only the plaquettes meeting $S$'s vertex neighborhood: at
$\partial p$ sharing a vertex with $S$, the entry
$\langle x,\chi_\theta(U_{\partial p})\,y\rangle$ is the changed-edge read
of Definition~\ref{def:algebra} at the labels incident to $p$; at
$\partial p$ off that neighborhood it factorizes,
\[
\langle x,\chi_\theta(U_{\partial p})\,y\rangle
=\mathrm{Eval}(\bar xy)\cdot\mathrm{Eval}(\chi_\theta),
\qquad
\mathrm{Eval}(\chi_\theta)=\delta_{\theta\mathbf 1}
\quad(\text{Proposition~\ref{prop:repring}}) .
\]
Consequently a label-graph isomorphism of the supports' vertex
neighborhoods transports every pairing, electric and magnetic read across
windows: each entry is one polynomial-pair read in the residue, window-free
once a window's region
contains the neighborhood. The \emph{locality band} is that reach,
the plaquette neighborhood's: a magnetic row's entries off it are
the factorized reads at the unit character, the sum's unit, so
every pencil row reads within one band of its support, at every
window.
\begin{proof}
$\mathrm{gram}$ is the orthogonal sum of the fibers' pairings at matched
configurations and $E$ scales each summand by its content, both index
reads on $S$ (Definitions~\ref{def:carrier},~\ref{def:pencil}); the
product $\bar xy$ is supported on $S$ at bounded content
(Proposition~\ref{prop:leastwindow}). For the factorization: two elements
on disjoint links and vertices have $\mathrm{Fib}$ data at disjoint
vertex sets and label data at disjoint links, so the product's index
summand at $(a,b)$ is the concatenation of the two summands, each
vertex's $\mathrm{vmult}$ reading its own incident labels; a concatenation
occupies a link exactly where a part does, so the
product's unit coordinate is the unit coordinates' product,
and $\mathrm{Eval}(\chi_\theta)=\delta_{\theta\mathbf 1}$, the Kronecker
delta at two labels, is of equal members, the far
plaquette's term with it. The near case is Definition~\ref{def:algebra}'s read, a
fusion computation at the labels incident to $p$.
\end{proof}
\end{lemma}

\begin{construction}[The coefficient carrier]\label{con:coeff}
The carrier's vertex-free companion drops the invariance constraint: per
link the \emph{coefficient family}
$\bigoplus_\lambda W_\lambda\otimes W_\lambda^\vee$, one block per label
(Construction~\ref{con:labels}; the blocks and duals of
Lemmas~\ref{lem:blockirr},~\ref{lem:dualread}),
per window the tensor product of its links' families over the window index
of Definition~\ref{def:carrier}, the involution the blocks' dual
exchange with the unit block fixed.
$\mathrm{Eval}$ extends as the per-link unit coefficient: a product of
coefficient elements decomposes by the fusion data, and its unit
coefficient is the invariant line's read on both data, the vector
datum's component at the orthogonal split
(Lemma~\ref{lem:blockcount}(i); Lemma~\ref{lem:orthsplit}) against the
functional datum's value on the line, the component the projection
fold's read at the line's member (Lemma~\ref{lem:lowerspan}).  Two
one-block elements pair at the \emph{block orthogonality},
\[
d_\lambda\,\mathrm{Eval}\big(\,\overline{u\otimes f}\ (u'\otimes f')\,\big)
\;=\;\langle u,u'\rangle\,\langle f,f'\rangle\ ,
\]
the product a coefficient of the dual block against the block, its
unit channel one line (Proposition~\ref{prop:repring}'s unit read;
Lemma~\ref{lem:blockirr}(ii),(iii)) at the coevaluation's tensor, and
the display the projection read at two adjugate identities: the
coevaluation against a member pair reads the factors' pairing, once
per datum, and its self-pairing reads the block's
count against the determinants (Lemma~\ref{lem:dualread}(i)); two
blocks' elements pair at equal members, the unit channel vacant at
distinct labels (Proposition~\ref{prop:repring}).  The
\emph{evaluation identity} $\sum_jU_{ij}\bar U_{kj}=\delta_{ik}$ is
the dual pairing's read: the evaluation's tensor is invariant
(Lemma~\ref{lem:dualread}(i)'s equivariance), so its coefficient
family is its unit component's constants, the Kronecker read entry by
entry.  $\mathrm{gram}$ is the orthogonal direct sum of the blocks'
standard inner products at the cleared block scale
$[\,1:d_\lambda\,]$: positive definiteness is by construction at the
positive scales, the gram is the evaluation's own pairing,
$\mathrm{gram}(x,y)=\mathrm{Eval}(\bar x\,y)$, the block orthogonality
collected over the summands, the multiplication walks across it at a
coefficient element $w$,
$\mathrm{gram}(w\,x,\,y)=\mathrm{gram}(x,\,\bar w\,y)$, the products'
exchange and regrouping at $\mathrm{Eval}$'s one value under the
involution (Definition~\ref{def:algebra};
Proposition~\ref{prop:algebra}), and the carrier sits inside
isometrically at its own gram (Definition~\ref{def:carrier}): a
configuration's state contracts the links' block coefficients at
the vertices' invariant lists, $\mathrm{Eval}$ factors over the
support's links at disjoint variables
(Lemma~\ref{lem:stableentries}), and two states' pairing collects
the block orthogonality link by link, the links' scales against
the slot pairings' product regrouped at the vertices' lists
(Definition~\ref{def:ground}), the fiber gram's own display.  A generator acts within each block (a matrix
coefficient of $\lambda$
differentiates to one), so $\mathrm{Eval}\circ X_a$ reads equal members at every argument, the
unit block the constants' line, and the pairing integrates by parts,
the two reads $\langle X_ax,\,y\rangle$ and $\langle x,\,X_ay\rangle$
balance partners ($X_a$ a derivation at one value
under the involution, $X_a\bar x=\overline{X_ax}$). Consequently every finite family
of coefficient vectors' Gram is positive semidefinite
(Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}): a sum of squares
$\sum_i\|T_ix\|^2$, each $T_i$ a generator action or a coefficient
multiplication, is a fold of squares, and where the summed operator
maps the carrier to itself the read restricts to every window.
The pairing's squared Cauchy--Schwarz is the two-member instance
at the coordinate folds, its gap a fold of squares displayed once
at the cross pairs $c_{ij}$, each joined to its exchange's
balance partner at the memberwise swap
(Definition~\ref{def:elim}),
\[
\Big(\sum_i u_iv_i\Big)^{2}
+\sum_{i<j}\big(\,c_{ij}+\check c_{ji}\,\big)^{2}
\;=\;\Big(\sum_i u_i^2\Big)\Big(\sum_i v_i^2\Big)
\qquad\text{at}\quad c_{ij}:=u_iv_j\ ,
\]
the cross monomials $u_iu_jv_iv_j$ entering each member once per
place pair, so two vectors' squared pairing sits at or below their
self-pairings' product, the comparison's witness the displayed
fold; a finite family of paired slots reads it at the slots'
joined coordinate list, one list.
\end{construction}

\begin{definition}[The electric and magnetic operators, and the pencil]
\label{def:pencil}
$E$ is the electric operator, diagonal in the label index, scaling
a configuration's summand by its content and reading the unit line
as its kernel, so $E$ is positive semidefinite, every content positive
(Definition~\ref{def:carrier});
$\Delta_\ell$ is its per-link summand, scaling by $C_2(a_\ell)$, so
$E=\sum_\ell\Delta_\ell$; $M$ is the plaquette multiplication
$\sum_p\chi_\theta(U_{\partial p})$ with $\theta=\mathrm{adj}$, the sum over
the region's plaquettes, $\#p$ their count. The \emph{pencil} is the family
of form pairs $(\alpha E:\beta M)$ with $H$ their site datum at the
join
\[
\alpha\,E\;=\;\beta\,M+H\big([\alpha\!:\!\beta]\big) ,\qquad
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb N
\]
over the end-weight pairs of
Proposition~\ref{prop:segment} with the two ends' one-member
instances, an end's pair reading its one member alone, the contact
end's datum the magnetic member's balance partner. On a window $\varepsilon_0$ is the
pencil's floor and the \emph{level gap} $\tilde E$ its witness, the
operator gap at $\varepsilon_0\,G+\tilde E=H$ with $\tilde E$
positive semidefinite,
$G$ the window pairing's gram (Definition~\ref{def:carrier};
Lemma~\ref{lem:split}, the floor the least located root);
$\psi$ denotes a window ground, a kernel point of
$\tilde E$, every read at $\psi$ quantifying over the grounds, and
$\omega=\langle\psi,\cdot\,\psi\rangle$. The gap chain reads a
coupling in its \emph{root coordinate}: the weights
$[\,q^2:p^2\,]$ at the pair $\tau=[\,p:q\,]$, $q\,\tau=p$ the
cross-multiplied tie, a cell's reads taken at pair $\tau$, the
pair-$\tau$ rays the coordinate's own points; toward the contact end the dual coordinate weights the couplings
$[\,\sigma:1\,]$ at pair $\sigma$. The \emph{word sector} is
$\mathrm{Sect}_\infty\,\psi$, the image of multiplication by $\psi$
(Definition~\ref{def:algebra}).
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[The base is a closed segment]\label{prop:segment}
A coupling is an \emph{end-weight pair} $[\alpha\!:\!\beta]$ at
$\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb N$, taken up to rescaling: $\alpha$ weights
the free end's electric member $E$ and $\beta$ the contact end's
magnetic member $M$,
the coupling pencil the pair $(\alpha E:\beta M)$'s site datum at
the displayed join. The base
$\mathcal C$ is the couplings with the two \emph{ends}, the
one-member weightings: the \emph{free end}, the electric member
alone, $H=E$ there, and the \emph{contact end}, the magnetic
alone, $H$ the member's balance partner. Every coupling pair is interior,
both weights present by the shape of its data, and the base is the
closed segment, the free and the contact end its two endpoints. A spectral quantity of the
pencil homogeneous of degree one scales by one factor along a ray,
so the pair of two such reads is one value on the ray, the
homogeneity principle's read (Definition~\ref{def:ground}), and
descends to $\mathcal C$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The pair's site datum is additive over its members and degree-one
in each weight by the join's shape, so
rescaling the weights by a natural $u$ rescales the pencil by $u$,
and scaling an operator by $u$ scales its spectrum by $u$: a
degree-one spectral quantity acquires that one factor, so both
components of a pair of two such reads acquire it and the pair
reads one value across the ray, the homogeneity principle's
read, every rescaling a ground rescaling and so
order-preserving by the type. The weight data up to that rescaling
are the two-member rays and the two one-member classes: the
segment's interior and its two ends.
\end{proof}

%=====================================================================

%=====================================================================
\section{The statement}

Write $H_k$ for the harmonic numbers
(Definition~\ref{def:ground}), $r$ for the member's derived
residue, the pairing's own fold
(Construction~\ref{con:sertables}; Proposition~\ref{prop:anchor}),
the $A$-series' declared scalar its instance with the successor
name the fundamental count $d_f=r+1$
(Construction~\ref{con:places}), $\mathcal C$ for the
closed segment of Proposition~\ref{prop:segment}, $E_0$ for the module count at
the free end (Proposition~\ref{prop:E0}), $\mathcal K$ for the invariant
(Definition~\ref{def:K}), and $c_1$ for the
multiplicity of the adjoint in $\mathrm{adj}\otimes\mathrm{adj}$,
$\theta$'s coroot-support count: two at the $A$-series at
$r\ge2$, one at its first member and at every further member of
the classification
(Construction~\ref{con:xfusion}; Proposition~\ref{prop:row}).

\begin{proposition}[The module energy is a link count]\label{prop:E0}
At the free end the module $\chi_\theta(U_{\partial p})$ is an eigenvector of
the pencil, with $E_0=4$ at every member and on either carrier.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
At the free end the pencil is its electric member $E$ alone
(Definition~\ref{def:pencil}; Proposition~\ref{prop:segment}), and
$E$ is diagonal in the label index, scaling the summand at $a$ by
$\mathrm{content}(a)=\sum_\ell C_2(a_\ell)$ (Definition~\ref{def:pencil}). In
that index $\chi_\theta(U_{\partial p})$ is the configuration assigning $\theta$
to each link of $\partial p$, so it is
an eigenvector of $E$ with eigenvalue its content, the fold over
$\partial p$'s links, four regardless of sharing
(Construction~\ref{con:lattice}): that
content is $4\,C_2(\theta)$; read against $C_2(\mathrm{adj})$
(Proposition~\ref{prop:form}) it is $4$. On the coefficient
carrier the module is the $\theta$-blocks' coefficient at the
boundary's four links, each link's $\Delta_\ell$ scaling its block
at $C_2(\theta)$ (a matrix coefficient of $\lambda$ differentiates
to one, Construction~\ref{con:coeff}) with every further link's
summand at the sum's unit, so the eigen-read is the four-link fold
there as well, one count on either carrier.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The free end's level is the fundamental loop]
\label{lem:freeend}
At the free end the window cut of Definition~\ref{def:K} is a diagonal read, and
\[
\mathcal K\;=\;\hat c_2(f)\;=\;[\,r(r+2):2d_f^{\,2}\,]
\qquad\text{at the free end},
\]
exact and symbolic in the residue, one value at every window of the
directed family (Definition~\ref{def:carrier}); at
a member the level is the member's floor, the least nonunit
$\hat c_2$ of its end lists (Lemma~\ref{lem:casfloor}), the
display its $A$-series read, the proof's two reads at the
floor's label.
\begin{proof}
At the free end the pencil is its electric member $E$ alone,
diagonal in the label index with the
contents as eigenvalues (Definition~\ref{def:pencil}). Every
configuration's content is positive
(Definition~\ref{def:carrier}), so the ground is the unit's line,
$\psi$ the algebra's unit
(Definition~\ref{def:algebra}) and $E$'s kernel point
(Definition~\ref{def:pencil}), the floor of equal members there and the
level gap reading $\tilde E=E$, and
the word sector is the window itself.
The cut's pair $(\tilde E^{\,2}:E_0\kappa\tilde E)$ is then
diagonal, the unit summand its kernel's and the
entries $c\,\langle c:E_0\kappa\rangle$ over the configurations'
contents $c$: the cut
at $\kappa$ holds exactly where every occupied configuration's
content is at least $E_0\kappa$.

\emph{The floor.} At an occupied configuration every touched vertex
reads an invariant count of at least one (Definition~\ref{def:carrier});
at a vertex
meeting exactly one support link an invariant line would be an
equivariant line between the unit block and a nonunit one, and an
equivariant line forces one label
(Proposition~\ref{prop:repring}; Lemma~\ref{lem:blockirr}(iii)),
against the support's nonunit datum. So an occupied configuration's
links meet each of their vertices at least twice; a finite graph of
minimum degree two contains a cycle (a forest has a leaf); every
cycle is even, and at least four, the graph simple (the even-cycle
and simplicity reads of Construction~\ref{con:lattice}); and each
occupied link
reads content at least the member's floor, $\hat c_2(f)$ the
$A$-read (Lemma~\ref{lem:casfloor}).
Every occupied configuration's content is therefore at least
four times the floor.

\emph{Attainment.} The configuration assigning the floor's label
$\omega$ around one plaquette's
boundary is occupied: each corner reads one
outgoing $\omega$ against one incoming,
$\mathrm{vmult}=N^{\mathbf 1}_{\omega\bar\omega}=1$
(Proposition~\ref{prop:repring}; Definition~\ref{def:carrier}, incoming
links dualized), at content four times the floor exactly.

So the cut holds at every $\kappa$ at or below the floor and
fails beyond, the
loop's entry $c\,\langle c:E_0\kappa\rangle$ on its lower side at
$c$ four times the floor: with
$E_0=4$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:E0}), the level is the member's
floor, and the
contents are window-free data, so the read is one diagonal computation at
every window.
\end{proof}

\end{lemma}

\begin{definition}[The invariant]\label{def:K}
$\mathcal K$ is the pencil's level read against the module count $E_0$ of
Proposition~\ref{prop:E0}, given by its window cuts: at a coupling $v$, a
window $\Lambda$ and a pair $\kappa=[\,p:q\,]$,
\[
\mathcal K_\Lambda(v)\ \ge\ \kappa
\qquad\textbf{is}\qquad
\big(q\,\tilde E^{\,2}\big)_\Lambda
:\big(E_0\,p\,\tilde E\big)_\Lambda
\ \text{ positive semidefinite},
\]
the pair clearing its pencil to integer coefficients, a
positive rescaling fixing the count (Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}),
with $\tilde E$ the level gap of Definition~\ref{def:pencil} read on the word sector
(Theorem~\ref{thm:coeffone}), and $(\cdot)_\Lambda$ the compression to the
window's word sector, taken of the detector form whole: the count reads the square's compression, a compression keeping the
positive-semidefinite read (Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}). The display
quantifies over the window grounds (Definition~\ref{def:pencil}),
the ground freedom's quotient read: at a ground the compression is
the detector pair's pullback along word images spanning the
sector, a spanning list by elimination (Definition~\ref{def:elim}),
a combination reading its image vector's pair and a kernel
combination reading the sum's unit, so the pulled-back pair is
positive semidefinite exactly with the pair on the word sector,
its entries quadratic in the ground's coordinates over the located
stage (Lemma~\ref{lem:stage}, the kernel's spanning list
Lemma~\ref{lem:split}'s), one reversal count per ground, exact by
$\mathrm{LDL}^{\mathsf T}$ there (Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia};
Corollary~\ref{cor:momentdivisor}), settled by integers; a ground
line reads one count, the pullback quadratic's scale the
homogeneity principle's read (Definition~\ref{def:ground};
Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}); a kernel of one line reads its quotient
at a point, and a deeper kernel indexes the display over its
ground space, the window pair's read covering every member at
once; and the window pair, the detector form whole, reads every
ground at once, a compression keeping the read
(Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}), the spectral read fixed at
Theorem~\ref{thm:levelequiv}. Each window cut is available at every point of the
\emph{closed} segment: a window compression is finite-dimensional
(Proposition~\ref{prop:windowfinite}), so a least eigenvalue and an
exact reversal count exist at either end and at every coupling ray, an
end's pencil its one member's own
(Proposition~\ref{prop:segment}). The cut of $\mathcal K(v)$ is the
pairs every window cut admits, the window cuts' meet over the
directed windows, $\mathcal K(v)\ge\kappa$ that membership, the
read $\mathcal K_\Lambda(v)\ge\kappa$ at every window; each cut,
the window cuts and the meet, is downward closed, its read, the
kernel point with every further level at or beyond $\gamma$,
weakening as $\gamma$ falls; a member pair at all is the positivity
read (every pair positive by its shape); and a value, of
$\mathcal K$ or of a window's $\mathcal K_\Lambda$, is its cut's
located edge where one is named.

\emph{Two scalings, and which one each clause reads.} Moving along a ray,
$[\alpha\!:\!\beta]\mapsto[\,t\alpha\!:\!t\beta\,]$, is the freedom the
pair reads through, one value on the ray
(Proposition~\ref{prop:segment}); rescaling the family, $E\mapsto uE$ and
$M\mapsto uM$, is a second freedom, and a level of the pencil is degree one in
each. $\mathcal K(v)$ takes $E_0$ at
the free end against the level at $v$, so it is free of the second and
degree one in the first: its \emph{side} is a property of the ray. Its
\emph{value} is free of both freedoms where the two reads are taken at the
two ends against each other, one family and one $u$ common to the two; that is $\mathcal K$'s
value at the contact end.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}\label{thm:main}
Let the member run over the classification's stated domain, each
member once: the $A$-series at the declared residue $r\ge1$
(Construction~\ref{con:places}), and $B_\ell$ at $\ell\ge2$,
$C_\ell$ at $\ell\ge3$, $D_\ell$ at $\ell\ge4$ and the five fixed
members at their weight tables
(Construction~\ref{con:sertables}), the index floors reading each
member once with the low-index coincidences the classification's
own identifications; let $r$ be the member's derived residue,
$d\in\mathbb N$ the direction count
(Construction~\ref{con:lattice}), and $[\,\alpha:\beta\,]$ the
coupling's end-weight pair at $\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb N$, interior
by the shape of its data
(Proposition~\ref{prop:segment}). Then:
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em,itemsep=2pt]
\item a pair is a member of the cut of
      $\mathcal K\big([\,\alpha:\beta\,]\big)$, positive by its
      shape; and
\item at the contact end the value is the contact pair of
      Definition~\ref{def:contactpair},
      \[
        \big(3,\ H_r\big) :
      \]
      the channel's order count at the base $c_1$, the member's
      $\theta$-support count (Proposition~\ref{prop:row}), and
      the walk's pole
      excess on the ruler's side, the pair's equality read
      componentwise, a count and one cross-multiplication identity
      (Definition~\ref{def:ground}); and at every window whose cut
      names an edge the edge is attained: the compressed pair's
      least root, read at a word image with the two moments at
      equality and the first positive
      (Lemma~\ref{lem:attained}(i),(ii)), the level the cut fixes
      there the mass point's own located read between the window
      cuts' edges and the bordered pencils' own, the mass weight's
      side the upper at base two and equal members at base one
      (Theorem~\ref{thm:closing}; Theorem~\ref{thm:channeldiv}); and
\item every continuum datum, a finite list of supports, separations
      and Euclidean pairs at pair coordinates against the corner
      scale, is one located object: the corner cell has positive
      extent with the contact end interior to it, the datum's
      scale reads close within priced brackets at the certified
      rates, and the corner heights read two-sided at the cell's
      positive located floor, the spacing that floor's read under
      the unit map; and each cell's window cut's edge, where one is
      named, is the compressed pair's least root read at a word
      image at the corner scale
      (Theorem~\ref{thm:continuum}; Lemma~\ref{lem:corner};
      Lemma~\ref{lem:cornerpivot}; Lemma~\ref{lem:attained}(i),(ii)).
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Clause (i) is Theorem~\ref{thm:gappos}: the cells' reads at the root
coordinate's rays, the counting sandwich of its clause (v) at every
further end-weight ray, the cutoffs reaching the cut within
Theorem~\ref{thm:truncation}'s priced modulus at the unit electric
representative. Clause (ii) is Theorem~\ref{thm:closing}, the
lattice point $(3,H_r)$ of the two divisor reads with the window
cuts' edges read at word images at the chain's located bracket.
Clause (iii) is
Theorem~\ref{thm:continuum}: the located brackets, the scale
closure, and the height floor on the corner cell, the cells' edges
read at word images (Lemma~\ref{lem:corner}).
\end{proof}

Clause (i) is a count and clause (ii) a lattice point, and $\mathcal K$ is the
one object both read (Definition~\ref{def:K}). Its two ingredients are each
weight-free, one value at every form point
(Proposition~\ref{prop:form}), and
$\mathcal K$ is a count against a count. Clause (iii)'s located
values are the family's continuum reads, the spacing among
its outputs (Theorem~\ref{thm:continuum}). The outputs factor,
the invariant residue-side and the corner outputs holding the
direction and scheme dependence (Theorem~\ref{thm:decomp}); the
fundamental-character family reads beside the adjoint's at one
bracket per output (Theorem~\ref{thm:twoaction}); and the
state's space is the word sector's own
(Lemma~\ref{lem:statespace}). Remark~\ref{rem:dictionary}
records the correspondence between these objects and their usual names, the
lattice point's reading against its rulers included.

\begin{remark}[Correspondence with the usual formulation]\label{rem:dictionary}
The objects above are recorded here under their standard names, so
that Theorem~\ref{thm:main} reads against the usual statement. The
correspondence reads into this remark alone: every
derivation above holds with the remark deleted.
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em,itemsep=3pt]

\item \emph{The theory.} $H(1,b)=E+bM$ is the lattice gauge Hamiltonian, $E$ its
electric term, a sum of link Casimirs, and $M$ its magnetic term, a sum of
plaquette characters (Definition~\ref{def:pencil}). Its space is the carrier
$\mathrm{Sect}_\infty$, a colimit of windows each finite-dimensional
(Definition~\ref{def:carrier}, Proposition~\ref{prop:windowfinite}); its
observables are the $\ast$-algebra of Definition~\ref{def:algebra}, whose
products obey a window bound stated on the index alone
(Proposition~\ref{prop:leastwindow}) and whose local algebras have $\mathfrak A$
as their limit (Definition~\ref{def:qla}). That bound and that limit are
locality, and both are read off the index. $H$ is a symmetric rational matrix
on every window, so each level read there is linear algebra
(Theorem~\ref{thm:pairpencil}). Classically the carrier's elements are
the gauge-invariant polynomial functions of the link holonomies,
$\mathrm{Eval}$ is the Haar integral of the compact group, and the pairing
is its $L^2$ one: the compact group, its invariant mean, and its coordinate
ring are one classical reading of the fusion data, which the calculus above
computes exactly. The label calculus's classical identities are
listed under their usual names: the blocks are the irreducible representations at highest
weights, the orthogonal complement of Lemma~\ref{lem:orthsplit} is what
Weyl's integration buys as complete reducibility, the scalar endomorphisms
of Lemma~\ref{lem:blockirr} are Schur's lemma, the alternant identity of
Theorem~\ref{thm:weylchar} is the Weyl character formula, the
alternant pair $a_\beta$ reading as the determinant
$\det\big(x_i^{\beta_j}\big)=\sum_w\mathrm{sgn}(w)\,x^{w\beta}$, the
pair's value its members' difference and the swap grading the sign of
the permutation, the full-column line of
Lemma~\ref{lem:dualread}(ii) the determinant representation, and
Corollary~\ref{cor:weyldim} its dimension formula,
Lemma~\ref{lem:pieri} the Pieri rule at one box,
Lemma~\ref{lem:pathsquare}'s path count $f^\lambda$ the standard
tableau count at the shape (the raised glyph the classical one,
the unraised $f$ the fundamental label's),
Corollary~\ref{cor:steinberg} the alternating fusion count
(Brauer, Klimyk), and Lemma~\ref{lem:mixedinv} the Schur--Weyl
invariant span. That reading
extends to the local objects: the characters are the blocks' traces on
holonomies, the product reading
$\mathrm{tr}(A\otimes B)=\mathrm{tr}\,A\cdot\mathrm{tr}\,B$ with
$d_a=\chi_a(1)$; the adjoint character runs pointwise over $[-1,n^2-1]$
and the fundamental trace's modulus over $[0,n]$, the tops at $U=1$, the
form caps of Lemmas~\ref{lem:fpcap} and~\ref{lem:loopcap} read
pointwise; the form line of Proposition~\ref{prop:form} is the invariant
symmetric bilinear forms on $\mathfrak{su}(n)$; the two-plaquette fiber
of Construction~\ref{con:fiber} is $L^2(G\times G)^G$, the functions of
the two plaquette holonomies invariant under simultaneous conjugation,
the theta graph's gauge reduction; and $\mathfrak A$
(Definition~\ref{def:qla}) is the quasi-local $C^*$-algebra, the norm
closure of the inductive limit of the local algebras.

\item \emph{The vacuum.} $\psi$ is a window ground and $\varepsilon_0$ its
eigenvalue, so $(H-\varepsilon_0)\psi=0$ and $H-\varepsilon_0\succeq0$ on
$\psi^\perp$ by construction (Definition~\ref{def:pencil}); on a window the
ground multiplicity is a count, a divisor read
(Theorem~\ref{thm:SO}), and the vacuum's correlation reads sit in
named window brackets, Euclidean pairs included
(Theorem~\ref{thm:groundreads}). A reader who doubts uniqueness in the limit
keeps every read: the cut is stated on counting functions, every
comparison a count of the pencil itself rather than a read at a chosen
state (Definition~\ref{def:K}; Theorem~\ref{thm:flatstep}), so
Theorem~\ref{thm:main} holds at every ground.

\item \emph{The lattice.} The carrier is stated over the interface of
Construction~\ref{con:lattice}, so the
derivations run on its fields alone. The reading here takes $\mathbb Z^d$, whose
plaquettes are its unit squares, at $d\ge2$, and the plaquette chain at $d=1$,
consecutive squares sharing one link. On either, a plaquette's boundary
is four links, the count Proposition~\ref{prop:E0} reads, and a
link sits in the plaquettes of its direction's pairs, the per-link
plaquette count the gap $g$ at $g+2=2d$ here, the per-vertex
count $d\,g$, a straight cell's stencil $[\,g^{2}:2\,]$, and each
at most two on the chain. $d$ counts the
space directions and time is the pencil's own, so $d=3$ is the case of
$\mathbb R^4$.

\item \emph{The lattice symmetries.} $E$ and $M$ run over all links and all
plaquettes at coefficient one, so $H$ commutes with every symmetry of
$\mathbb Z^d$ permuting those two sets: the translations, and the
hyperoctahedral group $B_d$ of signed coordinate permutations
(Theorem~\ref{thm:restoration}), whose permutation factor is the coordinate
exchanges and whose sign flips are the reflections. $B_d$ is what
Theorem~\ref{thm:restoration} is stated over, the translations entering
separately at the fibering (Lemma~\ref{lem:fiberdec}). What the continuum asks besides is $O(d)$, and
Theorem~\ref{thm:restoration} reads the difference at every correlation as a
multiplicity: the rings coincide at degree $2$, the multiplicity
positive at degree $4$, so the anisotropy is
suppressed by two degrees in the momentum; the theorem's isotropic
span reads classically as the $O(d)$-invariant ring, the first
fundamental theorem for $O(d)$ the classical side's own
identification.

\item \emph{The coupling.} The pencil's parameter is the coupling. The
free end's pencil is the electric term alone and the contact end's
the magnetic alone
(Proposition~\ref{prop:segment}), so the two ends are the strong- and
weak-coupling limits.

\item \emph{The continuum's points.} Clause~(i) is stated over
every interior end-weight ray, the root coordinate's rays the
cells' working points (Definition~\ref{def:pencil}). A reader's
continuum point (a Cauchy
or Dedekind real, a nested-bracket datum) reads the cut in through
its own rational comparisons: the counting sandwich of
Theorem~\ref{thm:gappos}(v) transports the rational reads to every
located coupling at its stated modulus, so ``at every interior
coupling'' holds at whatever continuum the reader brings, each point
reading itself in through the rationals beside it. The chain's own
points are the rational rays, and the sandwich is the whole of a
further point's read.

\item \emph{The scalar pairs.} The translation of the colon
notation is a bijection and reads one way only: a scalar pair
$[a\!:\!b]$ (Definition~\ref{def:ground}) is the positive
rational $a/b$, two pairs at the cross-multiplication
identity being one rational, so the stage glyph $\mathbb P$ reads
as the positive rationals, with $\mathbb Q$, the field, this
remark's own; under that map the displayed sum,
product and order of Definition~\ref{def:ground} are the field's
own on the positive rationals, the signed readings and the zero
this remark's own, a pair of equal members reading zero and an absent
summand a zero term, and
$H_k=\sum_{j\le k}[1\!:\!j]$ is the classical harmonic number
$\sum_{j\le k}1/j$. A coupling's end-weight pair $[\alpha\!:\!\beta]$
(Proposition~\ref{prop:segment}) is the point $[\alpha:-\beta]$ of the
real projective line in the classical $(E,M)$-coefficient
coordinates, the minus this remark's own; the scalar instance is the
affine reading at positive second component, and the division is the
remark's own reading of a clearing. A balance pair
$\langle u\!:\!v\rangle$ (Definition~\ref{def:ground}) is the
difference $u-v$, the subtraction this remark's own: its side is the
value's sign, equal members the value zero, and a bracket of
balance-pair endpoints is the signed interval its endpoints name.

\item \emph{The names.} The document's terms of art read against
their standard names, each at its defining site. The reversal
count $\operatorname{rev}(A\!:\!B)$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}) is
the negative index of inertia of the difference $A-B$, the
subtraction this remark's own, and its two-splits-one-count
clause is Sylvester's law of inertia. The division descent
(Theorem~\ref{thm:windowsep}) is the Euclidean algorithm, its
greatest common divisor taken monic over the pair carrier and, at
the integer representatives, primitive at the stated positive top
with the clearing multipliers the remark's own rescaling; the
cleared division at a stated top is the classical
pseudo-division. The
remainder lists at a monic $S$ (Definition~\ref{def:poly}) are the
quotient ring $K[x]/(S)$ read as data. The cofactor $c$ at
$Y\,c=X$ (Definition~\ref{def:ground}) is the exact quotient $X/Y$,
computer algebra's own cofactor, and
the gap $g$ at $q+g=p$ the difference $p-q$, the remark's own
operations (the classical matrix cofactors enter as the
adjugate's entries, under that name). The deck relation $z^2+1=wz$
(Definition~\ref{def:deck}; Theorem~\ref{thm:chordmap};
Lemma~\ref{lem:deckfactor}) is the
double cover $w=z+1/z$, the Joukowski map, its deck involution
the cover's deck transformation $z\mapsto1/z$, and the chord
$t=\zeta+\zeta^{-1}=2\cos k$ at the classical momentum $k$ is the
Chebyshev variable of the momentum fiber
(Lemma~\ref{lem:fiberdec}). The decimation
(Theorem~\ref{thm:decimation}) is the classical block decimation,
each step a Schur complement onto the head. A located
element (Lemma~\ref{lem:stage}) is located in Bishop's
constructive sense, the bracket its datum. The $d_f$-ality
(Lemma~\ref{lem:chargedcell}) is the classical $N$-ality at
$N=n$, the box count's class at full columns. The beta-set
(Construction~\ref{con:places}) is the partition's classical
beta-set at the positive shift, $x_i=\lambda_i+(d_f-i)+1$ against
the classical $\lambda_i+d_f-i$. A content (Construction~\ref{con:places}) is the
classical weight of the grading, `weight' in the chain the end and
form data's word; a configuration's content
(Definition~\ref{def:carrier}) is its total Casimir, the electric
term's total. The calculus at the symbolic residue is the
unitary group $\mathrm{U}(n)$'s, the determinant relations
per-residue data, the presentation kernel's
(Remark~\ref{rem:kernel}).

\item \emph{The members.} The classification's members read as
the simply connected compact simple groups at $r+1=h^\vee$, the
derived residue
the dual Coxeter number's read, the residue folds of
Construction~\ref{con:sertables} its per-member evaluations; the
title reads here. The $A$-series is $\mathrm{SU}(n)$ at
$n:=r+1=d_f$, whose fundamental representation has dimension $n$
and whose Lie rank is $n-1$, the declared names reading the
matrix size as the fundamental count $d_f$ and the Lie rank as
the residue $r$ (Construction~\ref{con:places}); the member
weight tables read $B_\ell$, $C_\ell$ and $D_\ell$ as
$\mathrm{Spin}(2\ell{+}1)$, $\mathrm{Sp}(\ell)$ and
$\mathrm{Spin}(2\ell)$, and the five fixed members the
exceptional groups. Theorem~\ref{thm:main}'s prefix instantiates
the classical statement at every simply connected compact simple
group and every
$d\ge1$, each group once: the index floors read each low-index
coincidence at one member, $\mathrm{Spin}(3)$ and
$\mathrm{Sp}(1)$ at $\mathrm{SU}(2)$, $\mathrm{Sp}(2)$ at
$\mathrm{Spin}(5)$, and $\mathrm{Spin}(6)$ at $\mathrm{SU}(4)$,
with
$\mathrm{Spin}(4)$ off the simple list, the identifications the
classification's own.

\item \emph{The weak-coupling comparison, and the root coordinate.}
Classically the weak-coupling theory is read against lattice Maxwell
theory per Lie-algebra direction: the link field is $\log U_\ell$, the
magnetic energy is the abelianized flux squared, and the comparison
diagonalizes in Fourier with dispersion
$\varepsilon(k)^2=2|b|\,\hat h(k)$ at the graph Laplacian $\hat h$ of
$\mathbb Z^d$, massless at $k\to0$. That is the classical identity of the
gap chain's objects: the root coordinate $\tau$ ($b=-\tau^2$, the
remark's reading of Definition~\ref{def:pencil}'s weights) is the harmonic frequency scale, and the
soft momenta the flat step reads cell by cell
(Lemma~\ref{lem:fiberdec}; Theorem~\ref{thm:flatstep}) are the sector
the massless comparison leaves ungapped: the lift above the comparison is
what the counts read, the corner scale its ruler
(Lemma~\ref{lem:corner}).

\item \emph{The gap.} $E_0$ is the energy of the one-plaquette excitation at the
free end, where the module is an eigenvector of the pencil
(Proposition~\ref{prop:E0}), and $\mathcal K$ is the mass of the lightest
excitation above the ground read against it (Definition~\ref{def:K}). Since
$E_0=4>0$, clause~(i) is the mass gap: on the word sector
(Definition~\ref{def:pencil}) the spectrum starts \emph{strictly} above the
vacuum. The spectrum condition itself, $H-\varepsilon_0
\succeq0$, is item~2's and holds by construction; clause~(i) is the strict
statement above it. Clause~(ii) is its
size in units of that excitation, the two ends read together, with
the level met at word images window by window
(Lemma~\ref{lem:attained}(i),(ii)): the lightest excitation is a state the
local observables create from the vacuum. A reader who
wants the level as an energy restores the two scales at which
$\mathcal K$ reads one value: it is $\mathcal K\,E_0\,C_2(\mathrm{adj})$, with $E_0$ moving with
the family's scale and $C_2(\mathrm{adj})$ with the invariant form
(Proposition~\ref{prop:form}), as the level does with each, which is what makes
the product an energy.

\item \emph{What clause~(ii) says at the contact end.} $\mathcal K$ reads
a value free of unit and of scale: one value at every form point
(Proposition~\ref{prop:form}) and at every family scale
(Definition~\ref{def:K}, Theorem~\ref{thm:unitschain}), a ratio of two
counts, the outputs' factorization Theorem~\ref{thm:decomp}'s,
the invariant residue-side with the corner outputs holding the
direction and scheme dependence. Clause~(i) is strict positivity at
every rational interior ray, transported to every located coupling by the
sandwich (the item on the continuum's points above); clause~(ii) is the lattice point
$(3,H_r)$ at the contact end, its excess on the ruler's side,
and $\mathcal C$ is closed
(Proposition~\ref{prop:segment}), so that endpoint is a value like any other
rather than a limit approached; and the approach is the corner
cell's, its extent and floor located outputs at the corner scale,
the couplings' geometric mean (Lemma~\ref{lem:corner}). The classical rulers are
$(\ln c_1,\,1)$, the axes the usual formulation measures with; read
against them the lattice point is the exact value
$\mathcal K=c_1^{\,3}\,e^{-H_r}$ at every member, $r+1=h^\vee$
the members item's read, the
base $c_1$ one off the $A$-series so the value
$e^{-H_r}$ there with the first ruler reading one, and
at the $A$-series $8\,e^{-H_{n-1}}$
at $n\ge3$ and $e^{-1}$ at $n=2$
(Construction~\ref{con:xfusion}; Proposition~\ref{prop:row}). The first
coordinate's order $2$ is classically the Szeg\H o geometric-mean read of
the spectral weight, and the second the walk residue's exponent;
the attainment clause reads classically as the excitation's own
state, a bound state at base two, the mass point an isolated pole
below the band, and at base one the mass point at the band's edge,
the mass weight's equal-membered read there
(Theorem~\ref{thm:channeldiv});
item~4's $O(d)$ multiplicities are the contact end's symmetry reads.

\item \emph{The continuum reading.} The usual formulation reads the
contact end's approach through the continuum construction: the spacing $a$
sets the units, the electric weight is $g^2/2a$ and the magnetic
$1/(ag^2)$ at the usual coefficients, and the construction holds the
physical gap $\Delta$ fixed while $a\to0$ along a coupling
trajectory $g\to0$, so its premise is that the gap in lattice units,
$a\Delta$, falls under every floor toward the contact end, the diverging correlation
length. The corner scale maps that premise onto the chain's data
exactly: the two weights' product is $1/2a^2$ at every $g$, one value
across the couplings, so the corner height of
Lemma~\ref{lem:corner} reads classically as $\sqrt2\,a\Delta$ at
every coupling of the approach. The premise therefore reads: the
gap falls below every corner-scale floor toward the contact end, the
corner cell's outputs under every floor. Its negation reads: a positive
floor on a positive extent, $a\Delta$ bounded below toward weak
coupling. The outputs are located data per member and direction
count (Lemma~\ref{lem:corner}), so the formulation's continuum
reading, restricted to this family, is a read of them: the corner
cell's floor is positive, a located datum per member and direction
count at the certificate's derived rates
(Lemma~\ref{lem:corner}'s cell floor);
clause~(ii)'s pair is the contact end's own value on either outcome, and
item~4's multiplicities are the reading's symmetry half.

\item \emph{The unit map.} The classical construction chooses a
trajectory $g(a)$ by hand; here the spacing is a read: at a
physical gap $\Delta$ the unit is $a=h/(\sqrt2\,\Delta)$ at the
certified corner height $h$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:corner}), one equation
per scale, the whole trajectory a located function.

\item \emph{The reconstruction data.} The Wightman reconstruction
reads $(\mathfrak A,\omega)$: the quasi-local algebra of
Definition~\ref{def:qla} with a window ground's state, positivity
definitional (Definition~\ref{def:carrier}), the Hilbert space the
GNS one at the word sector's own pairing
(Lemma~\ref{lem:statespace}), the vacuum the ground; the Osterwalder--Schrader detour is
the Euclidean route to what the pencil formulation holds by
construction (Theorem~\ref{thm:reconstruct}), so the ``at least as strong as'' clause is met on the
spectral side directly. The lattice correlators' certified reads
are Theorem~\ref{thm:groundreads}'s displays: window brackets,
clustering, and Euclidean pairs, with $e^{-u(H-\varepsilon_0)}$
this remark's reading of the Euclidean bracket families; the
Wightman functions are the bracketed reads' classical reading at
the moment folds' clearance (Lemma~\ref{lem:momentfold}), the
reconstruction's half there
(Theorem~\ref{thm:continuum}), the spacing the height floor's
output (Theorem~\ref{thm:continuum}(iii)).

\item \emph{The spectral condition.} $\mathrm{spec}(H)\subset
\{\varepsilon_0\}\cup[\varepsilon_0+E_0\kappa,\infty)$ is the window cut
(Definition~\ref{def:K}; Lemma~\ref{lem:speccut}), its edge met at a
word image window by window with the weight at the level where the
inclusion is read (Lemma~\ref{lem:attained}); the
energy--momentum decomposition is the fibering
(Lemma~\ref{lem:fiberdec}); the forward cone is the cone read under
the unit map (Lemma~\ref{lem:cone}), the dispersion isotropic at
degree two with the direction data two powers of the spacing down;
and the posed $\Delta>0$ is the corner floor's positivity read
through the unit map, a located datum per member at
the chain's direction count (the projection item's own reads)
at the certificate's derived rates
(Lemma~\ref{lem:corner}'s cell floor),
clause~(ii)'s pair the exact value at the
contact end.

\item \emph{Locality, and the net.} The regions' local algebras
with the window bounds (Proposition~\ref{prop:leastwindow};
Lemma~\ref{lem:stableentries}; Definition~\ref{def:qla}) read as the
Haag--Kastler net; the algebra is commutative outright
(Proposition~\ref{prop:algebra}), the equal-time half of
microcausality, and the unequal-time half is the propagation
bracket's read (Theorem~\ref{thm:groundreads}(vi)): the lower
family's reach is exact, commutator reads beyond it are the
remainder's own at the certificates' width, and the classical
reading's cone is the reconstruction's read at the located
families (Theorem~\ref{thm:reconstruct}), riding
the datum reads' closure at the spacing output (Theorem~\ref{thm:continuum}(ii),(iii)); the clustering brackets are
Theorem~\ref{thm:groundreads}(iv)'s.

\item \emph{Invariance.} Rotations: Theorem~\ref{thm:restoration}'s
count is the limit's $O(d)$ invariance, the breaking terms at
two powers of the spacing under the unit map, the rate certified.
Boosts: time is the pencil's own and continuous, and the
degree-two dispersion is one isotropic cone with the direction
data two orders down (Lemma~\ref{lem:cone}); the boost covariance
is the reconstruction's read at the cone
(Theorem~\ref{thm:reconstruct}), riding
the datum reads' closure at the spacing output
(Theorem~\ref{thm:continuum}(ii),(iii)); translations are the lattice's,
the momenta
reading through the $t$-cells at every continuum point.

\item \emph{Nontriviality.} The theory is interacting at every
member: $c_1$, the cubic-coupling count, is positive
(Construction~\ref{con:xfusion}); the connected four-point read
at the theta-graph pair holds a positive located floor on a cell
at the free end's side, the leading coefficient
$c_1^{\,2}\,\langle S\!:\![\,1:8\,]\rangle$ a displayed member
read (Lemma~\ref{lem:fourpoint}), while a generalized free
field's four-point function is its three two-point pairings'
sum, Wick's read, so its connected fold reads equal members at
every argument list: the
floor separates the state from every generalized free field's;
and clause~(ii)'s value separates
the family from every free field's reads.

\item \emph{The projection.} The posed problem asks one compact
simple group on $\mathbb R^4$, existence, and a positive gap. The
statement here is instantiated at every member of the
classification, every
direction count, and every coupling of the closed segment, with the
contact end a value rather than a limit, the constant exact where a bound
was requested, and the chain constructive over the naturals with
machine-checkable certificates: the posed problem is the $d=3$,
single-member, existence-only projection of Theorem~\ref{thm:main},
its clause (iii) the existence half's carrier,
read through the items above, each requirement one derived
read: the existence half reads Theorem~\ref{thm:continuum}(i)'s
located brackets with the spectral floor on the corner
certificate's cell, the heights two-sided at the cell's positive
located floor, the datum reads' closure the reconstruction's
read (Theorem~\ref{thm:reconstruct}), and
the gap half is the floor's clearance at the located spectral data
(Theorem~\ref{thm:continuum}(ii)). The emitted
records read at both coordinates: the assembled chains and the
corner records read at the plaquette chain's direction count, the
separated-sector instance at two directions and residue two
(Lemma~\ref{lem:relfiber}), and the derivations run at
every direction count with the stencil caps and the momentum sums
polynomial in it (Theorem~\ref{thm:gappos}(iv)): the posed $d=3$
is the derivations' instance at its stated coordinates, read as
every dimension is; the emitted records key by member, their
direction count the chain's, this item their statement.

\item \emph{The representation, and the bridge to the fundamental theory.} $M$
multiplies by the adjoint character. The bridge to the fundamental theory's coupling is
Theorem~\ref{thm:bridge}'s $b_f^{\mathrm{equiv}}=2n\,b_X$, the plaquette
characters' quadratic matching ($\chi_R$-curvature
$=d_RC_2(R)/\dim G$) generating the fundamental theory's continuum
action at that ratio, a ratio of two
weight-$1$ objects and so a count, the fundamental-character
family's corner outputs beside the adjoint's at one bracket per
output (Theorem~\ref{thm:twoaction}); $c_1$ counts the cubic couplings of the
adjoint (Construction~\ref{con:xfusion}).
\end{enumerate}
\end{remark}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{Fusion data}

\begin{proposition}[A fusion computation is a representation
computation]\label{prop:repring}
The fusion data are counts of the calculus: $N^c_{ab}$ is the number of
$c$-blocks in a product of blocks and $d_a$ the block dimension
(Lemma~\ref{lem:blockcount}, on labels by Construction~\ref{con:labels}),
$m\{a,b,c\}$ the invariant count of the triple product, the labels
the label set's (Construction~\ref{con:labels}). Each is read in the label index: $\chi_a$ is the index's generator at
$a$, the product of two expanding over the tensor product's blocks,
\[
\chi_a\chi_b=\sum_cN^c_{ab}\,\chi_c ,
\]
the coefficients the block counts (Lemma~\ref{lem:blockcount}(iii), on
labels by Construction~\ref{con:labels}); the dimension $d_a$ is the
block's count (Lemma~\ref{lem:blockirr}(iv)); and the unit label
$\mathbf 1$ has $\chi_{\mathbf 1}=1$, the constants' line's.

Four reads follow, and they are what the label index runs on. Write
$\mathrm{Hom}(x,y)$ for the linear maps from $x$ to $y$; then
$\mathrm{Hom}(x,y)\cong x^{*}\otimes y$, the intertwiners are its invariants,
and the unit label's multiplicity in any $x$ is its invariant count.
First,
\[
N^{\mathbf 1}_{ab}=\delta_{a\bar b} ,
\]
the unit read of Construction~\ref{con:labels}: the count of the
unit label in the product at $a$ and $b$ is the full-column-power count
of Lemma~\ref{lem:dualread}(iii), one exactly at the dual label. Second, the characters are orthonormal for the evaluation, the
unit-label coefficient read
$\mathrm{Eval}(\chi_c)=\delta_{c\mathbf 1}$, the block's invariant
count, so
\[
\mathrm{Eval}\big(\chi_a\overline{\chi_b}\big)
=\sum_cN^c_{a\bar b}\,\mathrm{Eval}(\chi_c)
=N^{\mathbf 1}_{a\bar b}=\delta_{ab} ,
\]
and a product expanded in the label index therefore has unique coefficients.
Third, $m\{a,b,c\}=N^{\bar c}_{ab}$, by
$m\{a,b,c\}=\sum_dN^d_{ab}N^{\mathbf 1}_{dc}$ with
the first read; so $m$ is symmetric in its three arguments, and that is the
symmetry of $N$ at self-dual labels. Fourth, the involution's index
read: the dual label's matrix is the transpose,
$N^c_{\bar a\,b}=N^b_{ac}$: the triple count reads one value at
every argument dualized, duals of blocks being blocks with the
unit's count kept (Lemma~\ref{lem:dualread}(i)), and the third
read's symmetry closes the exchange.
\end{proposition}

\begin{lemma}[The adjoint character]\label{lem:adjchar}
Write $f$ for the fundamental label, the one-box shape's, whose block
is $V$ itself at the declared count $d_f$
(Construction~\ref{con:places}), and $\bar f$ for its
dual, at the residue at or beyond one. Then
\[
f\otimes\bar f\;=\;\theta\oplus\mathbf 1 ,
\qquad\text{hence}\qquad
\chi_\theta(U)+1\;=\;|\mathrm{tr}\,U|^2 ,
\]
and the adjoint's
contents are the \emph{letter-pair moves}, one place raised
and one lowered, each ordered pair of distinct places once, with the
unit-monomial content at
multiplicity $r$, the
product's unit-monomial count splitting as the unit line's one with
the adjoint's, $r+1=d_f$.
\begin{proof}
$\bar f$ is the class of the complement $f^*$
(Construction~\ref{con:labels}), the one-column shape at the
complement gap, $r$ rows of one box (Lemma~\ref{lem:dualread}(ii)),
and $\bar f\otimes f$
exhausts by the one-box row (Lemma~\ref{lem:pieri}): the dominant
one-box additions of $(1^{r})$ are $(2,1^{r-1})$, the label
$\theta$ (Construction~\ref{con:labels}), and the full column
$(1^{d_f})$, the unit label's, one block each with every further
label vacant, and the factors exchange
(Lemma~\ref{lem:blockcount}(iii)): $f\otimes\bar f=\theta\oplus
\mathbf 1$. The product's contents are the factors' contents'
sums (Construction~\ref{con:places}): the one-box block reads each
letter once and $\bar f$'s block each withdrawn letter once
(Lemma~\ref{lem:pieri}'s complement read), so the product reads
the unit-monomial content at $d_f$, one per letter, and each
letter-pair move once, a letter against a withdrawn one. The
content refinement of Lemma~\ref{lem:blockcount}(ii) reads the
product's occupancies as the two blocks' at their counts, and the
full-column line is occupied at the unit-monomial content alone
(Lemma~\ref{lem:dualread}(ii)): $\theta$'s occupancies are the
product's with the line's withdrawn, each move once and the
unit-monomial content at $d_f-1=r$. Hence
$\chi_f\chi_{\bar f}=\chi_\theta+1$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:repring}), and
$\chi_f(U)=\mathrm{tr}\,U$ reads the display.
\end{proof}
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[The fusion matrix is capped by its dimension]\label{lem:fpcap}
On one link's character index, multiplication by $\chi_\lambda$ is the
fusion matrix $N^\mu_{\lambda\nu}$ against the orthonormal characters
(Proposition~\ref{prop:repring}), row-finite on every window
(Proposition~\ref{prop:fusionfinite}), with count entries,
symmetric at self-dual $\lambda$, and the dimension vector is its exact
positive eigenvector:
\[
\sum_\mu N^\mu_{\lambda\nu}\,d_\mu\;=\;d_\lambda\,d_\nu ,
\]
dimensions multiplying over a tensor product (Lemma~\ref{lem:blockcount}).
Hence
at self-dual $\lambda$ the fusion form is capped two-sidedly at the
dimension (Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}),
\[
N_\lambda\ \preceq\ d_\lambda
\qquad\text{with}\qquad
d_\lambda+N_\lambda\ \ \text{positive semidefinite}:
\]
the two squares $(d_\nu x_\mu+d_\mu x_\nu)^2$ and
$\langle d_\nu x_\mu\!:\!d_\mu x_\nu\rangle^2$, each positive by its
shape, price each entry's read $2\,x_\mu x_\nu$
two-sidedly at
$[\,d_\nu\!:\!d_\mu\,]x_\mu^2+[\,d_\mu\!:\!d_\nu\,]x_\nu^2$, the
comparison at its rescaling (Definition~\ref{def:ground}'s
homogeneity principle), the cleared weights the window's
co-products, the dims' product at the pair's two factors
withdrawn, every weight natural, and the
eigen-identity sums the rows. The fundamental's multiplication then squares to the
adjoint's: its pairing-adjoint is multiplication by $\chi_{\bar f}$, so
\[
M_{\chi_f}^\dagger M_{\chi_f}\;=\;M_{1+\chi_\theta}\ \preceq\
1+r\,(r+2)\;=\;d_f^{\,2} ,
\]
at the display's $\lambda=\theta$, self-dual with $d_\theta=r\,(r+2)$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[The loop character is capped by its dimension]\label{lem:loopcap}
On every window, multiplication by a plaquette's fundamental character
squares to the cap $d_f^{\,2}$: writing
$\chi_f(U_{\partial p})=\sum_{i,j}A_{ij}\,(U_4)_{ji}$ over a
cut of the loop, $A:=U_1U_2U_3$ the complementary path,
\[
\big\langle y,\ \chi_f(U_{\partial p})\,x\big\rangle^{2}
\ \le\ \Big(\sum_{i,j}\|\bar A_{ij}\,y\|^2\Big)
\Big(\sum_{i,j}\|(U_4)_{ji}\,x\|^2\Big)
\ =\ d_f^{\,2}\,\langle x,x\rangle\,\langle y,y\rangle
\]
by the squared Cauchy--Schwarz of the coefficient carrier's pairing
(Construction~\ref{con:coeff}), every square a pairing, each sum
contracting by the evaluation identity to the trace of the identity,
$\mathrm{tr}(A^\dagger A)=\mathrm{tr}(U_4^\dagger U_4)=d_f$, times the
pairing; at $y=\chi_f(U_{\partial p})\,x$ the display reads the
comparison at the factor $\langle y,y\rangle$: at the factor
occupied the order reads back (Definition~\ref{def:ground}), and at
the factor of equal members the conclusion is the pairing's own
side read, the two self-pairings at or above the sum's unit
(Construction~\ref{con:coeff}):
$\langle\chi_f x,\chi_f x\rangle\le d_f^{\,2}\langle x,x\rangle$. Hence,
with $\chi_\theta+1=|\chi_f|^2$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:adjchar}),
\[
\chi_\theta(U_{\partial p})+1\;=\;M_{\chi_f(U_{\partial p})}^\dagger
M_{\chi_f(U_{\partial p})}\ \preceq\ d_f^{\,2}
\qquad\text{as forms on every window:}
\]
the left side is a square, positive semidefinite at its split, the cap
$d_f^{\,2}$ its upper read, and the shifted term reads the character's
two-sided cap at unit width below and $r\,(r+2)$ above. At a
member the cap runs in the adjoint itself: over a cut of the
loop at the $\theta$-block's coefficients,
$\chi_\theta(U_{\partial p})=\sum_{i,j}A_{ij}\,(U_4)_{ji}$ with
$A:=U_1U_2U_3$ read in the block, the squared Cauchy--Schwarz of
the coefficient carrier's pairing contracts each sum by the
evaluation identity at the block to the identity's trace there,
$d_\theta$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:row}'s dimension fold), and
the order reads back as above: the multiplication's square is
capped, $M_{\chi_\theta(U_{\partial p})}^{2}\preceq
d_\theta^{\,2}$ as forms on every window; the term reads two-sided
at $d_\theta$ by one further squared Cauchy--Schwarz at a window
vector against its image (Construction~\ref{con:coeff}),
$\langle x,\chi_\theta\,x\rangle^{2}$ at or below
$\langle x,x\rangle\,\langle\chi_\theta x,\chi_\theta x\rangle$, at
or below $d_\theta^{\,2}\,\langle x,x\rangle^{2}$ at the square's
cap against the self-pairing's side (Construction~\ref{con:coeff}),
and the squared comparison reads back at the trichotomy
(Definition~\ref{def:ground}), the form and its memberwise swap
each at or below $d_\theta\,\langle x,x\rangle$; the $A$-series'
shifted route is the display above.
\end{lemma}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{The requirement, in two moments}

\begin{theorem}[The divisor-separation detector]\label{thm:detector}
The level-$\gamma$ \emph{requirement} is the probe family
$\mu_2(A)\ge\gamma\,\mu_1(A)$ at the two moments
$\mu_j(A):=\langle A\psi,\,\tilde E^{\,j}A\psi\rangle$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:momentform}'s reads), and its \emph{detector pair} at a
spectral point $\lambda$ is $(\lambda^2\!:\!\gamma\lambda)$, the
moments' spectral read: a probe's two moments read the weight fold
over the located spectrum (Lemma~\ref{lem:split}), so the
requirement's read at a probe is the fold of the detector pairs' at
the probe's weights. The pair's members are equal exactly at the
kernel point and at the edge $\lambda=\gamma$, the two neutral
points, its site datum the factor read
$\lambda\,\langle\lambda\!:\!\gamma\rangle$, and off them its side
is $\lambda$'s against $\gamma$ at positive $\lambda$: weight below
$\gamma$ drives failure, weight beyond passes, weight at the edge
is neutral, and the vacuum direction, the kernel point, is neutral
as well, the vacuum's saturation untouched.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[The level condition over probes is the spectral condition]
\label{thm:levelequiv}
Over the word sector, the level-$\gamma$ requirement (one condition per
probe) is \emph{equivalent} to the operator inequality
\[
\tilde E^{\,2}\ \succeq\ \gamma\,\tilde E
\quad\text{on the word sector}
\qquad(\text{the kernel point with every further level at or beyond
}\gamma).
\]
\begin{proof}
($\Leftarrow$) With the spectrum at the kernel point and at or beyond
$\gamma$, every detector pair reads its upper side or equal members
(Theorem~\ref{thm:detector}), and every probe's weight fold with
it; read at the pair's certificate, the positive-semidefinite
split prices every sector vector's form through the congruence at
its witness, the blocks' fold of squares
(Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}), and the site datum's decomposition
reads the priced form as the two moments' comparison, the
requirement's own display. ($\Rightarrow$) On a window the reversal count settles it: at
$\operatorname{rev}(\tilde E^{\,2}\!:\!\gamma\tilde E)\ge1$ a
lower-side
pivot sits among the elimination datum's $\mathrm{LDL}^{\mathsf T}$ pivots, and
Theorem~\ref{thm:certconstruct}(2) reads off the elimination a pair-entried $v$
with $\langle v,\gamma\tilde E\,v\rangle>\langle v,\tilde E^{\,2}v\rangle$,
the reversal's own read. The witness is a
vector of the sector, hence $A\psi$ at some word $A$
(Definition~\ref{def:pencil}), and that word's probe fails the
requirement outright, the witness's two reads its two moments
(Theorem~\ref{thm:momentform}).
\end{proof}

\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[The two-moment form]\label{thm:momentform}
The family $\mu_2(A)\ge\gamma\,\mu_1(A)$ over probes is the
two-moment form of the operator inequality
(\,$\mu_j=\langle v_A,\tilde E^jv_A\rangle$, both affine in
the state, so a floor at the extreme states extends to their
mixtures\,). For a
\emph{multiplication} probe $f$ (a carrier element acting by its product,
Proposition~\ref{prop:algebra}),
both moments are \emph{exact, $\beta$-free polynomial-pair atoms}:
$[H,f]=[E,f]$, so
\[
\mu_1(f)=\omega\big(f^\dagger[E,f]\big),\qquad
\mu_2(f)=\omega\big(Q_2(f)\big),\quad
Q_2(f):=[E,f]^\dagger[E,f],
\]
the first display Theorem~\ref{thm:coeffone}'s exact line read at
the state and the second the commutator's square, and the
requirement reads, per multiplication word, as one inequality
between two commutator reads at the state, with the
coupling entering \emph{only} through the state:
\[
\boxed{\ \omega\big(Q_2(f)\big)\ \ge\ \gamma\,
\omega\big(f^\dagger[E,f]\big)\ }
\] \emph{(The multiplication family suffices.)} Both moments depend on
$A$ only through $y=A\psi$, since $\psi$, $\tilde E$'s kernel point, gives
$[H,A]\psi=\tilde E\,(A\psi)$ at every probe, and both sides are quadratic
forms in $y$. A probe is an element of the carrier
(Proposition~\ref{prop:leastwindow}) and the carrier's elements multiply
pointwise (Proposition~\ref{prop:algebra}), so $A\mapsto A\psi$ is linear and
its image is the word sector (Definition~\ref{def:pencil}): the floor over
probes and positivity of the moment form on that sector are one statement, the
hopping class included.
\end{theorem}

\begin{corollary}[Two moments against a divisor]
\label{cor:momentdivisor}
Combining Theorem~\ref{thm:momentform} with Theorem~\ref{thm:levelequiv}, on a
window:
\[
\big(\forall A:\ \mu_2(A)\ \ge\ \gamma\,\mu_1(A)\big)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\big(\tilde E^{\,2}\big)_\Lambda:\big(\gamma\,\tilde E\big)_\Lambda
\ \text{ positive semidefinite},
\]
the compression to the window's word sector taken of the detector form
whole, since $\mu_2(A)=\langle v_A,\tilde E^{\,2}v_A\rangle$ and
$\mu_1(A)=\langle v_A,\tilde E\,v_A\rangle$ at $v_A=A\psi$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:coeffone}): one reversal count on a form pair over
the located stage, exact by $\mathrm{LDL}^{\mathsf T}$ there
(Lemma~\ref{lem:stage}), whose read is
the kernel point with every further level at or beyond $\gamma$.
Right to left by Theorem~\ref{thm:detector}'s side reads, the
split's transport pricing every sector vector at the site datum's
decomposition (Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}); left to right by the count's own
witness, which Theorem~\ref{thm:certconstruct}(2) reads off the elimination.
\end{corollary}

\begin{lemma}[The sector's span read]\label{lem:sectorspan}
Multiplication by a window ground is adjoint to its involution image's: at
carrier elements $w$, $x$ and a window ground $\psi$
(Definition~\ref{def:pencil}),
\[
\langle w,\ x\cdot\psi\rangle\;=\;\langle w\cdot\bar\psi,\ x\rangle\ ,
\]
both sides the read $\mathrm{Eval}(\bar w\,\psi\,x)$, the products'
one value under commutativity with the involution invariance
(Definition~\ref{def:algebra}; Proposition~\ref{prop:algebra}); and
a ground's involution image is a ground: the involution keeps the pairing
($\mathrm{Eval}$ one value under the involution,
