Definition~\ref{def:algebra}) and the pencil, $E$ at the dual
label's Casimir (Definition~\ref{def:c2hat}) and $M$ at the
self-dual adjoint (Lemma~\ref{lem:adjchar}), so it keeps the level
gap's kernel. The \emph{span read} at a window and a ground is the
product matrix's rank at the window's dimension: the matrix
$\mathrm{Eval}(\bar v\,w\,\bar\psi)$ at $w$ over the window list
and $v$ over the product window's
(Proposition~\ref{prop:leastwindow}), every entry a ground
computation, the rank the elimination's read
(Definition~\ref{def:elim}), one decidable read per window and
ground. The word sector's window projection fills the window
exactly at the span read: a window element perpendicular to every
word image pairs the ground product of its involution image against every
element at the sum's unit, the display's read, so the product is
the sum's unit at the positive pairing
(Definition~\ref{def:carrier}), the matrix's kernel the
perpendicular elements' own; and at the filled projection the
compression to the window's word sector is the window pair whole.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[A window spectral read is the cut at that window]
\label{lem:speccut}
At a window and a pair $\kappa$, the spectral read
\[
\mathrm{spec}(H)\ \subset\ \{\varepsilon_0\}\cup
[\varepsilon_0+E_0\kappa,\ \infty)
\]
is the window cut $\mathcal K_\Lambda$ of Definition~\ref{def:K} at
$\kappa$, at every window ground at once: the split of Lemma~\ref{lem:split}
maps the level gap $\tilde E$ ($\varepsilon_0G+\tilde E=H$,
Definition~\ref{def:pencil}) to a diagonal at the kernel point's read
and at reads at or beyond $E_0\kappa$, the detector pair at
each reads its upper side or equal members
(Theorem~\ref{thm:detector}, the two neutral points the ground's and
the edge's), so the pair
$(\tilde E^{\,2}:E_0\kappa\,\tilde E)$ is positive semidefinite on
the window, and a compression keeps that read
(Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}): every word-sector compression reads it,
the cut's own object, the detector form compressed whole; and one
ground at the span read closes the identification both ways, its
compression the window pair whole (Lemma~\ref{lem:sectorspan}), so
that ground's instance is the spectral read outright and every
ground's follows by the display above, the window cut's edge the
spectral edge there. The reading back rides the gap's own
floor: the detector pair's side is $\lambda$'s against $\gamma$
at positive $\lambda$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:detector}), the levels'
positivity $\tilde E$'s positive-semidefinite certificate
(Definition~\ref{def:pencil}), the datum locating every root's
side. At a level on its lower side the pair's read holds outright,
the square sitting on its upper side from either side of its
entry: the floor's certificate is the side's one source.
\end{lemma}

\begin{theorem}[The moments are read at coefficient
one]\label{thm:coeffone}
For a \emph{multiplication} word $c$ (its products with $M$ at one
value in either order, the algebra commutative), one exact line:
\[
E(c\psi)\;=\;c\,(\varepsilon_0+\beta M)\,\psi+\tilde E\,(c\psi)
\qquad\text{at the join}\quad E=\beta M+H:\qquad
[E,c]\,\psi=\tilde E\,(c\psi) ,
\]
so
$\omega(c^\dagger[E,c])=\langle v_c,\tilde E\,v_c
\rangle$ \emph{exactly}, on the multiplication probes' vectors: the pencil's
$\lambda$ is the Hamiltonian's own, at coefficient $1$, so both moments depend
on $A$ through $y=A\psi$ alone.
\end{theorem}

\begin{lemma}[The state space]\label{lem:statespace}
The word sector is the state's own space: at a window ground
$\psi$ and carrier elements $A$, $B$,
\[
\omega(\bar A\,B)\;=\;\langle A\psi,\,B\psi\rangle\ ,
\]
both sides the read $\mathrm{Eval}(\bar A\,B\,\bar\psi\,\psi)$,
the left at the pairing identity and the right at the
involution's product read, the products' one value under
commutativity joining the two
(Definition~\ref{def:algebra}; Proposition~\ref{prop:algebra}):
the pairing on the word sector is the state's, and the sector is
$\mathrm{Sect}_\infty\,\psi$, its own image
(Definition~\ref{def:pencil}), cyclic by construction.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[The level is attained at a word image]\label{lem:attained}
At a window, a window ground $\psi$ and the located edge $\gamma$ of
the level condition over the word sector
(Theorem~\ref{thm:levelequiv}; the window cut's own read at
$\gamma=E_0\kappa$, Definition~\ref{def:K}): \emph{(i)} the edge is
the compressed pair's least root; \emph{(ii)} a word image reads the
two moments at equality there, the first positive; \emph{(iii)} at
the window's spectral read at the edge the state's weight sits at
the level, a divisor point.

\emph{(i) The edge is the compressed pair's least root.} The compression
is finite-dimensional with pair entries
(Proposition~\ref{prop:windowfinite}; Theorem~\ref{thm:pairpencil}),
and $(\tilde E)_\Lambda$, the compression of the level gap, is
positive semidefinite, a compression keeping the read
(Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}), with the ground inside its kernel block:
$\psi$ is the unit's own word image and $\tilde E\,\psi$ the sum's
unit (Definition~\ref{def:algebra}; Definition~\ref{def:pencil}). A
kernel point $y$ of $(\tilde E)_\Lambda$ reads its self-pairing at
equal members, so $\tilde E\,y$ is the sum's unit: $\tilde E$ is
positive semidefinite (Definition~\ref{def:pencil}) and its split
reads the pairing as the fold over the blocks
(Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}), a fold of equal members putting the
coordinates off the kernel block at the sum's unit, so $y$ sits in
that block's span and its image with it. Then
$(\tilde E^{\,2})_\Lambda$ reads its row at $y$ at the sum's unit as
well, the pencil symmetric with
$\langle x,\tilde E^{\,2}y\rangle=\langle\tilde Ex,\tilde Ey\rangle$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:pairpencil}): both compressions sit on the
split's own columns off the kernel block, the complement list,
where $(\tilde E)_\Lambda$ is positive
definite (Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}). There Lemma~\ref{lem:split}
splits the pair
$\big((\tilde E^{\,2})_\Lambda:\gamma\,(\tilde E)_\Lambda\big)$ over
located roots, $\mathrm{count}(a)$ the roots below $a$: at $\gamma$
at or below the least root the roots sit at or beyond the level and
the pair is positive semidefinite, the congruence's diagonal
$\langle x\!:\!\varepsilon_j\rangle\,g_j$ reading its upper side or
equal members there, the least root's own read the sharpened one
(Theorem~\ref{thm:certconstruct}(1)),
while beyond that root the count is at least one and
Theorem~\ref{thm:certconstruct}(2) reads the count's own witness off
the elimination. So the level condition's
members are the levels at or below the least root and the located
edge is that root, the window cut's own edge at $\gamma=E_0\kappa$.

\emph{(ii) Its kernel point is a word image, and the two moments read
equal members there.} At the edge the site datum $Z$ at the join
$\gamma\,(\tilde E)_\Lambda=(\tilde E^{\,2})_\Lambda+Z$ has a kernel
point $y$ off the sum's unit
(Lemma~\ref{lem:split}'s kernel block at a located root), a vector
of the word sector and hence $A\psi$ at a word $A$, the sector being
the image of multiplication by $\psi$ with $A\mapsto A\psi$ linear
(Definition~\ref{def:pencil}; Theorem~\ref{thm:momentform}); $y$
sits on the complement list, where the compressed level gap is
positive definite, so its first moment is positive, and the kernel
read gives the second at the level's multiple,
\[
\mu_2(A)\;=\;\gamma\,\mu_1(A)\ ,
\qquad\mu_1(A)\ \text{positive},
\]
the moments read at coefficient one on the multiplication probes'
vectors (Theorem~\ref{thm:coeffone};
Corollary~\ref{cor:momentdivisor}). The level-$\gamma$ requirement
therefore holds at equality at that one word, the detector pair's
edge its neutral point (Theorem~\ref{thm:detector}), and the floor
over the probes (Theorem~\ref{thm:momentform}) is met at a probe of
its own. The edge sits off equal members with it: at equal members
the second moment reads them, its display
$\langle\tilde E\,y,\tilde E\,y\rangle$ putting $\tilde E\,y$ at the
sum's unit at the positive pairing
(Definition~\ref{def:carrier}) and the first moment at equal members
with it, against its positivity.

\emph{(iii) At the window's spectral read the weight sits at the level, a
divisor point.} Where the window reads
$\mathrm{spec}(H)\subset\{\varepsilon_0\}\cup
[\varepsilon_0+\gamma,\infty)$, the jump comparisons' own object
(Theorem~\ref{thm:flatstep}) and the cut's identification at a
ground at the span read (Lemma~\ref{lem:speccut};
Lemma~\ref{lem:sectorspan}), the two moments are the weight folds
over the located spectrum (Theorem~\ref{thm:detector}): the split's
congruence diagonalizes the gram and the pencil at once
(Lemma~\ref{lem:split}), so the level gap acts at the gaps
$\lambda_j$ there and its square at their squares, the weights $w_j$
the coordinates' squares against the gram's upper-side diagonal
entries, and
\[
\mu_1(A)=\sum_jw_j\,\lambda_j\ ,\qquad
\mu_2(A)=\sum_jw_j\,\lambda_j^{\,2}\ ,\qquad
\big\langle\,\mu_2(A)\!:\!\gamma\,\mu_1(A)\,\big\rangle
\;=\;\sum_j w_j\,\lambda_j\,
\big\langle\,\lambda_j\!:\!\gamma\,\big\rangle\ .
\]
Every $\lambda_j$ is the kernel point's read or sits at or beyond
$\gamma$, so every summand of the fold reads its upper side or equal
members, while the display above reads its left side at equal
members: gaps add over a fold, and a natural fold reads the sum's
unit exactly where every summand does
(Definition~\ref{def:ground}), so at every occupied weight
$\lambda_j$ is the kernel point's read or the level $\gamma$
exactly, and $\mu_1(A)$ positive puts an occupied weight at
$\gamma$. The level is then a located root of the window pencil, a
point of its divisor at that root's multiplicity
(Lemma~\ref{lem:split}; Theorem~\ref{thm:divisorid}(i)), the word
image's weight there positive, and its state is the word sector's
own, the state's space (Lemma~\ref{lem:statespace}).
\end{lemma}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{The window ground}

\begin{theorem}[The window multiplicity is a count, and the count is a
divisor read]\label{thm:SO}
On a window the ground multiplicity $\dim\ker\tilde E$ is one
integer, a count at a designation's data; it is the ground
root's multiplicity, one integer per cell of the crossing divisor,
reading $1$ at the free end and over its attached cell, and
moving across a located boundary only at a ground collision, the
boundary's own two-sided read
(Theorem~\ref{thm:divisorid}(ii),(iii)). Where the window read is $1$, a
symmetry of the window, an orthogonal map commuting with the pencil,
maps the ground line to itself at a graded read, $g\psi=\psi$ at
even and $g\psi+\psi$ the sum's unit at odd, the reads of $\omega$
blind to the grading.
\begin{proof}
\emph{The multiplicity is a count.}
A window compression is finite-dimensional with pair entries
(Proposition~\ref{prop:windowfinite}), and
$\mathrm{count}(a)=\operatorname{rev}(H\!:\!aG)$ counts its eigenvalues with
multiplicity below $a$, exact over the pairs by $\mathrm{LDL}^{\mathsf T}$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:certconstruct}; the multiplicity read is
Lemma~\ref{lem:split}). Theorem~\ref{thm:windowsep} gives a pair
$\delta$ below the separation of the pencil pair's site-datum
determinant's distinct located roots (Lemma~\ref{lem:split}'s $\chi$),
and a designation at that width (Theorem~\ref{thm:certconstruct})
reads levels $l<h$ at a gap at most $\delta$,
the pencil pair at $l$ positive semidefinite and
$\mathrm{count}(h)\ge1$. The bracket is narrower than the separation, so it
admits the ground root alone and
\[
\dim\ker\tilde E\;=\;\mathrm{count}(h) ,
\]
one count at the designation's data.

\emph{The count is a divisor read.} The multiplicity is the ground
root's order in the pencil determinant, the split's diagonal read
(Lemma~\ref{lem:split}), and
Theorem~\ref{thm:divisorid}(ii),(iii) lock it: one integer per cell of
the crossing divisor, read $1$ from the free end's level bracket over
the attached cell (every configuration's content is positive, the
unit's line the ground, Lemma~\ref{lem:freeend}), and one two-sided bracket read at
each located boundary.

\emph{The character.} At a window read of $1$, a symmetry $g$ has
$Hg=gH$, so it preserves the ground eigenspace, which is the line; an
orthogonal map on a line is a scalar $c$ at $c^2=1$
($g^{\mathsf T}g=1$), so $c=1$ or $c+1$ reads equal members; and
$\omega(g^{\mathsf T}Ag)=\langle g\psi,\,A\,g\psi\rangle
=c^2\,\omega(A)=\omega(A)$: the state reads its ground twice, an even
count, blind to the grading.
\end{proof}
\end{theorem}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{The integer constants}

With every pair at one value across the form's scales
(Proposition~\ref{prop:form}), every
constant below is literally an integer or a fusion pair: $4$ (links
per plaquette, the module energy); $2d$ (the neighbor count,
Construction~\ref{con:lattice}'s direction read); the winding floor $\hat c_2(\omega_{c_i})$ per cell and charged
direction, the charged margin's growth (Lemma~\ref{lem:chargedcell}); and the cells'
entries and levels, located roots and jump distances of the decimation's own
divisors, outputs rather than constants. The residue enters through
one pair, $\hat c_2(f)=[\,r(r+2):2d_f^{\,2}\,]$, the minimal content,
its two reads the floor's product (Lemma~\ref{lem:casfloor}) and the
clearing's count (Definition~\ref{def:c2hat}) at the one-value
identity $r(r+2)+1=d_f^{\,2}$, the $A$-series' read with the
member floors the end lists' (Lemma~\ref{lem:casfloor}); the
adjoint read against it is the
invariant $[\,2d_f^{\,2}:r(r+2)\,]$, and every further residue read of
the chain is a coordinate read of the cell calculus
(Lemma~\ref{lem:rankstable}(iii)).
What reads $4n$, $8n$, $2n$, $n$ in the dictionary's units
(Remark~\ref{rem:dictionary}) was that integer times the Casimir.

%=====================================================================
\section{The two-plaquette object}

\begin{construction}[Two-plaquette fiber]\label{con:fiber}
Fix the theta-graph of two square plaquettes sharing one link. The
\emph{invariant fiber} is the span of the permutation-presentation states
in the two plaquette variables (Construction~\ref{con:states}), with the pairing identity of Proposition~\ref{prop:algebra} through the
presentation Gram (\S\ref{sec:wg}).
\end{construction}

\begin{construction}[The two-plaquette data]\label{con:twoplaq}
On the invariant fiber of Construction~\ref{con:fiber}, the electric
operator's link weights are $(3,1,3)$, \emph{forced by the graph}: each plaquette
has four links, one of which is shared, so three unshared each,
\[
E \;=\; 3\,\Delta_U \;+\; \Delta_L \;+\; 3\,\Delta_V ,
\]
$\Delta_U,\Delta_V$ the plaquette Laplacians, $\Delta_L$ the
shared-link Casimir (simultaneous right translation, \S\ref{sec:ops}).
Energies and the coupling are read against $C_2(\mathrm{adj})$
(Proposition~\ref{prop:form}), so
$\hat c_2(f)=[\,r(r+2):2d_f^{\,2}\,]$,
$\hat c_2(\mathrm{adj})=1$, while the section's displayed operators
read at the trace form itself, $C_2(\mathrm{adj})=d_f$ there, the
weight clause below the conversion. The magnetic operator is $\chi_X$ per plaquette
(Construction~\ref{con:xfusion}).
Module states: $\Phi_{q_1}=\chi_{\mathrm{adj}}(U)$,
$\Phi_{q_2}=\chi_{\mathrm{adj}}(V)$, on which this graph's $E$ reads
$4\,C_2(\mathrm{adj})$, the
carrier's own link count $E_0=4$ at the weight
(Proposition~\ref{prop:E0}), here the weights reading
$3\,d_f+1\,d_f$ with the $\Delta_V$ read the sum's unit
($\Phi_{q_1}$ free of $V$). A Feshbach coefficient
(Construction~\ref{con:res}) on this
graph reads
\[
\big\langle \Phi_{q_2},\, M\,x\big\rangle,
\qquad
x\ \text{the solve of Construction~\ref{con:res} at }M\cdots\Phi_{q_1} ,
\]
one solve per resolvent step at the pair $(E\!:\!4d_f)$'s site
datum, $Q$ the projection off the two module
states (the site datum's kernel the module space,
Construction~\ref{con:res}), the inner product the pairing
identity's read (Proposition~\ref{prop:algebra}). The magnetic
multiplications read per plaquette: $\chi_X(W)$ at a state extends
its factor list at the variable's pair
(Construction~\ref{con:states}'s product at the pair's
presentation, Lemma~\ref{lem:adjchar}), so a $k$-step image is one state
per plaquette word of length $k$, each at its own factor list, the
solves at each word's stated list and the displayed coefficient
the pairing identity's fold over the words, a presentation state
sitting at one factor list with the algebra's sums reading through
the folds; a $k$-solve coefficient pairs with
$C_2(\mathrm{adj})^{k}$ to a weight-free read, one value at every
form point (Proposition~\ref{prop:form}).
\end{construction}

\section{The permutation presentation}

\begin{construction}[States]\label{con:states}
Fix a factor list $F=(v_1,\dots,v_m)$, each \emph{factor} a
variable $v_i\in\{U,V\}$ with its \emph{dagger read}, undaggered or
daggered. To a permutation
$\pi\in S_m$ associate
\[
\Phi_\pi(U,V)\;=\;\prod_{\text{cycles } (i_1\,i_2\cdots i_k)\ \text{of}\ \pi}
\tr\!\big(M_{i_1}M_{i_2}\cdots M_{i_k}\big),
\]
$M_i$ reading $v_i$ at an undaggered factor and $v_i^{\dagger}$ at a
daggered.
States are the $\Phi_\pi$'s combinations at polynomial-pair
coefficients in the residue (Lemma~\ref{lem:genericlift}). The
states' product is the sites' concatenation: two generators
multiply at the joined factor list, the second wiring's places
shifted by the first list's count, the joined wiring the two
blocks, and the coefficients multiply, the trace polynomials' own
product. Equivalently
$\pi(i)=j$ records that the row index of factor $i$ is contracted
against the column index of factor $j$. This is the trace-polynomial
reduction of the walled Brauer category: the Schur--Weyl dual
presentation, in which the group enters only as the loop value $d_f$
of the calculus.
\end{construction}

\begin{remark}[The word index, and the presentation kernel]
\label{rem:kernel}
The states are indexed gauge-free by the multisets of oriented cyclic
words in the four letters: $\Phi_\pi$ reads only its cycles' words, each
up to rotation, so the positions of repeated factors are a gauge mode of
the $(\pi,\text{positions})$ spelling, and a state's word coefficient is
the class sum of its $\pi$-coefficients, one finite projection at every
residue. Identities of character polynomials close on the word index outright,
coefficient comparisons per word multiset before any pairing, holding at
every residue; the Gram below enters only at the state reads. The word
index's one further datum is the
\emph{kernel}: the map from word monomials to functions has one, the
evaluation identity
$\sum_jU_{ij}\bar U_{kj}=\delta_{ik}$ (Construction~\ref{con:coeff})
imposing polynomial-pair relations among the $\Phi_\pi$ within a factor
list. All function-level linear algebra is
therefore performed through the pairing's Gram (\S\ref{sec:wg}); the null
space of that Gram is the presentation kernel, computed once per
factor list. Determinant ($\varepsilon$-tensor)
relations are invisible at the symbolic residue; this is precisely the statement that the calculus computes the
generic theory, and it is why $r=1$ is its own window read while $r\ge2$ reads
generically.
\end{remark}

\section{The electric operators as group-algebra insertions}
\label{sec:ops}

\begin{proposition}[Plaquette Laplacian]\label{prop:lap}
The factor list's $W$-factors split by their dagger read
(Construction~\ref{con:states}), $W\in\{U,V\}$, and
$c_f=C_2(f)=[\,r(r+2):2d_f\,]$ is the trace
form's own read, the section's displayed operators' scale. On states of
Construction~\ref{con:states} the insertion identity reads
$d_f$-cleared, each sum the fold over its stated family of
$W$-factors and the single-index sum over them all:
\begin{gather*}
d_f\,\Delta_W\,\pi
\;+\!\!\sum_{\substack{i<j\\ \text{undaggered}}}\!\!\pi
\;+\!\!\sum_{\substack{i<j\\ \text{daggered}}}\!\!\pi
\;+\;d_f\!\!\sum_{\substack{i\ \text{undaggered}\\ j\ \text{daggered}}}\!\!\mathrm{C}_{rc}(i,j)\,\pi\\
{}=\;
d_f\,c_f\sum_{i}\pi
\;+\!\!\sum_{\substack{i\ \text{undaggered}\\ j\ \text{daggered}}}\!\!\pi
\;+\;d_f\!\!\sum_{\substack{i<j\\ \text{undaggered}}}\!\!\pi\circ(i\,j)
\;+\;d_f\!\!\sum_{\substack{i<j\\ \text{daggered}}}\!\!(i\,j)\circ\pi\ ,\\
\mathrm{C}_{rc}(i,j)\,\pi=
\begin{cases} d_f\,\pi, & \pi(i)=j,\\[2pt]
\pi\circ(i\ k)\ \ \text{at the witness $k$ at $\pi(k)=j$}, & \text{else,}
\end{cases}
\end{gather*}
the witness $k$ at $\pi(k)=j$ off the permutation's own transpose
data, the inverse permutation (Construction~\ref{con:places}).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}[Derivation and verification]
$\Delta_W$, the per-variable summand of Definition~\ref{def:pencil}'s
diagonal $E$, is read through
the coefficient carrier's derivation action
(Construction~\ref{con:coeff}; Definition~\ref{def:generators}'s
$\Delta$ at the $W$-factors): each generator differentiates each
$W$-factor once by the Leibniz rule, an undaggered factor reading its
insertion at the row index and a daggered at the column index with the
memberwise swap, the transpose identity's side, so $\Delta_W$ folds over
the factor pairs, and each distinct pair's generator fold is the
Fierz display of Definition~\ref{def:generators}, $d_f$-cleared. A
within-factor pair reads the factor's own Casimir, the count $c_f$
per factor; a one-sided pair reads the Fierz's transposition member, right
multiplication at the undaggered pairs and left at the daggered; a
mixed pair reads the memberwise-swapped fold, its transposition term
joining the display's left side, the contraction $\mathrm{C}_{rc}$ at
the wiring through $\pi$, and the closed case $\pi(i)=j$ reads the
loop value $d_f$ of the calculus (Construction~\ref{con:states}). Every
case coefficient is the Fierz display's own read; the identity
instances: $\Delta_U\tr U=c_f\tr
U$; the unit $\mathbf 1$ is $\Delta_U$'s kernel point on the
presentation
$\mathbf 1=[\,1:d_f\,]\,\tr(UU^{\dagger})$; $\Delta_U\chi_{\mathrm{adj}}
=d_f\,\chi_{\mathrm{adj}}$; and the graded squares,
\begin{gather*}
d_f\,\Delta_U\,s+2\,s\;=\;d_f\,(d_f+1)\,s
\quad\text{at}\quad s=(\tr U)^2+\tr U^2 ,\\
d_f\,\Delta_U\,w+(d_f+2)\,w\;=\;d_f^{\,2}\,w
\quad\text{at}\quad w=\big\langle(\tr U)^2\!:\!\tr U^2\big\rangle
\end{gather*}
all cleared polynomial
identities in the residue.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Shared-link Casimir]\label{prop:link}
$\Delta_L$ is the Casimir of the simultaneous right translation,
Definition~\ref{def:generators}'s $\Delta$ read through the joint
derivation moving both variables at once, each factor of either
variable differentiated once (Construction~\ref{con:coeff}). Its
insertion identity is Proposition~\ref{prop:lap}'s at the sides
exchanged, the column index for an undaggered factor and the row for
a daggered, all factors of both variables participating, and a factor
pair joins the display at its dagger reads: a matched pair, two
undaggered or two daggered, reads its transposition term on the
display's right side, and a mixed pair on its left,
Proposition~\ref{prop:lap}'s collection. The identity
instances: $\Delta_L\tr U=c_f\tr U$,
$\Delta_L\chi_{\mathrm{adj}}(U)=d_f\,\chi_{\mathrm{adj}}(U)$, and the
cross-variable graded reads,
\begin{gather*}
d_f\,\Delta_L\,s+2\,s\;=\;d_f\,(d_f+1)\,s
\quad\text{at}\quad s=\tr U\,\tr V+\tr(UV) ,\\
d_f\,\Delta_L\,w+(d_f+2)\,w\;=\;d_f^{\,2}\,w
\quad\text{at}\quad w=\big\langle\tr U\,\tr V\!:\!\tr(UV)\big\rangle
\end{gather*}
with the module energy
$E\chi_{\mathrm{adj}}=4d_f\,\chi_{\mathrm{adj}}$, the count
$E_0=4$ at the weight. The link translation
acts through the site conjugation at $g_s$,
$U\mapsto U\,g_sXg_s^{\dagger}$ at the site unitary $g_s$, its
dagger read $g_s^{\dagger}g_s=1$ the evaluation identity's
(Construction~\ref{con:coeff}); on the fiber invariant under that
site conjugation the conjugation is absorbed, the read the proposition
takes.
\end{proposition}

\section{The pairing in the permutation presentation}\label{sec:wg}

\begin{lemma}[The generic lift]\label{lem:genericlift}
A \emph{polynomial pair} in the residue is a pair of ground
polynomials named as Definition~\ref{def:ground}'s constructed
pairs are, read at every natural argument through the members' Horner
reads and across representatives by the cross-multiplied identities;
every entry below is one such pair.
\emph{(i)} A polynomial pair whose reads at
infinitely many naturals are of equal members reads equal members at
every argument: its first member's located roots sit inside
Theorem~\ref{thm:windowsep}'s bound, finitely many.
\emph{(ii)} A matrix of polynomial pairs whose evaluation at infinitely many
residues is the Gram of an independent list has its determinant pair
off equal members at each such residue, (i) lifting the read, and
every unit read is solved by the adjugate column against the
determinant (Definition~\ref{def:elim}'s minors), the identity
$\mathsf M\,\mathrm{adj}(\mathsf M)=\det(\mathsf M)\,1$ the
solved witness's whole verification.
\emph{(iii)} A polynomial pair keeps one side beyond its members'
largest located root, the leading-coefficient read at
Theorem~\ref{thm:windowsep}'s leading-term bound: the settled side,
one decidable read per pair.
\emph{(iv)} A claim whose per-residue reads are the sides and
equal-member reads of finitely many polynomial pairs in the residue
therefore holds at
every residue beyond their computed radii once its symbolic reads
hold, and the finitely many residues at or below the radii are each
their own window read.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proposition}[Pairing]\label{prop:wg}
For states $A,B$ on factor lists $F_A,F_B$, the pairing
$\langle A,B\rangle=\mathrm{Eval}\big(\overline{\Phi_A}\,\Phi_B\big)$ of
Proposition~\ref{prop:algebra} is computed in the presentation by: (i)
the involution flips daggers and transposes permutations, the
transpose the inverse permutation (Construction~\ref{con:places}); (ii) per variable, with
$k$ undaggered and $k$ daggered factors (else the pairing reads equal
members), the
contraction sum
\[
\sum_{\sigma,\tau\in S_k}\Wg\!\big(\sigma\tau^{\mathsf T};d_f\big)
\prod_r \delta_{i_r l_{\sigma(r)}}\,\delta_{j_r k_{\tau(r)}},
\qquad
\Wg:\ \ \mathsf G\,w=e_{\mathrm{id}},\quad \mathsf G_{\alpha\beta}
=d_f^{\,\#\mathrm{cycles}(\alpha\beta^{\mathsf T})} ;
\]
(iii) the $\delta$-wirings close the permutation chains into loops, each
loop contributing a factor $d_f$.
Every output of the computation reads at one of two \emph{tags}:
\emph{symbolic}, one polynomial pair in the residue at its
\emph{clearance radius}, the factor count, read at every residue
beyond the radius (Lemma~\ref{lem:genericlift}); or
\emph{direct}, the residue's own contraction read through its Gram
with its presentation kernel (Remark~\ref{rem:kernel}), at each
residue at or below the radius. A symbolic read enters a residue's
comparison beyond its radius alone, so a window at or below a
radius reads directly, the kernel's relations read where they
occupy its factor lists. At a $B$, $C$ or $D$ member the
presentation runs at the defining factors' pair partitions with
the member Gram (Lemma~\ref{lem:serpairing}): a daggered factor
reads its slots exchanged at the form's twist, its entry the fold
of the dual pair's coefficient, the factor's own entry and the
form's, $\sum_{a,b}c_{xa}\,U_{ba}\,b_{by}$ at the dual pair $c$
and the form $b$, the defining table's two data
(Construction~\ref{con:sertables}; Lemma~\ref{lem:serpairing}),
and per variable the contraction sum runs over the partition
pairs,
\[
\sum_{P,Q}\Wg\big(P,Q\big)\
\prod_{(u,v)\in P}\!c_{x_ux_v}\
\prod_{(u,v)\in Q}\!c_{y_uy_v}\
\prod_j w_{y_j}\,,
\qquad
\Wg:\ \ \mathsf G^{\mathrm{ser}}\,\Wg\;=\;1\,,
\]
the row slots at the dual pair's coefficients alone and the
column slots at the pairing's weights $w$ beside them (a matrix
entry is a coordinate, so the pairing's weight enters at the
paired member alone), $\mathsf G^{\mathrm{ser}}$ the member Gram
at the loops' fold with the solve the adjugate against the
determinant (Lemma~\ref{lem:serpairing};
Lemma~\ref{lem:genericlift}(ii)); the wirings close the chains
into loops as the $A$-series' display's do, each loop one value:
the dimension $r+3$ at $B$ and $D$, the twist pair's weighted
product reading the identity at the dual pair against the form,
and the doubled rank $2r$ at $C$ at the loop's parity, half the
loop's edge count joined to the count of dual-pair edges read at
their displayed order and of form edges read against theirs, each
edge the form's multiple with the form's square reading the
unit's balance partner; and an odd factor count's
symbolic read equal members, the even powers the span's own
carrier (Lemma~\ref{lem:serpairing}); the two tags at
the rank clearance, the factor count with the $D$ series at its
successor; a fixed member's outputs
read direct outright, the finite arithmetic of its displayed
member data (Construction~\ref{con:sertables}).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}[Derivation and verification]
Per variable, a product of $k$ coefficients against $k$ involution images is a
monomial coefficient of the mixed power
$V^{\otimes k}\otimes(V^{\otimes k})^\vee$, and $\mathrm{Eval}$
reads its unit component (Construction~\ref{con:coeff}): the
family splits orthogonally over the blocks
(Lemma~\ref{lem:orthsplit}), so that component is the $(a,b)$
entry of the invariant projection, a spanning invariant list's
adjugate solve at its Gram, the membership residual's own read
(Lemma~\ref{lem:lowerspan}). At
$k\le d_f$ the invariants are the permutation span
(Lemma~\ref{lem:mixedinv}), the projection is the span's Gram
projection, and its entries are the displayed contraction sum at the
Gram's solved column, $\Wg$ the adjugate column against the
determinant with $\mathsf G\,\Wg=e_{\mathrm{id}}$ its whole
verification: the Gram's entries
are residue naturals reading $\sigma\tau^{\mathsf T}$ alone, the
transpose the inverse permutation
(Construction~\ref{con:places}), the solve
Lemma~\ref{lem:genericlift}(ii)'s at the per-residue Grams of
the independent permutation list, and the
$\delta$-wirings are the permutations' monomial entries; a product at unequal daggered and undaggered counts reads equal
members there, its two degrees differing by less than a full
column beyond the factor count. A scheme output at a fixed factor list is one
polynomial pair in the residue agreeing with the per-residue read at every
$k\le d_f$, so the identities hold as cleared polynomials in the
residue
(Lemma~\ref{lem:genericlift}(i)), the generic theory of
Remark~\ref{rem:kernel}. \emph{The direct tag's read.} At a
residue at or below the radius the read runs at the residue's own
carrier. A variable's cycle word reduces at the evaluation
identity (Construction~\ref{con:coeff}): the daggered factors
withdraw against the undaggered along the cycle, the word reads
the net power, and a cycle at equal counts reads the residue's own
count, a scalar factor; a state's variable datum is its
\emph{winding pair}, the net powers' occupancy family at the
undaggered side against the daggered side's, the read at every
wiring whose cycles each sit at one variable. The power word
expands over the alternants at the residue's count: the product
$p_\mu\,a_u$ is graded and regroups over the strictly decreasing
exponents (Theorem~\ref{thm:weylchar}'s conclusion walk at the
residue's variables), the \emph{character read}
$\chi^\lambda(\mu)$ its extraction,
\[
p_\mu\,a_u\;=\;\sum_{\lambda}\chi^\lambda(\mu)\,a_{\lambda+u}\,,
\]
one balance pair per shape of the degree at rows within the
residue, so $p_\mu=\sum_\lambda\chi^\lambda(\mu)\,\mathrm{ch}_\lambda$
over those shapes, the division at $a_u$ exact, $a_u=e\,V$ a
product of monic factors (Theorem~\ref{thm:weylchar}'s assembly).
The characters are orthonormal for the evaluation
(Proposition~\ref{prop:repring}), so two states pair at the
residue at the \emph{character fold}: per variable one shape read
at both winding sides,
$\sum_\lambda\chi^\lambda(\alpha)\,\chi^\lambda(\beta)$ at the
matched totals, the off-diagonal shape pairs withdrawing at the
orthonormality, the scalar factors' count multiplied through, and
an unmatched total reading equal members. The direct member read
is the at-residue Gram's own row read: at a factor list whose
factors sit at one variable, a combination at coefficients
occupied at the residue is the presentation kernel's member
exactly where its character-fold pairing reads the sum's unit
against every wiring of the list: the generators span the
carrier, so rows at that unit read the self-pairing's fold at it
and the pairing's definiteness closes the member, while
a member's rows are the null function's own
(Remark~\ref{rem:kernel}; Construction~\ref{con:coeff}); a
coefficient whose second member reaches the unit at the residue
sits off the read, the occupancy its own conjunct, and a wiring
at a crossing cycle reads at the factor list's own Gram,
Remark~\ref{rem:kernel}'s computation. The
scheme's identity instances read at the fusion
reads of
Proposition~\ref{prop:repring}:
$\langle\tr U,\tr U\rangle=1$, $\langle\mathbf1,\mathbf1
\rangle=1$, $\langle\chi_{\mathrm{adj}},\chi_{\mathrm{adj}}\rangle=1$,
$\langle\mathbf1,\chi_{\mathrm{adj}}\rangle$ of equal members,
$\mathrm{Eval}\big(|\tr U|^4\big)=2$, and
$\langle(\tr U)^2,\tr U^2\rangle$ of equal members; and the
closed form
\[
\Wg(\mathrm{id};S_4)=\big[\,\langle d_f^{\,4}+6:8d_f^{\,2}\rangle
\;:\;d_f^{\,2}\,g_1g_2g_3\,\big]
\qquad\text{at the gaps }k^2+g_k=d_f^{\,2},
\]
the gaps' existence the four-factor read's own validity ($k\le
d_f$ at the independent span), is reproduced by the adjugate solve. All are
cleared polynomial identities in the residue.
\end{proof}

\section{Resolvent, module deflation, and the presentation kernel}

\begin{construction}[The Feshbach resolvent at polynomial pairs]\label{con:res}
Given a state $y$ on factor list $F$:
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em,itemsep=1pt]
\item \emph{The deflated side}: for each module presentation
$m$ in $F$ (built as $\chi_{\mathrm{adj}}(W)$ times paired
$[1\!:\!d_f]$-contractions of the other factors), the deflation
$y'$ at the join
$\langle m,m\rangle\,y'+\langle m,y\rangle\,m
=\langle m,m\rangle\,y$, its $m$-pairing reading equal members, the
fold over the presentations in list order, each join at the prior
deflation.
\item \emph{The support}: the union of the $\pi$-support of $y'$ and
of the presentation kernel (Remark~\ref{rem:kernel}), closed
under the $E$-insertions (Proposition~\ref{prop:lap};
Proposition~\ref{prop:link}), the closure after the
union (the insertions map the joined support into it).
\item \emph{The solve}: $S\,x+k=y'$ at a presentation-kernel
member $k$, the member's read the symbolic tag's with a window at
or below the clearance radius reading its own kernel's members
(Proposition~\ref{prop:wg}; Remark~\ref{rem:kernel}), and $S$ the
site datum at the module level, the section's pair
$(E\!:\!4d_f)$'s, one exact
adjugate solve over the polynomial pairs, null-space augmented
with the member the augmentation's read
(Lemma~\ref{lem:genericlift}(ii)), then
\item \emph{The solution's deflation}: the solution's component off
the module space, the join of step 1:
$E$ is diagonal in $\hat c_2$, so at the generic residue every non-module gap
reads unequal members as a polynomial pair and the pair
$(E\!:\!4d_f)$'s site datum has the module space as its kernel. The
result is the Feshbach $Q$-resolvent applied to $y$.
\end{enumerate}
The solve's error is the sum's unit \emph{as a function}: the residual
$w$'s self-pairing reads equal members as a polynomial pair.
\end{construction}

\section{The invariant problem: the $X$-sector block data}

The problem statement's invariant object is the walk on the category
generated by the self-dual object $X$, and it restricts the
computation to the $X$-generated sector of the existing
calculus, with $M=\sum_i\chi_X(U_i)$.

\begin{remark}[The certificate target]
\label{rem:certtarget}
$\chi_X$ conserves the per-cell box charge, so within the $X$-theory the
restriction to the $X$-sector is a superselection fact;
the sector's two data are Construction~\ref{con:xfusion}'s, its
block reads and structural identities the certificate surface
(Theorem~\ref{thm:xdata}; Theorem~\ref{thm:network};
Theorem~\ref{thm:drift}), and the bridge
to the fundamental theory is Theorem~\ref{thm:bridge}'s $2d_f$.
\end{remark}

\begin{lemma}[The adjoint square's channels]\label{lem:channels}
The blocks of $\theta\otimes\theta$ are, at every residue, the labels
\[
\begin{gathered}
\mathbf 1,\qquad \theta,\qquad 2\theta=(4,2^{\,a}),\\
2\omega_1{+}\omega_2^*=(3,1^{\,b}),\qquad
\omega_2{+}2\omega_1^*=(3,3,2^{\,b}),\qquad
\omega_2{+}\omega_2^*=(2,2,1^{\,e}),
\end{gathered}
\]
named by their weights over the fundamentals and the complements,
$\omega_j$ and $\omega_i^*$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:casfloor};
Lemma~\ref{lem:dualread}(ii)), at the run counts $a$, $b$, $e$ at
$a+2=d_f$, $b+3=d_f$ and $e+4=d_f$, at multiplicities
$1,\,c_1,\,1,\,1,\,1,\,1$,
with $c_1=2$ at $r\ge2$ and $1$ at $r=1$; the outer pair are duals,
the complement arithmetic of Construction~\ref{con:labels} the
manifest swap of the names at $\omega_j^{\ast}=\omega_{j'}$, every
index pair joining to $r+1$, and the other rows are
self-dual. The list runs at every residue with each row entering
at its count $m\,d$: the closed forms read the drops themselves,
the dual
pair's $d$ with the factor $g$ at $g+1=r$, the residue's gap from one,
and the last
row's the root $r=2$, while at $r=1$ the row list is the
alternant read's, the run $4,\dots,d_f{+}1$ of $y$ occupied
exactly at $r\ge2$.
The unit channel enters at count one on the unit line, the
Casimir's kernel point, and the nonunit channels close in
the residue:
\[
\begin{array}{lccc}
\text{label} & m & d & \hat c_2\\
\hline
\theta & c_1 & r\,(r+2) & 1\\
2\theta & 1 & [\,(r+1)^2r\,(r+4):4\,] & [\,2r+4:r+1\,]\\
2\omega_1{+}\omega_2^*,\ \omega_2{+}2\omega_1^* & 1 &
[\,g\,r\,(r+2)(r+3):4\,]_{\,g+1=r} & 2\\
\omega_2{+}\omega_2^* & 1 & [\,(r+1)^2(r+2)\,w:4\,]_{\,w+2=r} & [\,2r:r+1\,]
\end{array}
\]
with the two closure identities
\[
\sum_Rm_Rd_R=r^2(r+2)^2,
\qquad
\sum_Rm_Rd_R\,\hat c_2(R)=2\,r^2(r+2)^2 ,
\]
the displayed reads of the proof, the sums over all blocks with the
unit channel's count one among them and its Casimir term's members
equal. The module's distances close in the residue as well:
against $\hat c_2(\theta)=1$, the gaps of the Casimir column read
$1$ at the unit channel and at the dual pair,
$[\,g:r+1\,]$ at $\omega_2{+}\omega_2^*$ over its gap witness
$g+1=r$, and $[\,r+3:r+1\,]$ at
$2\theta$, at the cross-multiplied witnesses $2r=(r+1)+g$ and
$2r+4=(r+1)+(r+3)$: each gap is positive at its row's residues,
one symbolic read at every one of them.
\begin{proof}
Corollary~\ref{cor:steinberg} at $a=b=\theta$: a permutation contributes
where the tie $\nu_\sigma+\sigma(\theta+u)=c+u$ meets a
shifted content of $\theta$, $u$ the unit set's sorted display,
$(1^{d_f})$ at multiplicity $r$ or its letter-pair moves at one each
(Lemma~\ref{lem:adjchar}), so, writing $y:=\theta+u+(1^{d_f})$, of value
multiset $\{2,4,5,\dots,d_f{+}1,\,d_f{+}3\}$, a label contributes only where
the strictly decreasing $c+u$ rearranges $y$ or $y$ with one entry
raised and one lowered by $1$, the result's entries distinct. Equality is
$c=\theta$ at matched degree, the count $2$ at $2+g=r$ against the
run's adjacent value pairs, $g$ their count: the identity's
read $r$ at the unit-monomial content on the even side against one odd
transposition per adjacent
value pair, the pairs sitting in the run $4,\dots,d_f{+}1$. The modifications
with distinct entries are exactly five: $2\to3$ with $d_f{+}3\to d_f{+}2$
(the unit label at matched degree); $2\to1$ with $d_f{+}3\to d_f{+}4$
($2\theta$); $4\to3$ with $d_f{+}3\to d_f{+}4$ ($2\omega_1{+}\omega_2^*$);
$2\to1$ with
$d_f{+}1\to d_f{+}2$ ($\omega_2{+}2\omega_1^*$); $4\to3$ with $d_f{+}1\to d_f{+}2$
($\omega_2{+}\omega_2^*$); every other
raise or lowering collides with an occupied value, the adjacent swaps
returning the equality case. Each modification preserves the decreasing
order, so the aligning permutation is the identity and each count is
one, on the even side. At $r=2$ the run $4,\dots,d_f{+}1$ is the single value $4$, so
$\omega_2{+}\omega_2^*$'s two
modified entries coincide: the row enters at $r\ge3$; the dual
pair's modifications each move one value of the run $4,\dots,d_f{+}1$,
occupied at $r\ge2$ alone, so the pair enters at $r\ge2$,
$\omega_2{+}\omega_2^*$ at $r\ge3$ as above, while equality reads
$r=1$.

\emph{The dimensions} are Corollary~\ref{cor:weyldim}'s telescopes. At
$2\theta=(4,2^{\,a})$ the factors from either end read one pair
$[\,g+2:g\,]$ over the gaps $1\le g<r$ and telescope to
$[\,(r+1)r:2\,]$ each, the
pair $(1,d_f)$ reads $[\,r+4:r\,]$, and the middle pairs read $1$:
the cofactor at $4\,d_{2\theta}=(r+1)^2r\,(r+4)$. At
$2\omega_1{+}\omega_{r-1}
=(3,1^{\,b})$, at the gap $g$ at $g+1=r$, the five
collected reads are $[\,rg:2\,]$, $[\,r+2:g\,]$, $[\,r+3:r\,]$,
$g$ and $[\,r:2\,]$:
the cofactor at $4\,d=g\,r\,(r+2)(r+3)$, the pair's second count
equal, the dual's
contents the balance partners
(Lemma~\ref{lem:dualread}). At $\omega_2{+}\omega_{r-1}
=(2,2,1^{\,e})$, at the gap $w$ at $w+2=r$, the six collected
reads are $[\,r-1:2\,]$, $[\,(r+1)(r+2):r\,(r-1)\,]$, $w$,
$[\,r\,(r+1):w\,(r-1)\,]$, $w$ and $[\,r-1:2\,]$: the
cofactor at $4\,d=(r+1)^2(r+2)\,w$.

\emph{The Casimirs} are Lemma~\ref{lem:casimir}'s bracket at the
reduced shapes: $\hat c_2(2\theta)=[\,2r+4:r+1\,]$, the $m=2$ read of the
tower law (Proposition~\ref{prop:anchor}); at $2\omega_1{+}\omega_{r-1}$,
$k=d_f$, $q=r+7$, and the top row's weight $3\,\langle r+2\!:\!2\rangle$
with the run's collecting reads
$\langle\lambda,2\rho\rangle=\langle 4r\!:\!2\rangle$, so the cleared
join $d_f+Q+2=(r+7)+4r$ reads
$Q=4\,d_f$: $\hat c_2=2$ at both members of the dual
pair; at $\omega_2{+}\omega_{r-1}$,
$k=d_f$, $q=r+5$, and the top rows' weights
$2\,\langle r+2\!:\!2\rangle+2\,\langle r+2\!:\!4\rangle$ with the
run's summing to equal members read
$\langle\lambda,2\rho\rangle=\langle 4r\!:\!4\rangle$, so the cleared
join $d_f+Q+4=(r+5)+4r$ reads $Q=4r$:
$\hat c_2(\omega_2{+}\omega_{r-1})=[\,2r:r+1\,]$.

\emph{The closure identities.} At $r\ge2$, with $v:=r\,(r+2)$ and
$x:=d_f^{\,2}$ at the join $v+1=x$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:adjchar}): the
self-dual outer rows' counts $\Sigma_o$ and the dual pair's
$\Sigma_d$ read the cross-added identities $2\Sigma_o+3x=x^2$ and
$2\Sigma_d+5x=x^2+4$, so, with the unit's one and the $\theta$ row's
$2\,v$,
\[
\sum_Rm_Rd_R+2x\;=\;x^2+1 ;
\]
and the self-dual outer rows' weighted reads collect at their common
factor to $x\,v$, the weights' tie $(r+4)+g=2\,d_f$ at the gap $g$ at
$g+2=r$ closing
it, with
the dual pair's weighted read twice its count, so
\[
\sum_Rm_Rd_R\,\hat c_2(R)+4x\;=\;2x^2+2 ,
\]
the displayed sums $v^2$ and $2v^2$, the statement's two
closes.
At $r=1$ the surviving rows read $1+3+5=9=r^2(r+2)^2$ and
$3\cdot1+5\cdot3=18=2\,r^2(r+2)^2$ directly.
\end{proof}
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[The window data are residue-stable on banded families]
\label{lem:rankstable}
A \emph{banded family} is a pair of weight words at the
fundamentals' two ends: the \emph{top word} $P$, a finite occupancy
family at $\omega_1,\omega_2,\dots$ indexed from the first
fundamental, and the \emph{bottom word} $Q$, one at the complement
fundamentals $\omega_1^*,\omega_2^*,\dots$ indexed from the last
(Lemma~\ref{lem:dualread}(ii): $\omega_i^*$'s column at the gap
$\ell$ at $i+\ell=d_f$), with
\emph{reaches} $a$ and $b$, the words' last keys; the member at the
residue $r$ is
\[
\lambda\;=\;\sum_{j\le a}P_j\,\omega_j\;+\;\sum_{i\le b}Q_i\,\omega_i^*\ ,
\]
one label per cleared residue, the \emph{reach clearance} the
residue at or beyond $a+b+2$, at least two keys off the words
between the reaches, and the member's rows are the weight family's tail sums,
$\lambda_i=\sum_{j\ge i}c_j$ at the coordinates $c$, each row one
fold over the member keys at or beyond it.
The vacuum is the unit pair, a member at every residue outright,
$\theta$ is one box on each word
($P$ at $\omega_1$, $Q$ at $\omega_1^*$, the fundamental with its
complement), and
Lemma~\ref{lem:channels}'s whole list is:
the families are the $X$-sector's labels, and the fusion-closed
interior at any cutoff and depth is one word-pair list at every
cleared residue.

\emph{(i) The Casimir and the dimension are single polynomial-pair
reads.} The
words read the rows: the \emph{height} $m$, the bottom word's
coordinate total, is the row across the run between the reaches, a
top row adds its tail sum, $\lambda_i=m+p_i$ at
$p_i=\sum_{j\ge i}P_j$ for $i\le a$, and the rows below the run
are the bottom word's prefix sums, $\sigma_t=\sum_{i\le t}Q_i$
the row at the join $\ell+t=d_f$. So $q(\lambda)$ splits into the
top rows' squares, the run term $u\,m^2$ at the \emph{run count}
$u$, $a+b+u=r+1$, and the
bottom rows' squares, each row a word read;
$\langle\lambda,2\rho\rangle=\sum_i\lambda_i\,\langle
r+2\!:\!2i\rangle$, the row weights balance pairs, splits into
word reads and the run's arithmetic sum; and the degree is
$k=\sum_j j\,P_j+\sum_i \ell_i\,Q_i$ at the complement columns
$\ell_i$, $i+\ell_i=d_f$, linear in the residue, so
the degree's square against the count, $[\,k^2:d_f\,]$, is one pair
term. Hence
$\hat c_2(\lambda)$ is one polynomial pair in the residue per family
(Lemma~\ref{lem:genericlift}), its
second member a product of integer shifts of the residue and its
powers. The dimension is one
read as well:
in Corollary~\ref{cor:weyldim}'s product the run--run factors are
$1$, and a word-against-run factor telescopes over the position gaps
$g$, at the joins $i+h=a$ and $i+b+g_2=d_f$,
\[
\prod_{h<g\le g_2}\big[\,p_i+g\,:\,g\,\big]
\;=\;\Big[\,\tbinom{p_i+g_2}{p_i}\,:\,\tbinom{p_i+h}{p_i}\,\Big]\ ,
\]
the top row's case at its tail sum $p_i$, with the bottom's its
reflection, the run against $\sigma_t$ at the row gap $\tau_{t+1}$,
$\tau_t=\sum_{i\ge t}Q_i$ the bottom tail sum at the join
$\sigma_t+\tau_{t+1}=m$, a pair of two integer binomials
polynomial in the residue each; a factor within one word is
residue-free, its rows' and its positions' gaps the word's own
data; and a cross factor, the top row $i$ against the bottom row
$\sigma_t$, is the pair $[\,p_i+\tau_{t+1}+g\,:\,g\,]$ at the
position gap's join $i+t+g=d_f$, one factor per key pair with
both members linear in the residue: $d_\lambda$ is one polynomial
pair in the residue per family.

\emph{(ii) The fusion rows are word reads, one row at every
residue.} Corollary~\ref{cor:steinberg} at the adjoint runs on
$x:=\lambda+u$, the beta-set's sorted display, on
Lemma~\ref{lem:channels}'s alignment: the
$\theta$-contents are the letter-pair moves at multiplicity one and
the unit-monomial content at $r$
(Lemma~\ref{lem:adjchar}), so the diagonal reads the unit-monomial
multiplicity against one odd transposition per unit gap
$x_i-x_{i+1}=1$, and a gap reads its weight key,
$x_i-x_{i+1}=c_i+1$ at the coordinate $c_i$: the diagonal count is
the occupied coordinates' count over the gap keys, at every shape
of the width, and at a word pair one at every key off
the words, beyond one at every member key. Hence, at every family
off the vacuum,
\[
N^{\lambda}_{\theta\lambda}
\;=\;\#\,\mathrm{supp}\,P+\#\,\mathrm{supp}\,Q\ ,
\]
the member-key count, residue-free: $2$ at every tower label
$m\theta$ (the $c_1$ of
Proposition~\ref{prop:row}), the vacuum's diagonal the defect, the
Kronecker delta of Proposition~\ref{prop:row}. Off the
diagonal a contribution raises one $x$-coordinate and lowers another by
$1$ with the entries kept distinct: at the run's clear keys
an interior raise or lower collides with its neighbor, so the
surviving moves sit within one key of the words, each target again
a word pair at each reach grown by at most one, and each count an
at-most-one read settled
by the identity alignment of Lemma~\ref{lem:channels}: a kept
move's count is one exactly, the identity's own tie at the moved
display with every further permutation's tie at the raised display
alone, at every shape of the width, and the row is a
word read, one row at every cleared residue. A
depth-$D$ fusion closure
of the vacuum therefore has as members word pairs at reaches summing
to at most $2D$,
and its whole data list, the labels, multiplicities, Casimirs, dimensions
and the network weights $w_k=[\,m_kd_k:r^2(r+2)^2\,]$, is one list of polynomial-pair reads at every
residue clearing the reach sum by two, each family with its
reach clearance there, the recoupling
entries included through
Proposition~\ref{prop:wg}'s generic computation, one polynomial pair
per entry agreeing with the per-residue read at every residue beyond the factor
count.

\emph{(iii) The residue is a cell coordinate.} At fixed content cutoff,
region and fiber, the window pencil's entries are the reads of (i)--(ii),
so they are one list of polynomial-pair data in $(t,\tau)$ and the
residue at every residue
clearing the reach sum by two; the
cutoff comparisons $\hat c_2\le\Lambda_C$ selecting the interior are
eventual-sign reads (Lemma~\ref{lem:genericlift}(iii)); and
the second members, integer shifts of the residue and its powers, keep one side there,
so clearing them is a positive rescaling fixing every count
(Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}). The fiber pencils' entries
then sit in $\mathbb P[t,\tau,r]$ and every count of the gap chain is
Lemma~\ref{lem:cellcount}'s cell function with $r$ a coordinate: a
divisor's roots in $r$ are located outright
(Theorem~\ref{thm:windowsep}, the leading-term bound the coarser
certificate), and each root reads against the compared families'
reach clearances: a comparison's residues are those clearing its
families' reaches, so at every root below them
the divisor keeps its leading side at every one of its residues
and one symbolic count is every residue's at the
cleared families; a family below its clearance reads its finitely
many residues' data directly, the calculus's displays at the
residue's label, one evaluation each; and
Lemma~\ref{lem:channels}'s $r=1,2$
degenerations are each their own window read.

\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[The series' window data are rank-stable]
\label{lem:serstable}
At a $B$, $C$ or $D$ member (Construction~\ref{con:sertables}), a
\emph{leading family} is a finite weight word
$W_1\ge\cdots\ge W_a$ at positive reads on the leading keys, its
member at the rank $\ell$ the word's own occupancy family over
those keys, and its \emph{reach clearance} the rank at or beyond
$a+2$.

\emph{(i) The Casimir and the dimension are single
polynomial-pair reads in the rank.} $q(\lambda)$ is the word's
square fold; $\langle\lambda,2\rho\rangle=\sum_iW_i\,(2\rho)_i$
folds the word against the displayed root-fold key reads, each
linear in the rank; so $\hat c_2$ is one polynomial pair per
family. The dimension is the member gap product
(Corollary~\ref{cor:weyldim}): the tail factors read one, the
word-against-tail factors telescope over the position gaps to
binomial pairs in the rank; the word-against-word difference
factors are rank-free, and the word sum, short and long factors
are single linear pairs in the rank, finitely many.

\emph{(ii) The adjoint fusion rows are word reads, one row at
every cleared rank.} The row runs at the alignment,
Corollary~\ref{cor:steinberg}'s member ties
$\nu_w+w(\lambda+\rho)=c+\rho$ over the member's Weyl list at
the $\theta$ contents (Proposition~\ref{prop:row}); the shifted
key $\lambda+\rho$ reads the word's values against the $\rho$
run, consecutive values at the displayed gaps, every entry
distinct, the junction gap the last value's successor, and a
word gap at the unit exactly at a repeated pair. An occupied
tie's element sits below the shifted key at a natural simple
fold, the image list's support read, the tie naming the fold as
the two contents' gap; the kept square reads the fold against the
key, twice the pairing at the fold's own square, with the key
strictly dominant, every coroot pair at the member's successor;
the tie collects the square against the target's own shifted key,
twice the fold's pairing there the two contents' square gap, so
the fold's length-weighted height sits at or below that gap at
the shifted target's coroot pairs, each at or beyond one by the
target's dominance, the contents' displayed lengths capping the
gap at two; a simple's raise beyond one sits off the list
outright, the raised content's square the raise's multiple of the
letter's scaled length against the doubled $\rho$-dot at the
raise once, the positive squares' cap at the doubled dots
(Proposition~\ref{prop:row}) refusing every raise beyond one
where the height cap alone admits the raise two at the length-one
keys; and the square equation with the list's membership refuses
every occupied solution of the finite residue beyond the
one-letter reads: the surviving elements are the identity and the
simple letters, each letter's fold the key's coroot multiple of
its own root and the letters' images distinct at the strict
dominance, one list member each. The letters read at the run: an
adjacent exchange at its unit gap, and the last letter the
\emph{end device}, $B$'s flip at the half value's double (the
short root's read, one), $C$'s at the long root (two), and $D$'s
last-pair exchange with both partners at the joined values (the
sum root's read, one). Each count is therefore the \emph{letter
fold}: the identity's read at the target's own content joined
against one signed read per simple letter at the raised content,
the raise the key's coroot multiple of the letter's root. The
evaluations close the row. The diagonal's odd fold reads the
letters at the key's unit coroot pairs, the vacant coroot
support, so the diagonal is the occupied support, the word's
distinct-value count (Proposition~\ref{prop:row}, the tower's
display its instance). An off-diagonal target is dominant
$\lambda+\nu$ at a content $\nu$, a leading word at reach grown
by at most two with the sum pair at the first two tail keys the
two-key growth; the coroot presentation clears the grown reach,
every further key reading its displayed pair at coordinates past
the reach, at $D$ off the last key alone, whose pair reads the two
tail coordinates' sum (Construction~\ref{con:sertables}) and so
reads the grown reach's own coordinate, occupied at the
whole-growth target at the rank one beyond the boundary and vacant
at every rank clearing the reach by four. Its count is an
at-most-one read: one at the
identity's tie alone, or the two ties evening at a named
pairing, $B$'s single-box moves against the end flip, $C$'s
tail-pair sum against the long at the exchange, and a repeated
pair's moves against their own exchange; at $D$ the difference
move at the last pair, its target's last coordinate one's
balance partner, is dominant at the boundary rank alone, the
reach clearance's least, its count one. Every fold entry is a
word read, so the row is one list at every rank clearing the
family's reach, $D$'s boundary rank joined by its last-pair
member alone, and a below-clearance rank reads its finitely many
labels directly, the member displays' own evaluations.

\emph{(iii) The rank is a cell coordinate.} At fixed content
cutoff, region and fiber, a series member's pencil entries are
the reads of (i) and (ii) with the recoupling entries
Proposition~\ref{prop:wg}'s, one list of polynomial-pair data in
$(t,\tau)$ and the rank at every rank clearing the reach sums by
two; the cutoff comparisons are eventual-sign reads and the
divisors' rank roots read against the clearances, one symbolic
count at every cleared rank with the below-clearance ranks
direct (Lemma~\ref{lem:genericlift};
Theorem~\ref{thm:windowsep}): Lemma~\ref{lem:rankstable}'s three
reads at the series' own coordinates, the $A$-series' the first
instance.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[The member pairing span]\label{lem:serpairing}
At a $B$, $C$ or $D$ member with its defining table
(Construction~\ref{con:sertables}), the invariants of the
defining power $V^{\otimes 2k}$ at the rank at or beyond $k$,
the $D$ series at or beyond $k{+}1$ (the doubled-rank
alternating invariant sits off the span at the tie
$2\ell=2k$), are
the \emph{pair-partition span}: one vector per pair partition of
the $2k$ places, the partition's fold of the form's dual pairs,
at the count $(2k{-}1)!!$.
\begin{proof}
\emph{The invariant read.} An invariant of the power is a vector
at the unit content with every raising image the sum's unit: a
unit-content top's block is the unit label's line, and a top at a
further content sits at its own label (Lemma~\ref{lem:tops};
Lemma~\ref{lem:blockcount}), so the invariants are the
unit-content summand's raising kernel, the two reads'
conjunction.
\emph{The defining rows.} $V$'s contents are the signed keys
with the unit content at $B_\ell$, and its fusion rows at a
block are one box up or one box down at the cleared ranks: the
alignment's form read (Proposition~\ref{prop:row}'s tie
analysis at the defining contents) reads the $B_\ell$ stay term
against its correction, the unit content's one against the last
letter's read at $(\lambda+\rho)(e_i^\vee)=2\lambda_i+2g+1$ over
the join $i+g=\ell$, one exactly at the last key off the
support: equal members at every label whose support clears the
last key, and the moved ties at the box targets alone; at
$C_\ell$ and $D_\ell$ the signed keys sit off the root list and
the unit content off the table, the rows the box moves
outright.
\emph{The count.} Iterating the rows, the unit-label count of
$V^{\otimes 2k}$ is the closed path fold
$\langle\mathbf 1,(U{+}D)^{2k}\,\mathbf 1\rangle$ over the
shapes at most $k$ rows deep (the clearance; a $B_\ell$ stay
reads at a full-depth shape, its boxes the step count's read,
and the return closes beyond the walk's $2k$ steps, so the
closed folds read the box moves alone), with
$DU=UD+\mathrm{id}$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:pathsquare}'s displayed
argument at the member's shapes) and the commutator telescope
$X^{m-1}U=U\,X^{m-1}+(m{-}1)\,X^{m-2}$ at $X:=U{+}D$, so the
fold obeys $m_m=(m{-}1)\,m_{m-2}$ from $m_0=1$: the double
factorial, the matchings' count, and the unit-label count is
the invariant count (Definition~\ref{def:blockcount};
Lemma~\ref{lem:blockcount} at table spaces).
\emph{Independence, and the Gram.} A pair partition's vector
reads a monomial the further partitions miss at the rank
clearing $k$, one key pair per part at distinct keys, so the
$(2k{-}1)!!$ vectors are independent and span. The Gram is the
loops' fold: two partitions' union closes into loops, each loop
contracting the form's dual pairs around its cycle; at $B_\ell$
and $D_\ell$ a loop reads the dimension, $r+3$; at $C_\ell$ a
loop reads the doubled rank at its \emph{crossing parity}, the
loop's pair count at either side with its against-order
crossings along one
traversal, the antisymmetric form's memberwise swap per
crossing: the entry is the loops' product, $(2r)$ per loop with
the parities' fold its side, one displayed traversal read per
entry.
\end{proof}
\end{lemma}

\begin{construction}[The $X$-sector is adjoint fusion]\label{con:xfusion}
$\chi_X=\chi_{\mathrm{adj}}$ at
$\chi_X+1=\chi_f\chi_{\bar f}=|\mathrm{tr}\,U|^2$
(Lemma~\ref{lem:adjchar}), so the
$X$-sector's calculus is tensoring by the adjoint, and its data are exactly
two: the fusion matrix $N_{\mathrm{adj}}$ and the weight-free Casimir
$\hat c_2$. The product expands as $\chi_{\mathrm{adj}}\chi_\lambda=
\sum_\mu N^\mu_{\mathrm{adj},\lambda}\chi_\mu$ with unique coefficients, and
the characters are orthonormal for $\mathrm{Eval}$
(Proposition~\ref{prop:repring}), so $M$ acts on the label index by
$N_{\mathrm{adj}}$ itself, symmetric because $m\{a,b,c\}=N^{\bar c}_{ab}$ is
symmetric and the adjoint is self-dual,
and $E$ is diagonal there with entries $4\hat c_2(\lambda)$. Every
anchor of the sector is a read of that pair:
$c_1=\langle\mathrm{adj}|\chi_X|\mathrm{adj}\rangle$ is the multiplicity of the
adjoint in $\mathrm{adj}\otimes\mathrm{adj}$, the $m{=}1$
alternant read of Proposition~\ref{prop:row}, $2$ at $r\ge2$ and
$1$ at $r=1$, and the member-key count of $\theta$'s word pair,
one box on each word, at the residues clearing the pair's reach
(Lemma~\ref{lem:rankstable}(ii));
$\langle q,M\,\mathrm{vac}\rangle$ is the multiplicity of the vacuum, $1$; and
$E_0=4$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:E0}).

The channel list is Lemma~\ref{lem:channels}'s, one word-pair
list at every residue with each channel entering at its count
(Lemma~\ref{lem:rankstable}), the two root-drops and the $r=1$ row list
that lemma's own reads, so
every quantity
below is a sum over the channels at their counts.

\end{construction}

\begin{theorem}[$X$-sector block data]\label{thm:xdata}
With $q_i=\chi_X(U_i)$, $E_0=4$:
$\langle q_1,Mq_1\rangle$ has the integer coefficients
$\langle q_1,M\,\mathrm{vac}\rangle=1$ and
$c_1=\langle q_1,Xq_1\rangle$, the fusion multiplicity of
Construction~\ref{con:xfusion} ($2$ at $r\ge2$, $1$ at $r=1$); the vacuum $Q$-dressing reads equal
members as a polynomial pair, $M\,\mathrm{vac}$ lying in the module space $Q$ projects off
(Construction~\ref{con:twoplaq}); the module is isolated, the channel
distances the Casimir gaps of Lemma~\ref{lem:channels}'s list.
Structural identities on the constituent data:
$\sum_R m_R\dim_R=r^2(r+2)^2$, the block count of the square
(Lemma~\ref{lem:blockcount}(ii) over Lemma~\ref{lem:channels}'s list),
and $\sum_R m_R\dim_R\hat c_2(R)=2\,r^2(r+2)^2$, cleared polynomial
identities in the residue, the
channel table's displayed closes (Lemma~\ref{lem:channels}); the second is
the uniform drift of Theorem~\ref{thm:drift} at $R=\mathrm{adj}$, where
$d_{\mathrm{adj}}=r\,(r+2)$ and $\hat c_2(\mathrm{adj})=1$ make
$d_Rd_{\mathrm{adj}}(\hat c_2(R)+1)$ read $2\,r^2(r+2)^2$.
\end{theorem}

\section{The charged anchor}

\begin{proposition}[Charged anchor for the $X$-chain]\label{prop:anchor}
Define charge as Cartan-weight density along the highest root
$\theta$.
The charged tower is the $m\theta$-string, the Cartan powers of
$\theta$ (Construction~\ref{con:labels}), and the residue is the
pairing's own read at the highest root: the row-weight fold reads
$\langle\theta,2\rho\rangle=2\,r$ against the pair-square fold at
$2\,d_f$, the declared scalar's own value, and the tower reads
\[
\hat c_2(m\theta)=\big[\,m\,(m+r):r+1\,\big]
\qquad\text{at the derived residue}\quad r ,
\]
an $l(l+1)$ in shape; at $r=1$ it is $[\,m^2+m:2\,]$, the
integer-spin tower's weight-free read. At a member's weight table
the residue is Construction~\ref{con:sertables}'s one fold,
$\langle\theta,2\rho\rangle=2\,r$ at the form's read
$\langle\theta,\theta\rangle=2$, and the tower reads the member's
form at its two degrees, the form additive in each argument
(Construction~\ref{con:gentable}),
$C_2(m\theta)=m^2\,\langle\theta,\theta\rangle
+m\,\langle\theta,2\rho\rangle=2\,m\,(m+r)$ against
$C_2(\theta)=2\,(r+1)$: the display at the member's derived
residue. The
linear coefficient $r$ is fusion strictness: the abelian tower reads
$C_2=m^2$ and this one $m\,(m+r)$.
\begin{proof}
The join's constructed witness (Definition~\ref{def:c2hat}) reads
a label at two folds,
$d_f\,Q(\lambda)=\sum_{p<q}\langle\lambda_p\!:\!\lambda_q\rangle^2
+d_f\,\langle\lambda,2\rho\rangle$, each homogeneous in the rows,
degree two and degree one
($\langle mu\!:\!mv\rangle=m\,\langle u\!:\!v\rangle$,
Definition~\ref{def:ground}'s displayed reads); the tower label's
rows are $m$ times $\theta$'s, $m\theta=(2m,\,m^{\,c})$ at
$c+2=d_f$, so the tower reads off $\theta$'s two folds,
\[
d_f\,Q(m\theta)\;=\;m^{2}\sum_{p<q}
\langle\theta_p\!:\!\theta_q\rangle^{2}
+m\,d_f\,\langle\theta,2\rho\rangle\ .
\]
At the reduced shape $\theta=(2,1^{\,c})$ the square identity
$d_f\,q(\theta)=k^{2}+\sum_{p<q}\langle\theta_p\!:\!\theta_q
\rangle^{2}$ (Definition~\ref{def:c2hat}) at $q(\theta)=r+3$ and
$k=d_f$ reads the pair squares at $2\,d_f$; and the row weights
$\langle r+2\!:\!2i\rangle$ sum to equal members over the run
$2\le i\le r$, so $\langle\theta,2\rho\rangle=2\,r$, the top row's
read, the pairing's read the residue doubled. So
$Q(m\theta)=2m^2+2mr$, and
against $Q(\theta)=2d_f$, the display's own $m=1$ read,
$\hat c_2(m\theta)=[\,m\,(m+r):r+1\,]$; at $r=1$ the value is
$[\,m^2+m:2\,]$.
\end{proof}

\end{proposition}

\section{The $X$-tower matrix and the walk}

\begin{proposition}[The $X$-tower matrix]\label{prop:row}
On the orthonormal character index the multiplication by $\chi_\theta$
is the symmetric matrix of fusion counts $N^\mu_{\theta\lambda}$
(Proposition~\ref{prop:repring}), and
its compression to the tower labels $m\theta$ is the \emph{tower
matrix} $J$, fusion counts alone. Every neighbor entry is one,
\[
N^{m\theta}_{\theta,(m+1)\theta}
=m\{\theta,(m{+}1)\theta,m\theta\}
=N^{(m+1)\theta}_{\theta,m\theta}
=1 ,
\]
upward the Cartan equality, $\theta+m\theta=(m{+}1)\theta$ as
reduced shapes (Construction~\ref{con:labels}), downward the
$m$-symmetry at the self-dual tower labels
(Proposition~\ref{prop:repring}; $(m\theta)^*=m\theta$ by the complement
arithmetic of Construction~\ref{con:labels}); and the diagonal is the
tower's own fusion multiplicity
$N^{m\theta}_{\theta,m\theta}$, one alternant read at
every $m$ (Corollary~\ref{cor:steinberg} at $a=\theta$, $b=c=m\theta$).
The vector $x:=m\theta+u$, at $u$ the unit set's sorted display, has
coordinates $2m+r+1$; the middle members $m+i$ at
$2\le i\le r$; and $1$: pairwise distinct, and a permutation
contributes only where $\sigma x=x$, the adjoint's unit-monomial content, or
where the tie $\nu+\sigma x=x$ holds at a root $\nu$, the adjoint's
occupied contents (Lemma~\ref{lem:adjchar}). At $\sigma=\mathrm{id}$ the
read is $r$, the adjoint's multiplicity at the unit-monomial
content, on the even side; a root tie
forces $\sigma$ to fix every coordinate off some $\{i,j\}$ (the
coordinates
are distinct), so $\sigma=(i\,j)$ at the two coordinates' gap one, an
odd transposition at the root's multiplicity one. At
$m\ge1$ the unit gaps sit in the run alone, $x_{k+1}+1=x_k$ at
$2\le k<r$, a run occupied at $r\ge2$, the two boundary
gaps reading $m+1\ge2$:
\[
N^{m\theta}_{\theta,m\theta}+g\;=\;r\quad(r\ge2,\ \ g\ \text{the
run's unit-gap count at}\ g+2=r),
\qquad
N^{m\theta}_{\theta,m\theta}\;=\;r\;=\;1\quad(r=1),
\]
the count $2$, one value at every $m\ge1$; at the vacuum the boundary
gaps close to $1$ and the two further odd transpositions even the
two sides' counts, the count $N^{\mathbf 1}_{\theta\,\mathbf 1}$ the
Kronecker delta at two labels (Proposition~\ref{prop:repring}): the
vacuum defect. So $J$ is the free Jacobi matrix with a single boundary
defect, neighbor entries one, the diagonal $c_1$ at every $m\ge1$ and
the vacuum's the Kronecker delta at two labels: integer data, $c_1$ the
one parameter, the shape's reads the fusion interface's own, the
Cartan strictness and the unit read at the self-dual $\theta$
(Construction~\ref{con:fusion}).
At a member's table the diagonal is the alignment's form read
(Corollary~\ref{cor:steinberg}'s member ties at $a=\theta$,
$b=c=m\theta$), the member's $\theta$ content list the root list
with its balance partners at one each and the unit content at
the coordinate count, the interface field's instantiation
(Construction~\ref{con:fusion}), derived on a block at top
$\theta$: a content's dominant image is a dominant root or the
unit (the descent, Lemma~\ref{lem:casfloor}, with the content
symmetry, Lemma~\ref{lem:strings}), the top's line reads one
(Lemma~\ref{lem:lowerspan}), the short dominant root's read is
the trace recursion's own fold there (Lemma~\ref{lem:casimir}),
one display per two-length class,
\[
(2\ell{-}2)\,m=2\,(\ell{-}1)\ \text{at}\ B_\ell,\quad
2\,m=2\ \text{at}\ C_\ell,\quad
6\,m=6\ \text{at}\ F_4,\quad
4\,m=4\ \text{at}\ G_2,
\]
each right side the fold of the form reads at the contents above
the short dominant root, and the unit content's read is the
recursion's unit fold, $m\,(r+1)$ the halved lengths' fold over
the root list, at the member join
$\sum_{\alpha}\langle\alpha,\alpha\rangle=\ell\,(r+1)$ over the
positive list, one displayed fold per member ($2\ell^2{-}\ell$
at $B_\ell$, $\ell^2{+}\ell$ at $C_\ell$, $2\ell^2{-}2\ell$ at
$D_\ell$, and $8$, $36$, $72$, $126$, $240$ at the fixed
members' length folds): the unit content at the coordinate
count. The dimension is the content list's own fold,
twice the positive count with the coordinate count,
\[
d_\theta=\big[\,(r+2)(r+3):2\,\big]\ \text{at}\ B_\ell\
\text{and}\ D_\ell,\quad r\,(2r+1)\ \text{at}\ C_\ell,\quad
r\,(r+2)\ \text{at the $A$-series},
\]
and $14$, $52$, $78$, $133$, $248$ at the fixed members, the
member gap product's evaluation at $\theta$ its cross-check
(Corollary~\ref{cor:weyldim}). A tie
$\nu_w+w\kappa=\kappa$ at $\kappa:=m\theta+\rho$ keeps the
form's square, so $2\,\langle\kappa,\nu_w\rangle$ reads
$q_0(\nu_w)$. At $\nu_w$ the unit content the element is
the identity, $\kappa$ dominant at every simple pair beyond the
unit with its images one per element (the regular reads,
Construction~\ref{con:sertables}), the read the coordinate count
on the even side. At $\nu_w$ a root read, a partner's case reads
the doubled pairing on its lower side against the square, so
$\nu_w$ is a positive $\beta$ at
$\kappa(\beta^\vee)=1$, the element the root's reflection alone,
$w\kappa=s_\beta\kappa$ at the regular reads, odd at the
transport with the fold descent reading $\beta$ a letter's
image; and
$\kappa(\beta^\vee)=m\,\theta(\beta^\vee)+\rho(\beta^\vee)$
reads one exactly at $\theta$'s coroot pair of equal members
with $\beta$ simple, the root fold's pair two at the simples and
beyond two at every further positive member (the fixed members'
displayed reads; the series' one fold per family), each such
$\beta$ one odd read at the root content's one. So the diagonal
joins the count $z$ of the simples at $\theta$'s equal-membered
coroot pairs to the
coordinate count, $N^{m\theta}_{\theta,m\theta}+z=\ell$ at
every $m\ge1$: the value is $\theta$'s occupied coroot support,
one at every member off the $A$-series, the displayed tables'
single keys, and the $A$-series' the run's read above, two at
$r\ge2$ and one at $r=1$; the
vacuum's ties read every simple at the pair one, the count the
Kronecker delta at two labels, the defect's read at every
member.
With strictly positive off-tower weight: the $X$-tower leaks, and the
family operator is the tower-compressed Jacobi pencil plus off-tower
coupling, the dimension weights riding the off-tower channels alone.
\end{proposition}

\section{The tower channel: chord map and divisor}\label{sec:channel}

\begin{theorem}[The chord map and the channel's base]\label{thm:chordmap}
Pass to the dual of the walk: the spectral side, in the deck
coordinate $z$, at the walk's level the balance pair
$y=\langle y_+\!:\!y_-\rangle$, its members cross-added onto the two
sides (Theorem~\ref{thm:certconstruct}'s levels), the \emph{deck
relation}
\[
z^2+y_+\,z+1\;=\;\big(c_1+y_-\big)\,z ,
\]
one display at every representative (a summand on both members
fixing the value, Definition~\ref{def:ground}),
degree two in $z$ with the \emph{deck involution} $\iota$ exchanging
its two roots, their product the constant coefficient one and
the level read jointly by both, Definition~\ref{def:deck}'s
relation at the chord $w$ joining the level's members,
$w+y_+=c_1+y_-$. The relation's
two roots are equal exactly at the \emph{crossings}, $\iota$'s fixed
points, a double root squaring to the constant coefficient one at
the display
\[
(c_1+y_-)^2+y_+^{\,2}\;=\;2\,y_+\,(c_1+y_-)+4\ ,
\]
the double root's read cross-added onto the two sides, one display
at every representative (Definition~\ref{def:ground}); the chord
is a member of the carrier at the lower crossing alone, and the
display holds
exactly at the
two crossing joins $y_++2=c_1+y_-$ and $y_+=c_1+y_-+2$; the \emph{band}
is the segment between the two crossing levels;
and the defect's root is $z_*$ at $c_1z_*=1$, where the relation
reads the bound-state level at the join $c_1y_++1=c_1y_-$.

\emph{The chord and the defect read distinct integers.} The
relation's two roots are the half-line walk's neighbors, the
coordination count that
fixes the band's width $4$, the level read jointly by the two
roots. The base of the
channel's value
is $c_1$ at $c_1z_*=1$, the mass point's own witness. The two integers
agree exactly at base two, the member-key count's two-key read
(Proposition~\ref{prop:row}), and separate at base one, where the
chord has two terms.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[The channel divisor]\label{thm:channeldiv}
The walk's boundary entry $G_0$ in the deck coordinate is the
renewal witness $G_0=z+c_1zG_0$'s series, its divisor the witness's
own at the mass point $z_*$ at $c_1z_*=1$, and the
channel's coordinate is the order pair, the determination: the mass
point a simple pole, order $1$, the $\iota$-symmetrized weight at
order $2$, both orders independent of the base and summing to $3$;
the base $c_1$ is the orders' read, the single-key member's display
at base one, the mass point at the crossing.

\emph{The divisor.} The walk's resolvent reads in the deck
coordinate, the level the balance pair
$y=\langle y_+\!:\!y_-\rangle$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:chordmap}. The
defect-free tail's boundary entry $m^{(L)}$ at the
tail's $L$-site cut $J_L$ is a solved witness of
Lemma~\ref{lem:genericlift}(ii)'s shape, the boundary entry of the
adjugate column against the determinant at the pair
$(J_L+y_-\!:\!y_+)$'s site datum (Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia};
Theorem~\ref{thm:certconstruct}'s levels, the datum at
$J_L+y_-=y_++S_L$): the boundary
couples to the tail by the single entry one, the unit-triangular
congruence of Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia} splits the pair
$(J_{L+1}+y_-\!:\!y_+)$'s datum as
the boundary pivot against $(J_L+y_-\!:\!y_+)$'s, and the boundary entry
solves the pivot identity,
\[
m^{(L+1)}\,\big(c_1+y_-\big)\;=\;1+m^{(L+1)}\big(y_++m^{(L)}\big) ,
\qquad
m^{(1)}\,\big(c_1+y_-\big)\;=\;1+m^{(1)}\,y_+ ,
\]
the one-site read its seed. The truncations generate one series
$m_1$, whose fixed-point identity is the deck
relation in the boundary entry,
$m_1^2+y_+\,m_1+1=\big(c_1+y_-\big)\,m_1$: its
two roots are the root pair, at product one, and the member at the
$z$-multiple series is the coordinate itself, the errors $e_L$ at
$m^{(L)}=z+e_L$ obey
$e_{L+1}=z^2e_L+z\,e_L\,e_{L+1}$, so $m^{(L)}$ agrees with $z$
beyond $z$-order $2L$, and $m_1(z)=z$. The defect site then reads one
further pivot, its
diagonal the vacuum's (Proposition~\ref{prop:row}), and the boundary
read solves it at the renewal witness
\[
G_0\;=\;z+c_1\,z\,G_0 ,
\]
the recursion's own series $\sum_kc_1^{\,k}z^{\,k+1}$
(Definition~\ref{def:poly}), coefficients naturals: its divisor is
the witness's own, the least occupied degree one and the
\emph{mass point} $z_{*}$ at $c_1z_*=1$, the clearing factor
$\langle1\!:\!c_1z\rangle$'s root. The deck
involution exchanges $z_*$ with its $\iota$-image $c_1$, the orbit's product
one: $z_*$ is the orbit's interior member, $z_*^2\le1$ one pair
comparison. The mass point's margin is the crossing read: the
relation at the mass point reads the symbol
$c_1\,w=1+c_1^{\,2}$, its crossing pair
$\langle w^2\!:\!4\rangle=[\,\langle
c_1^{\,2}\!:\!1\rangle^2:c_1^{\,2}\,]$, equal members exactly at
$c_1=1$, and the margin $\langle1\!:\!z_*^2\rangle$ is the mass
weight's own pair, its square the crossing pair's
$z_*^2$-multiple (Lemma~\ref{lem:deckfactor}): $[\,9:4\,]$ at
base two, the member-key count's two-key read
(Proposition~\ref{prop:row}).
Every channel datum is a read of it: the bound-state level
at the join $c_1y_++1=c_1y_-$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:chordmap}); the
mass weight, the pair $[\,\langle c_1^2:1\rangle:c_1^2\,]$ at the
complement $[\,1:c_1^2\,]$, the two summing to one; and the crossing
levels at their joins (Theorem~\ref{thm:chordmap}), with $c_1$
Proposition~\ref{prop:row}'s. The lower crossing's level reads equal
members exactly at
$c_1=2$, the infrared contact forced by the integer counting the cubic
couplings, and at $c_1=1$ the mass point sits at the crossing, $z_*=1$, the
divisor count unchanged there, as below.

\emph{What the channel contributes, and at which base.} The divisor's
data are \emph{two integer orders} and one base. The mass point is a simple
pole of $G_0$, order $1$. The weight reads the clearing factor
$\iota$-symmetrized, the $\iota$-image's cleared read and the symmetrized
pair
\[
z\cdot\big(\langle1\!:\!c_1z\rangle\circ\iota\big)
\;=\;\langle z\!:\!c_1\rangle ,
\qquad
\big[\,\langle1\!:\!c_1z\rangle\,\langle z\!:\!c_1\rangle:z\,\big] ,
\]
whose two $\iota$-image factors each read the base
once, $\langle1\!:\!c_1z\rangle$ at its leading coefficient and
$\langle z\!:\!c_1\rangle$ at its root
$c_1$, so the weight's read at the base is order $2$. Both orders are
independent of $c_1$, and the base collects at the orders' sum,
\[
c_1^{\,1}\cdot c_1^{\,2}\;=\;c_1^{\,3} .
\]
So the channel's coordinate is the integer $3=1+2$ and its base is $c_1$:
the two orders are what the theory determines, the base what they are read
at. The read is one divisor count at every position of the
$\iota$-image root, and $c_1=1$ places that root at the crossing,
the one count throughout.
\end{theorem}

\begin{definition}[The channel reads]\label{def:channelreads}
At a base $c_1$ the channel's derived objects
(Theorem~\ref{thm:channeldiv}) are ground reads
(Definition~\ref{def:poly}): the boundary entry $G_0$
at its renewal witness $G_0=z+c_1zG_0$, as the witness's two
coefficient lists; the mass point $z_*$ at $c_1z_*=1$;
the \emph{pole order} of $G_0$ at $z_*$, the gap of the clearing
factor's root multiplicity there against the series member's; the
$\iota$-symmetrized
weight's factor list
$\big(\langle1\!:\!c_1z\rangle,\ \langle z\!:\!c_1\rangle\big)$
over the second
member $z$,
each factor reading the base at degree one; and the \emph{order
count}, the pole order added to the factor list's count.
\end{definition}

\section{The cell certificate}

\begin{theorem}[Gap positivity on the interior, assembled]\label{thm:gappos}
At every interior end-weight ray of $\mathcal C$ the cut of
$\mathcal K\big([\,\alpha:\beta\,]\big)$ holds a member pair,
positive by its shape: the interior is a union of
located cells, the root coordinate's rays $q\,\tau=p$ read their
cells directly, the counting sandwich of (v) hands every further ray
its cut at the sandwich's stated width, and on each cell the cut is
finitely many counts at a positive level of the cell's own.

\emph{(i) The count has an object.} On a window the compression is
finite-dimensional (Proposition~\ref{prop:windowfinite}), so ``the number of
eigenvalues below a line'' denotes something and
$\mathrm{count}(a)=\operatorname{rev}(H\!:\!aG)$ is an exact reversal count.

\emph{(ii) The interior is covered by located cells.} At any interior
coupling and window, Theorem~\ref{thm:truncation}'s modulus puts the
cut's verification on a finite compression at a computable cutoff, at
every coupling, and the decimation presents that compression's counts
per cell of the named divisor list (Theorem~\ref{thm:decimation};
Lemma~\ref{lem:relfiber}; Lemma~\ref{lem:chargedcell}): the cells over
the coupling, the momenta and the residue
(Lemma~\ref{lem:cellcount}; Lemma~\ref{lem:rankstable}(iii);
Lemma~\ref{lem:serstable}(iii)), read at
each dimension through stencil caps and momentum sums polynomial in it,
cover the interior, the \emph{free cell} at the free end
(Lemma~\ref{lem:freeend}; Lemma~\ref{lem:freecell}), the \emph{emitted
cells} of the decimation's divisor list between, and the \emph{contact cell}
at the other end (Lemma~\ref{lem:contactcell}; the approach the
corner cell's, Lemma~\ref{lem:corner}), every boundary a
located root, counts persisting at a boundary beside a
positive-semidefinite cell.
