\emph{(iii) Per cell the cut is a flat step, and the cutoff is priced.}
On each cell the flat step verifies the cut at a level $\kappa$,
finitely many anchored jump comparisons, each two counts at located
pairs (Theorem~\ref{thm:flatstep}), and
Theorem~\ref{thm:truncation} prices the cutoff at the $\alpha{=}1$
representative: at an admissible cutoff's order
$E_{\mathrm{low}}<\Lambda_C$ with its witness gap $\varsigma$ at
$E_{\mathrm{low}}+\varsigma=\Lambda_C$,
\[
E_0\,\varsigma\,\mathcal K_\Lambda
\;\le\;E_0\,\varsigma\,\mathcal K(v)+\beta^2\#p^2\,d_\theta^{\,2} ,
\]
at every cutoff: given a pair $\vartheta$, taking
$\vartheta\,\varsigma>\beta^2\#p^2\,d_\theta^{\,2}$ transports the
window cuts to $\mathcal K(v)$ with a modulus rather than along a
subsequence, at a rate the member's adjoint dimension and the
region's plaquette count fix.

\emph{(iv) The level is positive per cell, and the whole read closes
on finitely many cells.} The cell's level is a divisor read: root
collisions sit on the crossing divisor alone
(Theorem~\ref{thm:divisorid}(ii)), so on a cell the decimated head's
first two jumps stay separated, the ground jump at its per-cell
height, read $1$ from the free end's side
(Theorem~\ref{thm:SO}; Theorem~\ref{thm:decimation}(i)), and
window-free by the decimation, off the unit fiber outright and within the
named geometric brackets on it (Theorem~\ref{thm:decimation}(iii)); the
counting functions are flat across $(\ell,\ \ell+E_0\kappa)$ at fixed
pairs of the cell's certificate, each count a cell function read
once (Lemma~\ref{lem:cellcount}; Theorem~\ref{thm:flatstep}), so a
pair $\kappa$ within that flat window less the brackets' width
is a located datum of the cell, a content cell's the
$H([\,1:\tau^2\,])$ level distance, the free and contact cells
reading theirs outright (Lemma~\ref{lem:freeend};
Lemma~\ref{lem:contactcell}; the contact
approach at the corner scale's heights, Lemma~\ref{lem:corner}) and each
interior boundary one located two-sided read of the divisor, and
\[
E_0\,\mathcal K(v)\;\ge\;\kappa
\]
on the whole cell, located point by located point, each cell read at
one pair (Lemma~\ref{lem:cellcount}). At the reach clearances of
the families each count reads, one
symbolic verification is every residue's, the rank divisors at
their leading sides at every one of their residues off the
classifications' displayed merged roots, those residues reading
their tied pairs at the multiplicity, with a
below-clearance family's residues read directly
(Lemma~\ref{lem:rankstable}(iii); Lemma~\ref{lem:serstable}(iii);
the classifications of Lemma~\ref{lem:chaininstances} and
Lemma~\ref{lem:serdivisors}), and the
channel-degeneration residues are each their own read. The
quantifier over the dimension closes at the derivations: the fibering,
the decimation, the charged grading and the flat step are derived at every
$d$, with the stencil caps and the momentum sums polynomial in it, and
the head's jump reads are counts at every window there
(Theorem~\ref{thm:SO};
Theorem~\ref{thm:decimation}(i)), so each cell's $\kappa$ is a positive
located datum at every dimension, the divisor's own read; a
dimension's cell list is one
instance of those derivations, as is a channel-degeneration
residue's, residues one and two at their own channel data
(Lemma~\ref{lem:channels}).

\emph{(v) The reads close across each cell, at the sandwich's
width.} The cut's
comparisons are counting-function reads, and counts obey a coupling
sandwich: at couplings $v,v'$ of a cell whose pencils' site datum
$S$ at $H(v)=H(v')+S$ is
capped two-sidedly at $\rho\,G$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}) per decimated
head, a cap the
decimated entries' coefficient folds price over the cell, window-free
off the unit fiber and within the deck brackets on it
(Theorem~\ref{thm:decimation}(iii); Lemma~\ref{lem:cellcount};
Theorem~\ref{thm:windowsep}'s arithmetic, against $G$'s stored
factorization), every count obeys
\[
\mathrm{count}_v\langle a\!:\!\rho\rangle\ \le\ \mathrm{count}_{v'}(a)\ \le\
\mathrm{count}_v(a+\rho)
\]
(Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}, counts monotone under $\preceq$), so every
jump bracket travels between the two couplings at width $\rho$: at
$\kappa$ below a cell's level, the level's margin names a pair
distance whose sandwich width it absorbs, the pair reads hold
across the cells' closed union by (ii)--(iv) with
Lemma~\ref{lem:cellcount}'s boundary clause, and the sandwich
transports the jump comparisons over (Theorem~\ref{thm:flatstep}): so
$\mathcal K\big([\,\alpha:\beta\,]\big)\ge\kappa^\ast$ at every
interior end-weight pair, at one positive pair $\kappa^\ast$ below
the finitely many cells' located floors, the trichotomy fold over
the cell list: the root coordinate's
rays read their cells directly, the corner cell's rays at its own
constancy (Lemma~\ref{lem:corner}), every further pair ray is a located
coupling between them whose margin absorbs the sandwich's width.

\end{theorem}

\begin{lemma}[The rank divisors]
\label{lem:chaininstances}
The rank divisors of the chain's
comparisons (Lemma~\ref{lem:rankstable}(iii)) close on $37$
records over the depth-two charge-one closure, the divisors read
at $n:=d_f$, the fundamental count
(Construction~\ref{con:places}): per band family the
\emph{excess gap}, the cleared read at
$2n\,C_2(\omega_1)+\mathrm{gap}_\lambda=2n\,C_2(\lambda)$ of
Lemma~\ref{lem:rankstable}(i)'s word data, each a product of
naturals at a gap witness,
\[
\begin{array}{llll}
\text{family} & \mathrm{gap}_\lambda & \text{witness} & \text{clearance}\\
\hline
\omega_2+\omega_1^* & 2n\,g & 1+g=n & 6\\
\omega_3+2\omega_1^* & 4n\,g & 1+g=n & 7\\
\omega_3+\omega_2^* & 4n\,g & 2+g=n & 8\\
2\omega_1+\omega_1^* & 2n\,(n+1) & & 5\\
\omega_1{+}\omega_2+2\omega_1^* & 2n\,(2n+1) & & 6\\
\omega_1{+}\omega_2+\omega_2^* & 2n\,g & 1+g=2n & 7\\
3\omega_1+2\omega_1^* & 4n\,(n+2) & & 5\\
3\omega_1+\omega_2^* & 4n\,(n+1) & & 6
\end{array}
\]
the frontier-binding comparisons, the balance pairs
$\langle\mathrm{gap}_i\!:\!\mathrm{gap}_j\rangle$ over the family
pairs, and the stencil cap $3\,g$ at $1+g=n^2$, the clearance
column the family's reach clearance read at $n$, the clearance
residue's successor
(Lemma~\ref{lem:rankstable}(ii)). A comparison's ranks are those
clearing its families' reaches, and the \emph{classification} is
the equal-members
reads in the rank domain, five, every one below a compared
family's clearance, one cross-multiplied evaluation each:
$\omega_3{+}\omega_2^*$'s gap at $n=2$ against its clearance $8$,
and the binding pairs'
$(2\omega_1{+}\omega_1^*,\ \omega_1{+}\omega_2{+}\omega_2^*)$ at
$n=2$ against $7$,
$(\omega_2{+}\omega_1^*,\ \omega_3{+}\omega_2^*)$ at $n=3$ against
$8$,
$(\omega_3{+}2\omega_1^*,\ 2\omega_1{+}\omega_1^*)$ at $n=3$
against $7$, and
$(\omega_3{+}\omega_2^*,\ 2\omega_1{+}\omega_1^*)$ at $n=5$
against $8$. So every divisor keeps its
leading side at every one of its ranks, every cell's count one
read along the rank direction, and one symbolic verification is
every rank's at the
cleared families; a family below its clearance reads its finitely
many ranks' data directly, the calculus's displays at the rank's
label (Lemma~\ref{lem:casimir};
Corollary~\ref{cor:weyldim}; Corollary~\ref{cor:steinberg}), one
evaluation each, a tied pair reading its merged level with the
counts at the multiplicity (Lemma~\ref{lem:cellcount}'s boundary
clause).

\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[The series' rank divisors]
\label{lem:serdivisors}
The rank divisors of the chain's comparisons at a $B$, $C$ or $D$
member (Lemma~\ref{lem:serstable}(iii)) close on the depth-two
sector closures at the member's class group
(Lemma~\ref{lem:chargedcell}(i)): $47$ records at $B_\ell$ and at
$C_\ell$ and $83$ at $D_\ell$, the divisors read at the derived
residue $r$ (Construction~\ref{con:sertables}). The sectors are
the unit class's, based at $\theta$, and one per nonunit class,
based at the class's end member
(Lemma~\ref{lem:chargedcell}(ii)): the spinor at $B_\ell$, the
first fundamental at $C_\ell$, and the vector with the two
spinors at $D_\ell$; the exchange of the last two simple letters
is a relabeling of the $D$ table, the last coordinate read to its
balance partner, fixing the form and the root fold (the last
key's read equal members) and exchanging the two spinor classes,
each sector's base with its closure, so the spinor pair's records
are one displayed list.

\emph{The families.} A sector's families are the base with an
\emph{added word}, the fusion row's dominant sums at the member's
$\theta$ content list
(Lemma~\ref{lem:serstable}(ii); Proposition~\ref{prop:row}),
iterated twice from a charged sector's base and once from
$\theta$ at the unit sector's, the vacuum's own depth-two
closure; each
count is the alignment's finite signed fold, rank-free, and the
displayed lists are the occupied folds. Two collision reads close
the further sums, Lemma~\ref{lem:serstable}(ii)'s at the moved
keys. At $B_\ell$, a short-move tie at a target leaving the last
key open pairs under the last coordinate's flip with its mixed
move at the last key, $\nu$ against $\nu$ joined to
$e_\ell$'s balance partner, one odd letter fixing the further
magnitudes, the two contents at one count each: the fold reads
the sum's unit, and the unit sector's short-move sums $(2,1)$
and $(1)$ read it at $\ell\ge3$ with $(1,1,1)$ at $\ell\ge4$,
the third's move at key three. At $C_\ell$, a mixed-move tie whose target's
shifted list reads the unit gap at the moved adjacent pair pairs
under that pair's transposition with the long move at its second
key, one odd letter: $\theta+e_2+e_3$ and $\omega_1+e_2+e_3$ read
the unit fold, the word $(1,1,1)$ entering at the second fusion
through the difference move at $(2,1)$, and the second fusion's
two further ties close under the moved pair's transposition
against the long move: $(2,1)+e_3+e_4$
reads the unit fold with its target off the sector's families
outright, and $(3)+e_2+e_3$ reads it with the word $(3,1,1)$
entering through the two-row source at $(2,1)+e_1+e_3$. A displayed family's ties
off the identity read off the content list, the two collision
conditions failing at its shifted list (the moved pair's gap
beyond one at the word's keys and the last key's read off the
flip's, Lemma~\ref{lem:serstable}(ii)'s alignment fold, rank-free
beyond the clearance), so its row's count is one, the
identity's own read, at every rank clearing the family's reach,
the added word's last key at Lemma~\ref{lem:serstable}'s
clearance.

\emph{The excess gaps.} The gap is the form's bilinear read at
the member's displayed data (Construction~\ref{con:gentable}'s
additive form; Construction~\ref{con:sertables}),
\[
C_2(\beta)+\mathrm{gap}_W\;=\;C_2(\beta+W)\ ,
\qquad
\mathrm{gap}_W\;=\;\langle W,W\rangle+2\,\langle W,\beta\rangle
+\langle W,2\rho\rangle\ ,
\]
$\beta$ the sector's base and $W$ the added word, three folds
over the word's occupied keys: the square fold and the doubled
base fold are rank-free, the word's own form square and its
doubled dot against the base's list, and the root fold's read is
the word's fold against the displayed key reads, linear in the
rank, so every gap is one linear read at the derived residue,
displayed as a natural fold or a doubled gap at its witness,
positive at every domain rank: every charged family sits strictly
above its base. The unit tables, the gaps against
$C_2(\theta)=2\,(r+1)$ with the $2\theta$ rows the tower law's
$m=2$ read (Proposition~\ref{prop:anchor}):
\[
\begin{array}{llll}
\text{family} & \mathrm{gap} & \text{witness} & \text{clearance}\\
\hline
B_\ell,\ D_\ell:\quad(2) & 4 & & 3\\
(1,1,1,1) & 2\,g & g+3=r & 6\\
(2,1,1) & 2\,(r+1) & & 5\\
(2,2)=2\theta & 2\,(r+3) & & 4\\[2pt]
C_\ell:\quad(1,1) & \langle 2r\!:\!2r+2\rangle & \text{margin
$2$, lower side} & 4\\
(2,2) & 2\,r & & 4\\
(3,1) & 2\,(r+1) & & 4\\
(4)=2\theta & 2\,(r+3) & & 3
\end{array}
\]
The charged tables, the gaps against the base's Casimir, the
spinor list one table for $B_\ell$ and $D_\ell$ with a word of
odd box count at $D_\ell$ read at the flipped base, the class
kept:
\[
\begin{array}{llll}
\text{spinor word} & \mathrm{gap} & & \text{clearance}\\
\hline
e_1 & r+3 & & 3\\
e_1{+}e_2 & 2\,(r+2) & & 4\\
e_1{+}e_2{+}e_3 & 3\,(r+1) & & 5\\
e_1{+}e_2{+}e_3{+}e_4 & 4\,r & & 6\\
2e_1 & 2\,(r+4) & & 3\\
2e_1{+}e_2 & 3\,(r+3) & & 4\\
2e_1{+}e_2{+}e_3 & 4\,(r+2) & & 5\\
2e_1{+}2e_2 & 4\,(r+3) & & 4
\end{array}
\]
\[
\begin{array}{llll|llll}
C_\ell,\ \omega_1 & \mathrm{gap} & \text{wit.} & \text{cl.} &
D_\ell,\ \text{vector} & \mathrm{gap} & \text{wit.} & \text{cl.}\\
\hline
(1,1,1) & 2\,g & g{+}1{=}r & 5 & (1,1,1) & 2\,g & g{+}1{=}r & 5\\
(2,1) & 2r+1 & & 4 & (2,1) & 2\,(r+2) & & 4\\
(3) & 2\,(r+2) & & 3 & (3) & 2\,(r+5) & & 3\\
(2,2,1) & 4\,r & & 5 & (1,1,1,1,1) & 4\,g & g{+}3{=}r & 7\\
(3,1,1) & 2\,(2r+1) & & 5 & (2,1,1,1) & 2\,g & g{+}1{=}2r & 6\\
(3,2) & 4\,(r+1) & & 4 & (2,2,1) & 4\,(r+1) & & 5\\
(4,1) & 4r+7 & & 4 & (3,1,1) & 4\,(r+2) & & 5\\
(5) & 4\,(r+3) & & 3 & (3,2) & 4\,(r+3) & & 4
\end{array}
\]

\emph{The records.} Per sector, the family gaps and the
\emph{frontier-binding comparisons}, the balance pairs
$\langle\mathrm{gap}_i\!:\!\mathrm{gap}_j\rangle$ over the
sector's family pairs, six per unit list and twenty-eight per
charged sector; and per member the stencil cap $3\,d_\theta$ at
the member's dimension fold (Proposition~\ref{prop:row}),
$[\,3\,(r+2)(r+3):2\,]$ at $B_\ell$ and $D_\ell$ and
$3\,r\,(2r+1)$ at $C_\ell$: the joins $10+36+1$ at $B_\ell$ and
$C_\ell$ and $10+2\cdot36+1$ at $D_\ell$ are the stated counts.

\emph{The classification} is the equal-members reads of the
displayed divisors in the rank domains, the domains the residue
folds' reads (Construction~\ref{con:sertables}), every even
residue from two at $B_\ell$, every residue from three at
$C_\ell$ and every odd residue from five at $D_\ell$: seven,
each one cross-multiplied evaluation. Five sit below a compared
family's clearance, the rank's data read directly: at $B_2$ the
spinor word $e_1{+}e_2$ reads $8$ with the four-key word beyond
the rank's keys, and at $B_3$ the word $2e_1$ reads $16$ so; at
$D_4$ the unit sector's $(2)$ and the key-four pair, the word
$(1,1,1,1)$ with its flip image, read one merged level at the
gap $4$, and the spinor words $e_1{+}e_2{+}e_3$ and $2e_1$ read
one merged level at $18$; and at $D_4$ the vector's $(1,1,1)$
reads $8$ with the five-key word beyond the rank's keys. Two sit
at cleared ranks, the merged levels: the spinor words
$e_1{+}e_2{+}e_3{+}e_4$ and $2e_1{+}e_2$ at $D_6$, the gap $36$,
and the vector's $(1,1,1,1,1)$ and $(3)$ at $D_7$, the gap
$32$, each root a located rank point whose tied pair reads its
merged level with the counts at the multiplicity
(Lemma~\ref{lem:cellcount}'s boundary clause). So every
displayed divisor keeps its leading side at every further
cleared rank, one symbolic read every such rank's, a
comparison's ranks those clearing its families' reaches; and a
family below its clearance reads its finitely many ranks' data
directly, the calculus's displays at the rank's label
(Lemma~\ref{lem:casimir}; Corollary~\ref{cor:weyldim};
Corollary~\ref{cor:steinberg}), the short-move sums joining
those ranks' unit closures at their own displayed folds, $(2,1)$
at $6$ with $(1)$ at $\langle4\!:\!6\rangle$, margin $2$ on its
lower side, at $B_2$ (the occupied last key at the first, the
last key's own move at the second) and $(1,1,1)$ at $2$ at
$B_3$, with the last key's flip moves at $D_4$.
\end{lemma}

%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%
\section{The count certificates}

The statements below are the cell calculus's arithmetic, made finite
and constructible: the counts, the located roots, and the window reads
the decimation and the flat step take.

\begin{lemma}[The reversal count]\label{lem:inertia}
Over a located stage (Lemma~\ref{lem:stage}), where every entry's
members' equality and side are stage reads, the \emph{reversal count} of a
form pair, written $\operatorname{rev}(A\!:\!B)$ at symmetric $A$,
$B$ of order $N$, is the side count of the pair: the dimension of
a maximal list on whose span $B$ exceeds $A$ at every combination off
the sum's unit, the two lists of the proof its witnesses. The count is
read off a \emph{pivot split} of the
pair's elimination datum $S$, the site structure at
$A=B+S$ read entrywise (Definition~\ref{def:elim}): a \emph{unit
congruence}, $T$ at its witness $T'$ at $TT'=1$ and $T'T=1$, the
two reads the statement's data,
$T^{\mathsf T}ST=D$ with $D$ block diagonal in nonsingular blocks of
orders $1$ and $2$ and one \emph{kernel block}, its columns' $T$-image
spanning the datum's kernel; a nonsingular block reads its
\emph{sides}, one per unit: an order-$1$ block its entry's side, and
a symmetric $2\times2$ block two side reads off its data (the
determinant on its lower side reads one unit per side, the
\emph{mixed} block; on its upper side the identity
$S_{11}S_{22}=\det+S_{12}^{2}$ puts the
diagonal product on its upper side, both entries of unequal members at
one shared side, which both units read), so a split reads a
lower-side count,
the order-$1$ blocks' sides with the $2\times2$ reads. Every
symmetric $S$ has a split, closed in at most $N$ pivot steps, and
every two splits read one lower-side count:
$\operatorname{rev}(A\!:\!B)$ is that shared count, the split's
pivot ordering certificate data, and $\operatorname{rev}S$
abbreviates the count at $S$ its own elimination datum. A split
enters cleared: over pair data the congruence reads at an integer
$T$, square at the datum's order, its determinant of unequal
members with the adjugate against the determinant the witness's
solve (Definition~\ref{def:elim}), and the block diagonal absorbs
the columns' rescaling at every side fixed, the cleared read the
unit congruence's by the homogeneity principle
(Definition~\ref{def:ground}), one count at either spelling. The split
reads the \emph{rank}
with it, the kernel block's complement, one count at every split and
blind to unit congruence with the reversal count: a split
$(T,D)$ of $S$ is the split $(T,\check D)$ of the memberwise swap
$\check S$ (each entry's member swap) at the block
sides exchanged, so the rank is the two counts' sum,
$\operatorname{rev}(S)+\operatorname{rev}(\check S)$. A datum is
\emph{positive semidefinite} where a split's nonsingular blocks read
the upper side throughout, the split the statement's witness, and
\emph{positive definite} where those blocks' orders sum to the
datum's; a form pair $(A\!:\!B)$ is positive semidefinite where
its elimination datum is, the glyphs $B\preceq A$
and $A\succeq B$ denote that read, and $S\succ0$ denotes $S$
positive definite; a datum $S$ is \emph{capped
two-sidedly} at $C$ where $S\preceq C$ holds and the sum $C+S$ is
positive semidefinite, two splits as the statement's witnesses.
Consequently:
$\operatorname{rev}(T^{\mathsf T}ST)=\operatorname{rev}(S)$
at $T$ with its witness;
$\operatorname{rev}(qS)=\operatorname{rev}(S)$ at a pair $q$ above
the sum's unit, a
split $(T,D)$ of $S$ being the split $(T,qD)$ of $qS$ with every
block's sides and determinant side fixed; a symmetric $\mathrm{LDL}^{\mathsf T}$ reads
$\operatorname{rev}$ as its count of lower-side pivots, and when
the leading principal minors read unequal members the pivots are
their successive cofactors ($m_j\,d_{j+1}=m_{j+1}$), so $\operatorname{rev}$ is the minor
sequence's own orientation-reversal count, the reversals along
$1,m_1,\dots,m_N$ (Jacobi's rule), the name's read;
$S\preceq S'$ gives
$\operatorname{rev}(S)\ge\operatorname{rev}(S')$; a
compression's counts are at most the full form's; inertia adds at a
nonsingular principal pivot,
\[
\operatorname{rev}(S)=\operatorname{rev}(P)
+\operatorname{rev}(S_P)
\quad\text{at}\quad
S=\Big[\begin{smallmatrix}P&B\\B^{\mathsf T}&Q\end{smallmatrix}\Big],
\qquad
B^{\mathsf T}C_P+S_P=Q\ \ \text{at the solve}\ \ P\,C_P=B ,
\]
$S_P$ the pivot's \emph{deflation}, its witness $C_P$ the
adjugate column against the determinant, a pivot at stated places
entering at the leading position by the congruence at the places'
permutation matrix, its transpose the witness
(Construction~\ref{con:places}'s transpose identity at the matrix
carrier) with the count the congruence read's own, and the determinant
splits with the inertia at the cleared scale: each trailing row
enters at the pivot's determinant against the leading rows'
combination at the solve's witness column, one scale per trailing
row, the minor's scale and combination reads
(Definition~\ref{def:elim}) reading the scale's power at the
deflation's order against the reduced list's determinant, the
coupling's exchange withdrawn at the solve so the reduced list is
block triangular with its trailing block the deflation at one
further scale per row, and a block-triangular list's determinant is
its diagonal blocks' product, every assignment crossing into the
vacant slab reading an entry at the sum's unit:
$\det P\cdot\det\big((\det P)^2S_P\big)=(\det P)^{2o}\det S$
at $o$ the deflation's order, $\det S=\det P\cdot\det S_P$ its
read at the off-unit scale's injectivity;
two square lists' \emph{product}, its entries the first's rows
against the second's columns at the coordinate fold, reads its
determinant at the factors' own, one joined list's two reads: at
the join of the identity's memberwise swap beside the second list
over the first list beside a vacant block, the trailing slab
clears against the leading rows at the first list's own
coefficients, one combination per row with the further summands
repeated-row reads of equal members
(Definition~\ref{def:elim}), onto the vacant slab beside the two
lists' product, and the cleared join's determinant is the
block-triangular read at the swapped identity against the
product's; the slabs' exchange, one transposition per row pair
(Definition~\ref{def:elim}), reads the joined list block
triangular at the factors instead, and the exchange count joins the swapped
identity's diagonal to an even family
(Construction~\ref{con:places}): the signs withdraw and the
product's determinant is the determinants' product;
inertia adds at a block diagonal outright,
\[
\operatorname{rev}(P\oplus Q)\;=\;
\operatorname{rev}(P)+\operatorname{rev}(Q) ,
\]
the addition's instance at a coupling of equal members: the two
splits concatenate to a split of the block diagonal, the
congruences joined at that off-block with the block lists appended,
the kernel columns collected to the tail and the kernel orders
added;
and a symmetric matrix of unequal-membered determinant has a
nonsingular principal submatrix of order at most two.
\begin{proof}
\emph{Existence.} At a nonsingular principal block $P$ of order $1$
or $2$, the unit-triangular congruence at upper block $\check C_P$,
the witness's memberwise swap ($P\,C_P=B$),
maps $S$ to $P\oplus S_P$, and the recursion descends to the
deflation. With every principal minor of orders $1$ and $2$
of equal members, the diagonal reads equal members entrywise, each
order-two minor then reads $S_{ij}^{2}$, and $S_{ij}$ with it, of
equal members: a symmetric
block either has such a pivot or is the kernel block, so the
leftmost admissible pivot closes a split in at most $N$ steps, the
per-step congruences composing to $T$; in particular a symmetric
matrix of unequal-membered determinant has a nonsingular principal
submatrix of order at most $2$.

\emph{The mixed blocks' two witnesses.} At
$P=\big[\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\b&c\end{smallmatrix}\big]$ with the
determinant $\langle ac\!:\!b^2\rangle$ on its lower side, a
lower-side vector is read off the entries:
$e_1$ at $a$ on its lower side; $(b,\,\check a)$ at $a$ on its upper
side, $\check a$ the member swap,
reading $a\,\langle ac\!:\!b^2\rangle$ on the lower; and at $a$ of
equal members, where $b$'s members differ, the vector
$(w,\,b+b)$ at $w$ the balance partner of $c+1$, its read the
balance partner of $(b+b)^2$, the cross terms collecting at the
constant's swap, on the lower side at $b$'s occupied square. An upper-side
vector of $P$ is a lower-side vector of the memberwise swap
$\check P$, the recipe read there.

\emph{The two lists of a split, and the exchange read.} From a split
$(T,D)$ at lower-side count $\nu$, the \emph{lower list} takes, per
lower-side unit, the $T$-image of one block-supported vector: each
lower-side order-$1$ column, both columns of each $2\times2$ at the
shared lower side, and each mixed block's lower-side vector. The list's
$\nu$ members are independent, the strict read's own consequence
(the forcing clause's read below), and $S$ reads the lower side at every
combination off the sum's unit:
distinct blocks' pairings read equal members through $D$, so a combination reads a
per-block sum, every term on its lower side or of equal members and
the terms at coefficients of unequal members on the lower side. The \emph{complement list} takes the
upper-side order-$1$ columns, both columns of each $2\times2$ at the
shared upper side, each mixed block's upper-side vector, and the kernel
block's columns: its member count $\mu$ at $\nu+\mu=N$, the block
orders' total, independent, with every $S$-read of a combination
on its upper side or of
equal members. For two
splits at counts $\nu_1$, $\nu_2$, join the first's lower list to
the second's complement list: the joint rank is one computation, and
a rank defect exhibits a dependency whose read is one stage element
with $x^{\mathsf T}Sx$ on its lower side and off it at once (a
dependency whose lower-list part is the sum's unit sits inside the
independent complement list), against a stage read's one side
(Lemma~\ref{lem:stage}): the joint list is independent,
$\nu_1+\mu_2\le N$ at $\nu_2+\mu_2=N$, and the exchange symmetrized reads
$\nu_1=\nu_2$. The joint-rank read at an arbitrary first list is the
\emph{forcing clause}: a list of $k$ vectors with every
$S$-read of a combination off the sum's unit on the lower side,
joined to any split's complement list,
forces $\operatorname{rev}(S)\ge k$, the strict read forcing the
list's independence outright: a combination reading the sum's unit
at coefficients off the unit family reads its own lower side
besides, against the trichotomy. The \emph{dual clause} runs at
the complement side with the independence its own stated clause,
an at-or-above read admitting a dependency at the sum's unit: an
independent list of $k$ vectors with every $S$-read of a
combination at its upper side or equal members, joined to any
split's lower list, forces $k+\operatorname{rev}(S)\le N$.

\emph{The consequences.} A split of $T^{\mathsf T}ST$ at $U$ is a
split of $S$ at $TU$: the count is invariant. Splits of $P$ and of
$S_P$ concatenate through the displayed congruence to a split of
$S$: inertia adds, and the two forcing clauses price the identity
besides: the pivot's and the deflation's lower lists, padded and
lifted through the solve with every cross pairing the witness's
own unit read, force one side, and the complement lists at the
dual clause the other. At a coupling of equal members the lift is
a padding besides, and the pricing runs without the solve: the two
lower lists, each padded into its own half of the join, read the
block diagonal at the two forms' sum with the cross pairings at
the sum's unit, and the complement lists at the dual clause read
the other side. An $\mathrm{LDL}^{\mathsf T}$ is a split at
order-$1$ blocks, the congruence by the unit-triangular witness $T$
at $L^{\mathsf T}T=1$, one back-substitution, onto the pivot
diagonal, whose $k$-th leading minor is the product of the first $k$
pivots. At $S\preceq S'$ the datum $R$ at $S'=S+R$ is positive
semidefinite, so $x^{\mathsf T}Sx+x^{\mathsf T}Rx=x^{\mathsf T}S'x$ at every
$x$ through the
congruence with the $R$-read on its upper side or of equal members;
on $S'$'s lower list the $S$-read sits below the $S'$-read, on the
lower side with it, and the
forcing clause reads
$\operatorname{rev}(S)\ge\operatorname{rev}(S')$. For a
compression $P^{\mathsf T}SP$, the compression's lower list
maps through $P$ to an independent list with every $S$-read of a
combination off the sum's unit on the lower side (a
combination with $Pc$ the sum's unit at $c$ off it reads
$c^{\mathsf T}P^{\mathsf T}SPc$ on its lower side and of equal members
at once, against the trichotomy), and the forcing clause closes it.
\end{proof}
\end{lemma}

\begin{theorem}[The designation, and the witness]
\label{thm:certconstruct}
Let $H,G$ have entries of a located stage
(Lemma~\ref{lem:stage}) with $G$ positive definite, and let $\mathrm{count}(a):=
\operatorname{rev}(H\!:\!aG)$; at a balance-pair level
$\langle x\!:\!y\rangle$ (Definition~\ref{def:ground}) the count is
\[
\mathrm{count}\langle x\!:\!y\rangle\;:=\;
\operatorname{rev}\big(H+y\,G\;:\;x\,G\big)\,,
\]
the level's second member cross-added onto the pencil's own side, so
a root on its lower side reads its bracket as one on its upper does; each
count exact by symmetric $\mathrm{LDL}^{\mathsf T}$
(Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}).
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em,itemsep=1pt]
\item \emph{(The designation.)} A \emph{designation} of a jump of
the counting function is the data $(l,h,w)$ at $l+w=h$ with the two
counts read at a gap, $\mathrm{count}(l)+g=\mathrm{count}(h)$ at
$g\ge1$, the width $w$ at most the $\delta$ of
Theorem~\ref{thm:windowsep}: the bracket is narrower than the
separation, so it admits one distinct root alone, at multiplicity
$g$, and the reads are its whole verification. At the least root the
lower read sharpens to the pair at $l$ positive semidefinite, one
split and one count. Designations exist at every stated width: the
radius of Theorem~\ref{thm:windowsep} reads two starting levels, the
lower below every root at the split and the upper beyond every root
at the full count; from two levels at counts at a gap, the mean's
count sits at a gap against one end's, the half at that gap the next
bracket, the width halving, a refinement closing in the least $k$ at
$w_0\le2^k\delta$ from the starting gap $w_0$ at $l_0+w_0=h_0$,
each step one count; and at the least-root instance a mean at the
lower end's count either is positive semidefinite, moving the lower
level, or is singular there, the mean naming the root and one
recentering at
$2w'\le\delta$ emitting the designation. The refinement runs at any
stage read whose trichotomy exchanges sides along a segment, a
polynomial's side read at Theorem~\ref{thm:windowsep}'s separation
among them, the equality outcome naming a root outright with the
recentering emitting its bracket. An emitted designation enters as
certificate data, its reads re-read at every verification.
\item \emph{(The witness $u$.)} For \emph{any} form pair $(A\!:\!B)$ of pair
entries with $\operatorname{rev}(A\!:\!B)\ge1$, the symmetric $\mathrm{LDL}^{\mathsf T}$
of its elimination datum has a lower-side pivot, and the corresponding column of the accumulated
pair-entried transform is a vector $u$ with
$u^{\mathsf T}Bu>u^{\mathsf T}Au$, the reversal's read
off the elimination. At $(H\!:\!aG)$ that is the certificate's witness whenever
$\mathrm{count}(a)\ge1$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{lemma}[The count is a cell function]\label{lem:cellcount}
Let $(H(x),G(x))$ be a symmetric pencil whose entries are polynomial in
$x=(x_1,\dots,x_m)$ over $\mathbb P$, with $G(x)\succ0$ on a box $X$, let
$a$ be a pair, and write $D(x)$ for the pair
$\big(H(x)\!:\!aG(x)\big)$'s site-datum determinant. Then
$\mathrm{count}(x):=\operatorname{rev}\big(H(x)\!:\!aG(x)\big)$ is
constant on every segment between stage points of $X$ avoiding
$D$'s root locus (the stages of Lemma~\ref{lem:stage}), the
avoidance's certificate the proof's pivot cover: finitely many
subintervals chained at shared endpoints, each with a designated
principal minor of order at most two whose determinant keeps its
side along the subinterval at the priced side read
(Lemma~\ref{lem:stage}) at a bound containing the subinterval, at
order two its leading entry so as well where the determinant sits
on its upper side, and
beneath it the cleared deflation's own cover, the determinant's
root-freeness the cover's read; the locus's points are located
over $D$'s squarefree part, the division descent's iterated
divisions with the B\'ezout witnesses the certificate data
(Theorem~\ref{thm:windowsep}'s descent at
Lemma~\ref{lem:stagesplit}'s squarefree read), a bracket's root
count the segment count at the cleared variable
(Lemma~\ref{lem:hermitesign} at Lemma~\ref{lem:deckfactor}'s
clearing); hence the count is one integer on each
piecewise-linearly connected cell of $X$ off that locus; and at a
stage point of the locus adjacent to a cell whose pair is positive
semidefinite, every pencil
root is at least $a$, the point's read entering as its own split
(Theorem~\ref{thm:certconstruct}'s certificate entry).
\begin{proof}
Write $S(t)$ for the pair's site datum at $H=aG+S$ along the
segment, entries polynomial in $t$, its
determinant with every root off the segment; the claim is by induction
on the order. At order one the count is the side's, and the entry
keeps its side along each piece of the cover at the priced side
read (Lemma~\ref{lem:stage}), the endpoint value clearing the
piece's width against the derivative's coefficient fold at a bound
containing the piece. At order
$N$: inertia adds at a nonsingular principal pivot of order at most $2$,
and $S(t)$, its determinant of unequal members, has one at every $t$
(Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}). The principal minors of orders $1$ and $2$
with a coefficient of unequal members have finitely many roots; at
each root point, and at the segment's ends, the unequal-membered
determinant names a minor of unequal members there, whose own roots sit off that point at a distance a bracket
certifies, so the segment is covered by finitely many
subintervals of pair endpoints, each with one designated minor whose
members differ throughout, consecutive
ones overlapping at pairs. On one subinterval with pivot $P(t)$: the
pivot's inertia is constant ($\det P$ keeps one side, the order-one read; at
order two, the determinant on its lower side reads one unit per
side, the mixed block, and on its upper side both diagonal
entries keep one shared side, their roots off the subinterval,
Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}); and
$(\det P)^2\,S_P$ has polynomial entries, the deflation cleared by
the pivot's adjugate
($P\,\operatorname{adj}P=\det P\cdot1$, Definition~\ref{def:elim}'s
minors), the positive square fixing every
count, with $\det P\cdot\det\big((\det P)^2S_P\big)
=(\det P)^{2o}\,\det S$ at $o$ the deflation's order
($k+o=N$; Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}'s split), its
roots off the
segment, so the
induction reads $S_P$'s count constant, and
$\operatorname{rev}(S)=\operatorname{rev}(P)
+\operatorname{rev}(S_P)$ is one integer on the subinterval. The
overlaps chain the subintervals, so the count is one integer along the
segment, and cells connected by such segments read one count. At a
boundary point $x^*$ beside a positive-semidefinite cell, a root below $a$ gives
$u$ with the read $u^{\mathsf T}S(x^*)u$ on its lower side; along a segment into the cell,
$t\mapsto u^{\mathsf T}S(x(t))u$ is a polynomial, on its lower side at
the boundary point and so
on it over an initial piece its coefficient folds name, putting a
lower-side direction inside the cell: against the cell's count.
\end{proof}
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[The located stage]\label{lem:stage}
\emph{The arithmetic.} A \emph{located root} of a polynomial over a
\emph{located stage} ($\mathbb P$, or a field built here, every element
with its bracket) is a root
$\varepsilon$ of its squarefree part $S$ (the division
descent, Theorem~\ref{thm:windowsep}),
with an isolating bracket of balance-pair endpoints
(Definition~\ref{def:ground}) off $S$'s roots, narrower
than half the separation of Theorem~\ref{thm:windowsep}, whose
derivation is stage arithmetic outright, division reads,
coefficient folds and bracket comparisons. The stage $K(\varepsilon)$ is $K[x]$
read at $\varepsilon$: at $p\in K[x]$ with $g$ the greatest common
divisor of $(p,S)$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:windowsep}'s descent), the value
$p(\varepsilon)$ reads equal members exactly where $g$ exchanges sides on the bracket
($g$ a factor of the squarefree $S$, so its one possible root there is simple,
and it is $\varepsilon$, the bracket isolating); at $p(\varepsilon)$
of unequal members
the root sits on the cofactor $S_1$ at $g\,S_1=S$, coprime to $p$ since $S$ is
squarefree, and the B\'ezout read $u\,p+v\,S_1=1$ reads at the root
to the \emph{unit witness}, $p(\varepsilon)\,u(\varepsilon)=1$.
Sums and products
are remainder reads, the division at $S$, a value's unit is
the B\'ezout read's witness,
and a value's side at the root is a count read
(Definition~\ref{def:ground}), the bracket the certificate naming
which root, every entry an elimination read of the stage below, so
the adjunction stays grounded and the recursion ends at the ground
pairs' own comparisons. At $p(\varepsilon)$ of unequal members the
unit witness floors the value: at $U_1$ the coefficient
fold of $u$ over the bracket (Theorem~\ref{thm:windowsep}'s
magnitudes), the
witness identity read cross-multiplied floors the margin of
$p(\varepsilon)$ at $\mathrm{margin}\cdot U_1\ge1$; and the
\emph{priced side read}: at bracket endpoints inside a stated
bound's segment, the bound entering at a second datum beyond the
sum's unit, the radius segment among them at the leading
coefficient's margin, two bracket
points' values of $p$ differ by at most the bracket's width against
the derivative's coefficient fold at the bound
(Theorem~\ref{thm:windowsep}'s magnitudes, the binomial identity
per occupied key), so an endpoint value of $p$ clearing that price
keeps its side at every bracket point, the root's among them: the
endpoint side with the cleared comparison is the side read's
certificate, $p$'s image width over the bracket priced by the
fold. The construction closes on itself: $K(\varepsilon)$'s elements each
have their bracket (the isolating data, their image widths priced
above) and its division reads, coefficient folds, endpoint
evaluations and count reads
are the
display's, so $K(\varepsilon)$ is a located stage and adjunction
iterates; a finite root list enters as a \emph{tower}, one
extension per root, each extension's polynomial over the stage the
earlier extensions built, the extension order certificate data: a
stored tower names its
order, and every read runs through the arithmetic above.

\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[The pencil's split]\label{lem:split}
For symmetric $H$ and $G\succ0$ of order $N$ over a
stage, with $Z(x)$ the level pair's site datum at $x\,G=H+Z(x)$ and
$\chi(x):=\det Z(x)$,
\[
\chi(x)\;=\;\det G\ \prod_{j=1}^N\langle x\!:\!\varepsilon_j\rangle,
\qquad
\mathrm{count}(a)\;=\;\#\{\,j:\varepsilon_j<a\,\}
\ \text{ at $a$ off the roots},
\]
the factors Definition~\ref{def:poly}'s linear factors at the
$\varepsilon_j$, located roots, and a unit congruence $S_\chi$ over the
stage they generate maps the pencil to diagonal form,
\[
S_\chi^{\mathsf T}\,Z(x)\,S_\chi\;=\;
\mathrm{diag}\big(\langle x\!:\!\varepsilon_j\rangle\,g_j\big),
\qquad g_j\ \text{on their upper sides}.
\]
\begin{proof}
Over the pairs the counting function climbs to $N$ from the
positive-semidefinite read between the radius reads of
Theorem~\ref{thm:windowsep}, and its jumps enter as designations at
the separation (Theorem~\ref{thm:certconstruct}). At a located root $\varepsilon$, the
kernel of $Z(\varepsilon)$ over $K(\varepsilon)$ has a spanning list by
elimination (Lemma~\ref{lem:stage}), of some dimension $m\ge1$, $G$-orthogonalized within,
and its $G$-complement $W$ another; at $z$ in the kernel and $w\in W$,
$z^{\mathsf T}Z(x)\,w=\langle x\!:\!\varepsilon\rangle\,z^{\mathsf T}Gw$,
of equal members at the complement's defining read, so the
congruence by the two lists is block diagonal, the kernel block
$\langle x\!:\!\varepsilon\rangle\,G_0$ at the kernel's Gram $G_0\succ0$ diagonal, and
\[
\chi(x)\;=\;c\,\langle x\!:\!\varepsilon\rangle^m\,\chi_W(x),\qquad
c\ \text{on its upper side},
\]
$\chi_W$ the deflated pencil's. $\chi_W(\varepsilon)$ is of unequal
members: a kernel vector
of the deflated pencil at $\varepsilon$ has its pairings against $W$ and
against the kernel both of equal members, so it is a kernel vector of the full
pencil lying inside $W$, and the kernel meets $W$ at the sum's unit alone.
Induction on $W$ splits $\chi$ over located roots, each
deflation's root adjoined over the stage the earlier roots built
(Lemma~\ref{lem:stage}), and concatenates the per-step lists to
$S_\chi$, block diagonal for $G$ and for $H$ at once by
the pairings above; the count identity is then the congruence read
(Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}): at $a$ off the roots, the pair
$(H\!:\!aG)$'s elimination datum is the displayed datum's
balance partner, so
$\operatorname{rev}(H\!:\!aG)=\operatorname{rev}\,
\mathrm{diag}\big(\langle\varepsilon_j\!:\!a\rangle\,g_j\big)=\#\{j:\varepsilon_j<a\}$.
The completed congruence returns the factorization read: the
congruence identity's determinant collects the product read at the
exchanged lists (Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia};
Definition~\ref{def:elim}) to $\det S_\chi^{\,2}\,\chi(x)$ against
the diagonal blocks' product
$\prod_j\langle x\!:\!\varepsilon_j\rangle\,g_j$; the identity's
linear coefficient, $S_\chi^{\mathsf T}GS_\chi$ at the diagonal of
the scales, collects its own determinants to
$\det S_\chi^{\,2}\,\det G$ against $\prod_jg_j$, and at a located
root's pair data the cleared factor enters at the scale, the
diagonal's entry $g_j\,(d_jx-n_j)$ at the root
$[\,n_j\!:\!d_j\,]$ with the linear coefficients collecting to
$\prod_jd_j\,g_j$ and the clearing $\prod_jd_j$ riding onto the
factorization's first member, one value at every representative
(Definition~\ref{def:ground}); and the shared
factor $\det S_\chi^{\,2}$, off equal members at the unit
congruence, withdraws at the products' one value key by key
(Definition~\ref{def:poly}): the displayed factorization is the
congruence certificate's own read.
\end{proof}
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Slab pivots: the factorization, the counts, and the Green
products]\label{lem:greenprod}
Let $S$ be symmetric over a located stage with a slab split $1..L$,
entries at the diagonal and neighboring slab pairs alone,
$A_i:=S[i,i]$, $B_i:=S[i,i+1]$, and the two
pivot recursions at solved witnesses, each solve the adjugate column
against the determinant,
\begin{gather*}
X_L=A_L,\quad X_i+B_i\,R_{i+1}=A_i\ \ \text{at}\ \
X_{i+1}R_{i+1}=B_i^{\mathsf T};\\
Y_1=A_1,\quad Y_{i+1}+B_i^{\mathsf T}\,C_i=A_{i+1}\ \ \text{at}\ \
Y_iC_i=B_i ,
\end{gather*}
at pivots of unequal-membered determinant (a singular slab pivot
re-orders within its slab by
Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}'s principal pivots of order at most two, the count
unchanged). Then:
\emph{(i)} the Schur complement of the tail $[i{+}1,L]$ onto $[1,i]$
maps $A_i$ to $X_i$ and fixes every other block: eliminating slab $L$
touches $A_{L-1}$ alone, the entries at $i<L-1$ against $L$ off the
band, and downward induction
extends it, so one recursion datum holds the whole tail;
\emph{(ii)} $\operatorname{rev}(S)=\sum_i\operatorname{rev}(X_i)$
and $\det S=\prod_i\det X_i$, each elimination a unit-triangular
congruence (Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia});
\emph{(iii)} with $Z_j$ the join's datum at $Z_j+A_j=X_j+Y_j$, read
off the two recursions, the solution of $S\,u=v$ at $v$ supported in
slab $j$ reads blockwise, at $i\le j$,
\[
Z_j\,u_j=v_j,
\qquad
u_i=T_i\,T_{i+1}\cdots T_h\,u_j\quad\text{at}\quad h+1=j\ ,
\]
at the \emph{transfer factors} $T_k$, each the memberwise swap of
the witness $C_k$, so $Y_k\,T_k+B_k$ is the sum's unit:
every Green block is a product of transfer factors and one corner
solve.
\begin{proof}
(i) and (ii) are the displayed inductions and congruences. (iii): solve
$Su=v$ at $v$ supported in slab $j$. The rows above $j$ read
$X_{i+1}u_{i+1}+B_i^{\mathsf T}u_i$ at the sum's unit, the tail closing from $L$
downward as in (i); the rows below read
$Y_i\,u_i+B_i\,u_{i+1}$ at the sum's unit at every $i$ below $j$,
the induction from $1$ on the $Y$
recursion; and
substituting both into row $j$ leaves $Z_j\,u_j=v_j$. The display
telescopes the descent, and at $L=2$ it is the two-slab solve outright.
\end{proof}
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[The spectator law]\label{lem:spectator}
Let $S$, $S'$ be two families as in Lemma~\ref{lem:greenprod} on one slab
split, with equal data beyond slab $w$: $A'_i=A_i$ at $i>w$ and
$B'_i=B_i$ at $i\ge w$.
\emph{(i) One sandwich identity.} The tail pivots are equal outright, $X'_i=X_i$
at $i>w$, the recursion running on equal data from an equal seed; the
deviation sits in the boundary-direction pivots alone, and at $i\ge w$
the deviation datum $D_i$ at $Y'_i=Y_i+D_i$ obeys the exact sandwich
\[
D_{i+1}
\;=\;T_i^{\mathsf T}\,D_i\,T'_i
\]
at the two families' transfer factors
(Lemma~\ref{lem:greenprod}(iii)),
the two recursions' joins at equal $A$, $B$ read against each other,
telescoping to a product form: the deviation at depth $i$ beyond the
perturbation is $D_{w+1}$ sandwiched by the transfer factors of
Lemma~\ref{lem:greenprod}(iii). Re-convergence of the recursion and the
Green products' falloff are the one sandwich identity, stated twice.
\emph{(ii) Caps propagate geometrically.} At certified pairs
$\lambda_i,\lambda'_i$ with
$T_i^{\mathsf T}G\,T_i\preceq\lambda_i^2\,G$ and
$T'^{\mathsf T}_iG\,T'_i\preceq\lambda'^2_i\,G$ at the two families'
transfer factors, a cap
$D_i$ two-sided at $c_i\,G$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}) propagates to
$2\,D_{i+1}$ two-sided at $c_i\,(\lambda_i^2+\lambda'^2_i)\,G$: at
$u:=T_ix$ and $v:=T'_ix$, the cap's two splits at $u+v$ and at
$u+\check v$, $\check v$ the memberwise swap, collect to
$2\,u^{\mathsf T}D_iv$ capped two-sidedly at
$c_i\,(u^{\mathsf T}Gu+v^{\mathsf T}Gv)$, and
the certificates price the two squares. Iterating, the bracket at depth
$i$ is named and geometric.
\emph{(iii) The bulk pivot is the deck-interior solvent.} At bulk data
$A_i=A$, $B_i=B$ at $B$ of unequal-membered determinant, read in a
congruence mapping
$G$ to the identity ($G$'s factorization a stage datum,
comparisons then the spectral reads of Lemma~\ref{lem:split}), the
\emph{deck polynomial} $p$, the determinant of the quadratic pair
$(B^{\mathsf T}z^2+B\!:\!Az)$'s site datum, obeys
the deck symmetry, its coefficients equal at every index pair joining
to $2m$ ($A$ symmetric, the transpose identity exchanging $B$ with
$B^{\mathsf T}$), the display of its deck symbol
(Lemma~\ref{lem:deckfactor}), and a solution pair $(Y,X)$ of the
pivot identities $YX=B$ and $Y+B^{\mathsf T}X=A$ has its
\emph{solvent} $X$ satisfying
$B^{\mathsf T}X^2+B=A\,X$, and the
pair's site datum factors exactly,
\[
U(z)\;=\;U_1(z)\,U_2(z)
\qquad\text{at}\quad
B^{\mathsf T}z^2+B=A\,z+U(z),\quad
zB^{\mathsf T}+B^{\mathsf T}X=A+U_1(z),\quad z=X+U_2(z)\,,
\]
so $X$'s polynomial is a factor of $p$ (the adjugate identity reading
Cayley--Hamilton). Off the band (Lemma~\ref{lem:deckfactor}'s band
read, the deck relation's crossing locus), $p=c\,p_{\mathrm{int}}\,
p_{\mathrm{ext}}$ over a stored tower; the \emph{interior kernel}
$V:=\ker p_{\mathrm{int}}(C)$ at the companion action $C$, the shift
$(x,y)\mapsto(y,y')$ at its second component's witness
$B^{\mathsf T}y'+Bx=Ay$,
has a spanning list by elimination over the tower, each companion
step one solved read at $B^{\mathsf T}$, stacked
$\big[\begin{smallmatrix}V_1\\V_2\end{smallmatrix}\big]$, and the
\emph{graph read} $\det V_1$ is one tower sign. Where it reads
unequal members, $X_*$ at $X_*V_1=V_2$ satisfies the solvent identity
with polynomial $p_{\mathrm{int}}$ and $Y_*$ at $Y_*X_*=B$ solves
the pivot identities; and every solution whose solvent polynomial is
$p_{\mathrm{int}}$ equals it, its graph filling the one kernel $V$.
Three counts at a located symmetric $\hat Y$ and pairs
$\lambda<1$, $y_0$, $\rho$, $\rho_0$, at the \emph{pivot map}
$Y\mapsto Y^{\sharp}:=A+B^{\mathsf T}T$ over the transfer factor $T$,
the memberwise swap of the witness at $Y\,C=B$, so $Y\,T+B$ is the
sum's unit (Lemma~\ref{lem:greenprod}(iii)),
\[
\hat T^{\mathsf T}\hat T\preceq\lambda^2,
\qquad
\hat Y\succeq y_0+\rho,
\qquad
D_\circ\ \text{two-sided at}\ \rho_0\ \ \text{at}\ \ \hat Y^{\sharp}=\hat Y+D_\circ ,
\]
with $\lambda_\circ$ at $y_0\,\lambda_\circ=\lambda\,(y_0+\rho)$,
$\mu:=\lambda_\circ^2<1$
and $\rho_0+\mu\rho\le\rho$, make the map send the ball at deviation
data $D$ two-sided at $\rho$, $Y=\hat Y+D$, to itself and contract its
deviations at
modulus $\mu$: a ball member's certificate is $\lambda_\circ$, its
transfer factor priced through the floor by the identity
$Y\,D_T=D\,\hat T$ at $\hat T=T+D_T$, the image
deviation is (i)'s sandwich capped by (ii)'s certificates, and the
center's displacement tops up to $\rho$. A ball member prices its
form at the floor: the deviation's cap withdraws against the
center's floor at the gram's read, the member's form at or beyond
$y_0$'s multiple of the gram's at every vector, so every split of
a ball member reads the upper side throughout
(Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}). The graph read joins the
validity data: where it reads unequal members, the bracket comparison
$D_*$ two-sided at $\rho$ at $Y_*=\hat Y+D_*$ is one count at the tower datum, and where
it holds $Y_*$ is the ball's one solution of the pivot identities: two ball
solutions read their deviation datum $D$ through (i)'s sandwich, the
two-sided cap at $c\,G$ returning at $\mu\,c\,G$ by (ii) and
iterating below every pair, and a located entry below every
pair is of equal members; the solvent is capped inside by
the first count ($X^{\mathsf T}X\preceq\lambda_\circ^2$ prices every
polynomial root's square below $1$), so its polynomial is
$p_{\mathrm{int}}$ and its graph fills the one kernel $V$. The graph
read's side and the three counts are each cell data of the family's
parameters (Lemma~\ref{lem:cellcount}; Lemma~\ref{lem:stage}). The scalar instance, the walk pencil's bulk data at $B=1$: the
boundary entry solves the pivot identity $m\,Y=1$, the
solvent, $p_{\mathrm{int}}=\langle z\!:\!z_*\rangle$ at the
interior member $z_*$, and
(i)'s sandwich is the error law $e_{L+1}=z^2e_L+z\,e_L\,e_{L+1}$,
contraction factor $z^2$, marginal exactly at the crossings,
$z^2=1$ there.
\end{lemma}

\begin{theorem}[The window is a root separation, and is explicit]
\label{thm:windowsep}
For a polynomial $P$ over $\mathbb P$ (Definition~\ref{def:poly}), or
over a located stage, every step below one of its operations, a
division, a coefficient fold or a bracket comparison: the stage's
distinct roots have a computed pair separation floor and a computed radius,
by ground arithmetic alone.

\emph{The magnitude carrier.} The \emph{magnitude} of a
balance pair is its members' larger against smaller, the sorted pair
$|\langle u\!:\!v\rangle|:=
\langle\,\max\{u,v\}\!:\!\min\{u,v\}\,\rangle$ at the trichotomy
read, one value at every pair (a summand on both members fixing
it), its value at unequal members the margin, the exceeding
member's gap (Definition~\ref{def:ground}). A sum's magnitude
sits at or below the magnitudes' sum, and a product's magnitude is
the magnitudes' product, the members' trichotomies read case by
case with a memberwise swap of either datum swapping the product's
members and fixing every magnitude. So a polynomial's evaluations
at points of magnitude at or below a bound are capped by the
\emph{coefficient fold}, the coefficients' magnitudes folded
against the bound's powers, one read over the occupancy family.

\emph{The squarefree part, and the radius.} The \emph{division
descent}, iterated divisions of Definition~\ref{def:poly},
computes the \emph{greatest common divisor} $c(P,P')$: the
descent's last occupied remainder, monic at the rescaling by its
leading coefficient (each coefficient the cofactor at the leading
one), a factor of $P$ and of $P'$ (each remainder joins the two
above it through its division, so the last is a factor of
every earlier one, the two seeds among them), and greatest as
named: a common factor $g$ of the two seeds is a factor of every
remainder down the descent, $P=SQ+R$ at the cofactors $g\,P_1=P$
and $g\,S_1=S$ reading $R=g\,(P_1+\check T)$ at $T=S_1Q$, the
check the memberwise swap over the balance pairs, the last
remainder among them.  At the integer representatives the
rescaling enters as data: the divisor at its primitive list with
the stated positive top, the cofactors at their clearing
multipliers, and the division at a stated top the cleared
descent, $\ell^{\,k}\,P=q\,D+r$ at the top $\ell$, one scale per
eliminated key, the exactness the remainder's read; each
remainder strips to its primitive list at its coefficients' one
shared count, the walk's rows at their stated multipliers with
the strip absorbed there, and every certificate reads at the
cleared data.  The squarefree part
$S$ is the cofactor at
$c(P,P')\,S=P$, and the B\'ezout pair $uS+vS'=1$ arrives with the descent
at $(S,S')$ ending at a unit: an irreducible factor $q$ of $S$ has
some multiplicity $m\ge1$ in $P=q^{m}R$, the remainders of $q'$
(the derivative's degree drop) and of $R$ (the exact
multiplicity) at $q$ occupied and their B\'ezout witnesses
composing to an occupied remainder of $m\,q'R+q\,R'$ at the count
$m$ a natural, $q\,R'$'s summand the sum's unit there; at $m=1$
the derivative is that read outright, $P'=m\,q'R+q\,R'$, and at
the join $m'+1=m$ it is $P'=q^{\,m'}(m\,q'R+q\,R')$ at
multiplicity exactly $m'$: the greatest common divisor's $q$-part sits one power
below $P$'s, the unit at $m=1$, so the cofactor $S$ reads $q$ at
one, and at $S=qT$ the remainders of $T$ and of
$S'=q'T+qT'$ at $q$ are occupied: $P$'s roots are $S$'s, each
simple there. Every root over the stage sits in the \emph{radius segment},
between $B$'s balance partner and $B$ at the pair
$B:=[\,c+\mathcal H:c\,]$, $c$ the leading coefficient's margin
(the top key's coefficient of unequal members, the degree's own
read)
and $\mathcal H$ the height, the largest coefficient magnitude: at a
point of margin $y=1+g$ at or beyond $B$, with $k$ the top key,
the geometric telescope $g\sum_{i<k}y^{\,i}+1=y^{\,k}$ reads the
tail's cap $\mathcal H\sum_{i<k}y^{\,i}$ below $c\,y^{\,k}$ at the
radius' read $c\,g\ge\mathcal H$, so the leading term dominates
and the evaluation reads unequal members on the leading term's own
side, the tail joined at a magnitude below the term's.

\emph{The separation.} At a root $\alpha$ the unit witness
$v(\alpha)\,S'(\alpha)=1$ reads at the magnitudes: $V$ the
coefficient
fold of $v$ at the radius, so the margin of $S'(\alpha)$
cross-multiplies to $\mathrm{margin}\cdot V\ge1$. Between two roots
over the stage, at the stage gap $G$ at $\alpha+G=\beta$, the binomial
identity per occupied key at $i\ge2$, the constant key's read
equal at both points and the key-one read the gap's own join
$\beta=\alpha+G$,
\[
\beta^{\,i}\;=\;\alpha^{\,i}+i\,\alpha^{\,h}\,G
\;+\;G^{2}\!\!\sum_{a+b+2=i}\!(a{+}1)\,\alpha^{\,a}\beta^{\,b}
\qquad(h+1=i) ,
\]
summed against the coefficients, reads
$S(\beta)=S(\alpha)+G\,S'(\alpha)+G^{2}\,T$ at the collected tail
$T:=\sum_is_i\sum_{a+b+2=i}(a{+}1)\,\alpha^{a}\beta^{b}$; the two
roots' equal-membered reads leave $G\,(S'(\alpha)+G\,T)$ of equal
members, so at $G$'s unit witness $S'(\alpha)$ and $G\,T$ are
balance partners, and the margin of $S'(\alpha)$ sits at or below
$G\,\Lambda$ at the \emph{curvature fold}
$\Lambda:=\sum_i\tbinom i2\,|s_i|\,B^{\,e}$ over the occupied
keys at the joins $e+2=i$, $|s_i|$ the coefficient's magnitude, the
splitting count $\sum_{a+b+2=i}(a{+}1)=\tbinom i2$ and both roots
capped by the radius. So the stage's distinct
roots satisfy
\[
\Lambda\,V\,G\ \ge\ 1\ ,
\qquad
2\,\max\{\Lambda\,V,\ 1\}\ \delta\;=\;1\ ,
\]
one pair comparison and one cofactor per polynomial
(Definition~\ref{def:ground}), $\delta$ below the separation and at
most $[\,1:2\,]$; the radius $B$ is the
\emph{leading-term bound}. The object is a distance between
roots of ground polynomials, and such a distance is a separation
bound, which is explicit.
\end{theorem}

\begin{lemma}[The stage splits every polynomial]\label{lem:stagesplit}
Every polynomial of degree at least $1$ over a located stage splits,
over a stored tower (Lemma~\ref{lem:stage}), into located linear
factors and \emph{definite} quadratic factors at pair data $(h,q)$,
the root relation $z^2+q=h\,z$, at the definiteness gap $g$ at
$h^2+g=4q$, the data tower elements, multiplicities by
iterated divisions.
\begin{proof}
\emph{Factorization closes over finite cofactor lists.} Over
$\mathbb P$ a polynomial reads at its primitive integer form, the
pair coefficients cleared at one shared second member with the
content collected, and a factor reads primitive as well, the
contents splitting off (Gauss); a primitive factor of degree $d$ is
determined by its values at $d+1$ integers of occupied value, a
point of equal-membered value reading its linear factor outright,
and each value is one of the finitely many cofactor reads of the
primitive form's value there at either side, a natural or its
balance partner: the value lists are finite, each selection
interpolates to one polynomial, and the division tests it,
the remainder of equal members exactly at a factor
(Kronecker). Over an extension $K(\varepsilon)$ the norm
$\mathrm{Res}_x\big(S_\varepsilon(x),f\big)$
(Definition~\ref{def:elim}'s resultant, in the variable $x$) maps
$f$ to the
stage below; at a shift $z\mapsto z+\lambda\varepsilon$ whose norm
is squarefree (the failing $\lambda$ are roots of one resultant,
finitely many, a scalar-pair $\lambda$ off them read directly), the
greatest common divisor of $f$ with each factor of the shifted
norm, the division descent's end, recovers $f$'s factors
over the extension (Trager), descending to $\mathbb P$. The
remainder lists at an
irreducible factor (Definition~\ref{def:poly}) are a
discrete field, every occupied element at its B\'ezout
unit witness, so iterating the adjunction reads a full formal root
list, the \emph{splitting data}: finite
algebra over the stage, in which a root of a product is a root of
one factor.

\emph{The quadratic extensions.} A located positive $c$ adjoins
one extension, the remainder lists at the relation $z^2=c$'s polynomial
(Definition~\ref{def:poly}); the \emph{adjoined root at} $z^2=c$
is the linear monomial's class, located at the bracket of $c$'s
sorted display against $1$, the two side reads the refinement's seed
(Theorem~\ref{thm:certconstruct}(1)) with the equality outcome
naming the root outright, so the extension is a located stage again
(Lemma~\ref{lem:stage}). The \emph{located
pairs} are the remainder lists at $z^2+1$
(Definition~\ref{def:poly}), $(a,b)$ written $a+ib$ at
$i$ the linear monomial's class, the \emph{key swap} the linear
key's memberwise swap, an automorphism of the carrier's reads. A
located pair at $b$ of unequal members has a square root
$u+iv$ on three such extensions:
$w$ the adjoined root at $z^2=a^2+b^2$, then over $K(w)$
$u$ the adjoined root at $z^2=[\,1:2\,]\,(w+a)$ and
$v$ the adjoined root at $z^2=[\,1:2\,]\,g$ at the gap
$g$ at $a+g=w$, the order's witness ($w^2$ exceeds $a^2$ at $b$'s
occupied square, so $w$ exceeds $a$ and its balance partner),
$v$'s side one bracket read against $b$'s; the tower
reads $u^2=v^2+a$ and $(2uv)^2=b^2$ outright, so
$(u+iv)^2=a+ib$. A quadratic over located pairs at the root
relation $z^2+q=hz$ splits over located pairs, the trichotomy of
its \emph{discriminant datum} $D$ at $D+4q=h^2$: at $D$'s second
component of unequal members the extensions read its square root
$u+iv$, the two roots the data at $2z=h+(u{+}iv)$ and
$2z+(u{+}iv)=h$; at the second component of equal members the
first component $D_1$'s trichotomy reads: on its upper side, $e$
the adjoined root at $z^2=D_1$ with the roots the data at
$2z=h+e$ and $2z+e=h$; at equality, the double root, the
datum at $2z=h$; and on its lower side, $e$ the adjoined root
at $z^2=m$ at $D_1$'s margin $m$, the roots the data at
$2z=h+e\,i$ and $2z+e\,i=h$.

\emph{The Laplace descent.} The claim: a stage polynomial $p$ of
degree $k\ge1$ has a located root or a definite quadratic
divisor, verified by the division. Induct on the degree's
$2$-adic valuation $\nu$. At odd degree the leading term dominates
beyond Theorem~\ref{thm:windowsep}'s radius, the side reads at the
radius and at its balance partner differ, and a located root's
designation follows at every width,
Theorem~\ref{thm:certconstruct}(1)'s refinement at the entry's side reads.
At positive $\nu$, at a
scalar pair $t$ the resolvent
\[
B_t(x)\;:=\;\prod_{l<l'}\big\langle\,x\;:\;z_l+z_{l'}+t\,z_lz_{l'}\,\big\rangle
\]
over $p$'s formal root list has symmetric coefficients, hence stage
coefficients through the elementary reads (the lexicographic descent
on symmetric monomials, one generic identity), and degree
$\binom k2$ at the valuation join $\nu'+1=\nu$: the induction hands
each $t$ a
located root or a definite divisor, either way a located pair
$\beta_t$ (the second component of equal members, or the adjoined
root at $z^2=[\,1:4\,]\,g$ at the definiteness gap $g$), a
witness-verified root of $B_t$. Take
the splitting data over the one tower with every $\beta_t$ and
$i$: there $B_t$ is the displayed product and $\beta_t$, a root of
$B_t$ in a field, is one factor's own root: $\beta_t$ meets a pair
$(l,l')$. Over $\binom k2+1$ distinct scalar pairs $t$ two meet one
pair, and there, the lesser parameter written $t'$ at the gap $w$
at $t'+w=t$,
\[
w\,q+\beta_{t'}\;=\;\beta_t\,,
\qquad
h+t\,q\;=\;\beta_t
\]
name the located pairs $q$ and $h$, each one value by the positive
gap's rescaling, the displays' own joins at the meeting pair
($\beta_t=z_l+z_{l'}+t\,z_lz_{l'}$ at both parameters), with the
relation $z^2+q=hz$'s quadratic a factor of $p$; the finitely many
parameter pairs are each tested by the division, and the
display guarantees a passing test. At a passing pair the
discriminant trichotomy
splits the quadratic, giving a root $a+ib$ of $p$ with $a,b$ tower
elements. At $b$ of equal members, a bracket read, $a$ is a
located root; at $b$ of unequal members the key swap fixes
$p$ and maps the root to its swap image, the division of $p$
at $z^2+(a^2+b^2)=2az$ reads its remainder $Rz+T$ with $Ra+T$ and
$Rb$ of equal members at the two roots, so $R$ and $T$ read equal
members at $b$'s unit witness: a definite divisor. Iterated
divisions close the split.
\end{proof}
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[The located sign is a count]\label{lem:hermitesign}
Over a located stage $K$, let $S$ be squarefree of degree $N$ with
its split over a stored tower
(Lemma~\ref{lem:stagesplit}). A stage value at a root is
a balance pair, its \emph{side} the members' order read, the
\emph{upper} at the first member exceeding and the \emph{lower} at
the second (Definition~\ref{def:ground}); the pairing below is
additive over a value's site, $H_u=H_v+H_p$ at $u=v+p$, so the
weight $p$ is the site datum and the side is its read. The carrier is $S$'s \emph{remainder
lists} (Definition~\ref{def:poly}), length-$N$ coefficient lists
with the convolution's remainder as product, and $M_f$,
the \emph{remainder multiplication} at a polynomial $f$, is the
$N\times N$ matrix whose column $j$ is the remainder of $f\,x^j$.
At a weight $p\in K[x]$ the
\emph{Hermite pairing} $H_p$ is the symmetric form
\[
H_p[i,j]\;=\;\operatorname{tr}M_{x^{i+j}p}\,,\qquad\text{the keys
$i$, $j$ below $N$},
\]
$\operatorname{tr}$ the
matrix trace, every entry one elimination read over $K$
(Definition~\ref{def:elim}).

\emph{(i) The trace adds over the split's blocks.} The split's
factors are coprime ($S$ squarefree), their B\'ezout reads yield
idempotent remainders, $e^2$ and $e\,e'$ reading $e$ and equal members, and
the CRT list is a unit congruence's base change mapping every $M_f$ to
blocks, one per factor, each the factor's own remainder
multiplication; the trace reads one value at every spanning list
($\operatorname{tr}AB=\operatorname{tr}BA$), so it adds over the
blocks: a linear factor's block reads
$f(\varepsilon_i)$, and a definite factor's block reads $f$'s
remainder $\alpha+\beta x$ there at $2\alpha+\beta h$, $M_1$ the
identity and $\operatorname{tr}M_x=h$ at the factor's data $(h,q)$,
its companion trace and constant (Lemma~\ref{lem:stagesplit}).

\emph{(ii) The pairing splits, and a block is one orientation read.}
Cross-block entries are traces of actions by $e\,e'$, whose remainder
is the sum's unit, so the CRT list is a unit congruence of $H_p$ onto the
blocks' forms over the tower, the
reversal count and the rank preserved (Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}, the
rank the kernel block's complement; a stage split is a tower split, so
the tower's count is the stage's own). A linear block at
$\varepsilon_i$ is the one entry $p(\varepsilon_i)$ at a list
member's square: at $p(\varepsilon_i)$ of unequal members it is one reversal exactly where
the value's side is the lower, the reversal count at order one and the
stage's own comparison; at $p$ on the root it is the kernel
block. A definite block, at its factor's data $(h,q)$ and the
\emph{definiteness gap} $g$ at $h^2+g=4q$ (the definite read of
Lemma~\ref{lem:stagesplit}), is $2\times2$, and it reads one unit
per side, the mixed block,
exactly where its mixed entry's square exceeds its diagonal
entries' product (Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}'s $2\times2$ read); it
does so exactly at $p$ off the factor, by one additive identity of
the block's traces at $p$'s remainder $\alpha+\beta x$ there,
\[
4\operatorname{tr}(M_{xp})^2
\;=\;4\operatorname{tr}(M_p)\operatorname{tr}(M_{x^2p})
+\big((2\alpha+h\beta)^2+g\,\beta^2\big)\,g ,
\]
one quadratic-form identity in the remainder (each side quadratic,
$\operatorname{tr}M_{x^kp}$ linear in it), settled at the three
remainders $1$, $x$ and $1+x$ by (i)'s companion rule: the excess is
a square with the gap's multiple of a square, positive by its shape
at a remainder off the sum's unit, and at $p$ on the factor the block is
the kernel block, every entry the sum's unit action's trace.

\emph{(iii) The bracket's pencil pairs the reads, and the counts
close.} At a located root $\varepsilon$ with isolating bracket
$(l,l')$ of balance-pair endpoints off $S$'s roots, its width $w$ at
$l+w=l'$, and $p(\varepsilon)$ of unequal members, the localized read is the form
pair
\[
\mathsf B\;:=\;\big(\,H_{x^2p}+l\,l'\,H_p\ :\ (l+l')\,H_{xp}\,\big),
\]
the bracket's pencil at the three pairings, a balance-pair
coefficient reading cross-added with its second member's multiple on
the pencil's other side (Definition~\ref{def:ground}), its reversal
count and rank its split's (Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}). The three pairings admit
one CRT congruence (the blocks' multiplication matrices are shared),
so the pencil splits per factor. At a linear factor the datum is
$p(\varepsilon_i)$ times the \emph{bracket comparison} at
$\varepsilon_i$, of $\varepsilon_i^{\,2}+l\,l'$ against
$(l+l')\,\varepsilon_i$; the sides' gap is one gap product at every
position, $d\,(w+d)$ for the first side at $t=l'+d$ or $t+d=l$, and
$c\,c'$ for the second at $t=l+c$, $c+c'=w$: above exactly off the
closed bracket, below exactly inside. So the block at $\varepsilon$
reads its one reversal exactly at $p$ on its upper side at
$\varepsilon$, and
at every other located root its reversal read is $p$'s block's own.
At a definite factor the pencil's block is (ii)'s at the shifted
remainder, the kernel block exactly with $p$'s: a common factor with the
bracket's own data would read the sum $g+w^2$ at the sum's unit
at the endpoints' pair
($(l+l')^2$ against $4\,l\,l'$ at the width's square), or a stage
root $t$, whose equality $4t^2+h^2+g=4ht$ reads $d^2+g$ of equal
members at $2t=h+d$
or $2t+d=h$, against the summands' shapes both times; so the shift
is coprime to the factor, its B\'ezout read mapping $p$'s remainder
to the shifted one by a unit. The
bookkeeping closes on two natural identities:
\[
p\ \text{sits on its upper side at $\varepsilon$ exactly at}\quad
\operatorname{rank}H_p+2\operatorname{rev}\mathsf B
\;=\;\operatorname{rank}\mathsf B+2\operatorname{rev}H_p+2 ,
\]
and on its lower exactly at
$\operatorname{rank}H_p+2\operatorname{rev}\mathsf B+2
=\operatorname{rank}\mathsf B+2\operatorname{rev}H_p$: every other
located root at $p$'s support adds one rank to each and equal
reversal reads, every definite factor off $p$ two ranks and one
reversal to each, the kernel blocks off both counts, and $\varepsilon$
adds one rank to each with the pencil's reversal at $p$'s upper side
and $H_p$'s at its lower. At the unit weight and any endpoint pair off $S$'s roots the split
reads the \emph{segment count}: every located root inside the closed
segment adds one reversal to $\mathsf B$ beyond $H_1$'s, every root
outside and every definite factor equal reversals to both, so the
count is the gap at $\operatorname{rev}H_1+\#=\operatorname{rev}
\mathsf B$, the isolating bracket the one-root instance; at a
polynomial off the squarefree site the three pairings read its
distinct roots, the multiplicities entering as positive weights at
the roots' moment folds, so the segment count reads the distinct
roots inside, the squarefree part's own count. With the
entries
elimination data over $K$ and the two counts one split each, a
located sign is finitely many reads of the stage below, descending
to the ground pairs' own comparisons; Jacobi's minor rule is the
read's nonsingular instance (Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}).
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[The deck symbol, and the interior factor]\label{lem:deckfactor}
A polynomial $p$ over a located stage with the \emph{deck
symmetry}, its coefficients equal at every index pair joining to
$2m$ and its keys beyond $2m$ at the sum's unit, and its
unit-monomial coefficient of unequal
members is the display of its \emph{deck symbol}, a stage polynomial
$P$ of degree $m$ in the deck's spectral variable:
\[
p\;=\;\sum_{i+j=m}P_j\;z^{\,i}(z^2+1)^j ,
\]
each deck monomial its own coefficient join ($z^i(z^2+1)^j$ reading
one at the top pair $i+2j$ against $i$, equal ends joining to $2m$),
the map from $P$'s
coefficients to $p$'s top coefficients unitriangular, the symbol one
exact back-substitution, and the involution the identity on the
symbol. A root pair of the deck relation $z^2+1=wz$
(Definition~\ref{def:deck}) at a located
$w$-datum is one quadratic extension (Lemma~\ref{lem:stagesplit}), the two
roots at product one, the constant coefficient, and the
\emph{interior member} is the one at $z^2\le1$, one pair
comparison, strict off the crossing values, the relation's
double-root values at $w^2=4$, the interior margin the crossing
read's own,
$\langle1\!:\!z^2\rangle^2=z^2\,\langle w^2\!:\!4\rangle$, the
relation's square at the sum $z^4+1+4z^2=w^2z^2+2z^2$; a definite $w$-datum's
roots enter over its pair's quadratic extensions, the interior member at
the modulus comparison. The \emph{band read} is $P$'s segment count
at the crossing endpoints: the
bracket pencil of Lemma~\ref{lem:hermitesign} at those endpoints
reads every root of $P$ off the closed crossing segment,
reversal-count reads throughout, with the endpoint reads $P$'s own,
off equal members. At an integer display off the monic carrier the
count runs at the \emph{cleared variable}: the display scales to
the monic at the top's magnitude, each coefficient against the
magnitude's power at its key's gap below the top's predecessor
with the members swapped at a top on its lower side, and the
scaled display reads its roots at the display's own rescaled by
the magnitude, the display's evaluation and the cleared one
composing by formula at the homogeneity principle
(Definition~\ref{def:ground}): the segment count reads at the
crossing endpoints rescaled to the doubled magnitude. With the
band read clear, over $P$'s
split at the stored tower (Lemma~\ref{lem:stagesplit}),
\[
p\;=\;c\ p_{\mathrm{int}}\;p_{\mathrm{ext}}\,,
\]
$p_{\mathrm{int}}$ the monic product over the located interior
members, one per $w$-datum at its multiplicity, degree $m$,
existence by construction, and $p_{\mathrm{ext}}$ the product over
their $\iota$-images, the pairing $z\,z'=1$ per extension: on the
relation each symbol monomial reads $z^i(z^2+1)^j=z^m w^j$, so $p$ is
$z^m$ against the symbol's read at $w$; each split factor
$\langle w\!:\!w_k\rangle$ clears at $z$ to the root pair's
monic quadratic, $z\,\langle w\!:\!w_k\rangle$ reading $z^2+1$
against $w_kz$; so the pair quadratics' product against $c$, the
symbol's leading coefficient and the display's unit-monomial read, is
$p$: the degrees split evenly, $m$ each.
The definite factors are the point: they enter
at their pair data, and every side is Lemma~\ref{lem:hermitesign}'s
count.

\end{lemma}

\begin{theorem}[The pencil is a symmetric pair matrix on every window]
\label{thm:pairpencil}
Every level read the development takes is on a window compression,
finite-dimensional with pair entries
(Proposition~\ref{prop:windowfinite}): $E$ is diagonal in the label index
(Definition~\ref{def:pencil}) and $M$ is multiplication by the involution-fixed
$\sum_p\chi_\theta(U_{\partial p})$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:adjchar}) against the
pairing identity of Proposition~\ref{prop:algebra}, hence symmetric, with
$\mathrm{gram}$ positive definite by construction
(Definition~\ref{def:carrier}). So the pair $(\alpha E:\beta M)$'s
site datum $H([\alpha\!:\!\beta])$
is symmetric there at every end-weight pair, its entries pair data at
pair weights.
\end{theorem}

\section{The chain's invariant form}

\begin{theorem}[The fiber family is a bounded chord pencil]
\label{thm:trigpencil}
At the banded rows, every pencil entry within the locality band of
its support (Lemma~\ref{lem:stableentries}), and the
module--pair coupling $|v|^2=2+t$ at the chord $t$ of the
translation, its deck relation $T^2+1=t\,T$
(Definition~\ref{def:deck}), the
momentum-fiber pencil is a \emph{chord} pencil of
\emph{bounded degree per grade}: the fiber enters each graded block
through finitely many translation powers per direction,
$T_i^{\,j}$ at the direction's own translation, the power bounded
by the grade's spatial support, each transpose-paired read a
deck-family polynomial at the direction's chord
(Definition~\ref{def:deck}): the momentum variables are the
chords $t_1,\dots,t_d$, one per direction, the momentum point $K$
their list (Lemma~\ref{lem:fiberdec}). The chain object is therefore one algebraic
family over the $\big(K;[\alpha\!:\!\beta]\big)$ base at the
chords,
with integral chord data; its divisor records the band, the gap, and every ``assembled'' coefficient as reads at the ends of $\mathcal C$.
Its \emph{fiber symbol} is the family's characteristic Laurent polynomial
$\mathcal S(K;k,r)$, the pair $(k\!:\!A(K))$'s site-datum determinant at the
level $k$ scalar, of finite Newton polytope by the
localities above, with polynomial-pair coefficients in the residue;
$A$ is the momentum fiber of a
symmetric pair pencil (Theorem~\ref{thm:pairpencil}), so a
separation's block is the reversed separation's transpose, the
symbol's own deck symmetry.
\end{theorem}

\begin{lemma}[The window floor fibers over momentum]\label{lem:fiberdec}
On a torus window of odd side $L$, the fibering's family the
\emph{odd tori} at $L\ge5$ and the commuting and chord reads below
at every odd side, the translations
(Construction~\ref{con:lattice}) commute with $H$ ($E$
and $M$ run over all links and plaquettes at coefficient one,
Definition~\ref{def:pencil}): a translation $T$ permutes the label
index isometrically, an orthogonal map, its transpose
the inverse permutation's matrix (Construction~\ref{con:places}), with $T^L=1$
along its direction. Its \emph{chord operator} is
$S:=T+T^{\mathsf T}$, symmetric, commuting with $H$ and with the
gram, and $T$ reads the deck relation at it,
\[
T^2+1\;=\;S\,T ,
\]
the product $T^{\mathsf T}T=1$ collected
(Definition~\ref{def:deck}). The geometric word reads its deck symbol
at the operator,
\[
\sum_{a+b+1=L}T^{\,b}\;=\;T^{\,m}\,P_L(S)
\qquad\text{at}\quad 2m+1=L ,
\qquad P_L:=1+\sum_{j\le m}p_j ,
\]
$P_L$ monic integer of degree $m$ at the deck families, the partial
sums keeping the top at the last family joined
(Definition~\ref{def:deck}; Lemma~\ref{lem:deckfactor}), the powers
matched about the middle collecting at the plus read
$T^{\,j}+(T^{\mathsf T})^{\,j}=p_j(S)$, the root pair
$(T,T^{\mathsf T})$ of product one. Write $X:=T^{\,m}P_L(S)$ for the
word: at $T^L=1$ the word is its own $T$-multiple, $T\,X=X$, and
$T^{\mathsf T}$ against that read gives $T^{\mathsf T}X=X$, so
$S\,X=2\,X$, and the orthogonal $T^{\,m}$ clears at its witness
$(T^{\mathsf T})^{\,m}$:
\[
S\;P_L(S)\;=\;2\,P_L(S) .
\]
Divided at the monic $\langle x\!:\!2\rangle$, $P_L$ leaves its Horner
read there, $P_L=q\,\langle x\!:\!2\rangle+L$
(Definition~\ref{def:poly}): $p_j$ at the chord $2$ reads $2$ at every
key, the recursion's own read from the seeds, so $P_L$ at $2$ reads
$1+2m=L$, the word's value on the \emph{unit fiber}, the fixed
vectors' block: at the datum $D_1$ of $1+D_1=T$ the product
$T^{\mathsf T}T=1$ expands to
$1+D_1+D_1^{\mathsf T}+D_1^{\mathsf T}D_1=1$ and the chord to
$S=2+D_1+D_1^{\mathsf T}$, the two joining at
$D_1^{\mathsf T}D_1+S=2$, so at $S\,v=2\,v$ the read
$\langle D_1v,D_1v\rangle$ is the sum's unit and $D_1v$ with it at
the positive pairing (Definition~\ref{def:carrier}): membership in
the kernel of the pair $(S\!:\!2)$'s site datum is the fixed read
$T\,v=v$. That value is occupied at the odd
side; the division display is the B\'ezout read, and the two factors
of the split polynomial $\langle x\!:\!2\rangle\,P_L(x)$ are coprime.
So the window splits orthogonally into the \emph{chord blocks}: the
kernel of the pair $(S\!:\!2)$'s site datum, its members the reads
$S\,v=2\,v$ at the join, and the kernels of the pair
$(S\!:\!t_j)$'s site data at $P_L$'s roots $t_j$, the display above
reading $\langle x\!:\!2\rangle\,P_L(x)$ an annihilating polynomial
of $S$. The roots are located and the factorization linear: $S$ is
symmetric against the positive gram
(Definition~\ref{def:carrier}), so its site-datum determinant
splits over located roots with a unit congruence to diagonal
(Lemma~\ref{lem:split}), the located linear factorization the
lemma's own display, and $P_L$'s roots are the occurring ones at
that annihilation; each root is separated and bracketed by
ground arithmetic (Theorem~\ref{thm:windowsep}) and located on the
stage (Lemma~\ref{lem:stage}), the tower the split stores
Lemma~\ref{lem:stagesplit}'s. A repeated root descends at the
symmetry, a datum's square read against a member being the image's
pairing against itself, so the distinct roots' linear factors split
the window on their own, coprime at their separation; each kernel is
a spanning list by elimination (Definition~\ref{def:elim}) and the
idempotents are the division displays' witnesses
(Lemma~\ref{lem:hermitesign}'s device, $e^2$ and $e\,e'$ reading $e$
and equal members). $S$ is transpose-closed, so the splitting is
orthogonal with every complement invariant
(Lemma~\ref{lem:orthsplit}), and each block is invariant for $H$ and
for the gram at the commuting. A \emph{fiber} is a chord block. The
count splits with the splitting, the
$\mathrm{LDL}^{\mathsf T}$ of a block diagonal being its blocks' at
the concatenated lists
(Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}). The directions' translations commute,
the action's one composition read
(Construction~\ref{con:lattice}), and a chord operator is the
join of its direction's translation with the complementary
step's, the transpose's own witness, so the chord operators
commute pairwise and each maps every further direction's chord
blocks into themselves; the splitting iterates direction by
direction within the prior steps' blocks, each step this lemma's
own at its direction's operator,
and the window splits orthogonally into the \emph{joint chord
