blocks}: the \emph{momentum point} $K:=(t_1,\dots,t_d)$ is the
chord list, one coordinate per direction, each the chord $2$ or a
located root of $P_L$ along its own direction, the
\emph{$K$-fiber} the joint block at it, and the counts split with
the joint splitting as with each step's
(Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}). The fiber counts reach the directed
windows of Definition~\ref{def:carrier} through the decimated data,
window-free off the unit fiber with the window entering through the
deck brackets on it, every local read transported across windows at the
label-graph isomorphism (Lemma~\ref{lem:stableentries}). This
family runs at odd side $L\ge5$, where the torus's own comparisons
clear its winding states: a winding support occupies at least $L$
links, one at each of its direction's transverse cuts
(Construction~\ref{con:lattice}'s cut family), each reading content
at or beyond the member floor
$\hat c_2(f)$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:casfloor}), so its content is at
least $L\,\hat c_2(f)$ against the plaquette level's
$4\,\hat c_2(f)$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:E0};
Lemma~\ref{lem:freeend}), the one comparison $5>4$ at every member;
so the winding classes read at or beyond the level at every window
of the family, and the directed windows are free of them outright
(Definition~\ref{def:qla}). $\varepsilon_0$ is the least first jump over the fibers, its
designation certificate data (Theorem~\ref{thm:certconstruct}), the unit's
line sitting in the unit fiber, and the window cut of
Definition~\ref{def:K} holds at level $\kappa$ once each
fiber's counting function is flat across
$(\varepsilon_0,\varepsilon_0+E_0\kappa)$, a fiber at the first jump
reading its second jump at or beyond the top and every other fiber $k$
its first,
\[
\operatorname{rev}\big(H^{(k)}:(h+E_0\kappa)\,G^{(k)}\big)\;=\;0
\]
at a pair $h\ge\varepsilon_0$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:certconstruct}):
together the reads are
$\mathrm{spec}(H)\subset\{\varepsilon_0\}\cup
[\varepsilon_0+E_0\kappa,\infty)$ on the window, the cut at every
window ground at once (Lemma~\ref{lem:speccut}). On a chord block at
a located root $t$ of $P_L$, $S$ reads the root itself and $T$ the
deck relation at the located \emph{chord}, $T^2+1=t\,T$; the
transpose-paired reads collect at the deck families,
$T^{\,b}+(T^{\mathsf T})^{\,b}=p_b(t)$ on the block, the plus read's
instance at the root pair (Definition~\ref{def:deck}), so every such

datum $A$ at $T=T^{\mathsf T}+A$ squares against the band read,
$A^2+4=S^2$ at the products $T\,T^{\mathsf T}=1$ and
$T^{\mathsf T}T=1$ collected, hence
$A^2+4=t^2$ on the block. $P_L$'s roots sit interior to the band, and
the block's own pairing reads that: transposing $T=T^{\mathsf T}+A$

$\langle Av,Av\rangle$; the block read against $v$ is then
\[
t^2\,\langle v,v\rangle+\langle Av,Av\rangle
\;=\;4\,\langle v,v\rangle ,
\]
the pair $\langle t^2\!:\!4\rangle$ at its lower side or at equal

$\langle Av,Av\rangle$ is the sum's unit at every member, $Av$ with it
at that pairing, so $T=T^{\mathsf T}$ on the block and $T^2=1$ at the
product $T\,T^{\mathsf T}=1$; $T^L=1$ at odd $L$ then reads
$T=T\,(T^2)^{\,m}=T^{\,L}=1$ at $2m+1=L$, the coprimality of $2$ and
$L$ spelled as that word, and $S=2$ on the whole block, so $t$ reads
$2$ against $S\,v=t\,v$ there, against $t$ a root of $P_L$ and the
two factors coprime at the division display above ($P_L$ at $2$
reading the occupied $L$). So every root of $P_L$ reads $t^2$ on its
lower side against $4$, strictly interior, $A^2$ reads the pair
$\langle t^2\!:\!4\rangle$ off equal members, $A$'s determinant with
it, and the \emph{band root} $s$ at $4s^2+t^2=4$ adjoins one
quadratic extension (Lemma~\ref{lem:stagesplit}), that read cleared.
An antisymmetric matrix of odd order reads its determinant at its own

(Construction~\ref{con:places}'s swap grading): the block's order is

The deck families hold the doubling's own identity. At a root pair
$(z,z')$ of the relation $z^2+1=x\,z$ at a free chord $x$, product

read (Definition~\ref{def:deck}). The geometric telescope is

\[
z^2W^2+2zW+1\;=\;W^2+2z^{\,L}W+z^{2L} .
\]
Join $W^2+1$ to both members: the relation collects the left to

p_L(x)\;=\;\langle x\!:\!2\rangle\;P_L(x)^2+2 ,
\]
one identity of the deck families, the chord free over the base and
both members its polynomials, every step remainder-list arithmetic at
the relation (Definition~\ref{def:poly}; Definition~\ref{def:deck}).
The partial sums read the telescope additively, one join per step
from the seed,
\[
\langle x\!:\!2\rangle\,P_m+p_m\;=\;p_{m+1}\ ,
\]
and the key list's fold collects to the symbol's own multiple,
\[
\sum_{b<L}p_b\;=\;p_m\,P_L\ ,
\]
one induction per side: the step joins the two new keys at the
telescope, the doubling display, and the products' square
$p_{m+1}\,p_{m+1}=p_{2m+2}+2$, its three collectors.

Per free translation orbit the doubling is witnessed and squeezed.
Take a free orbit of the label index, its $L$ translates $T^{\,b}w$
independent, and at a root $t_j$ the deck-family column
\[
u_j\;:=\;\sum_{b<L}p_b(t_j)\;T^{\,b}w .
\]
The word's read at the root pair of the located chord $t_j$ has
$W=z^{\,m}P_L(t_j)$ the sum's unit at $P_L(t_j)$'s, so the telescope
reads $z^{\,L}=1$: the powers wrap, $p_{L+b}(t_j)=p_b(t_j)$ and
$p_{L-b}(t_j)=p_b(t_j)$, with $p_L(t_j)=2$ the displayed identity's
read at the located root. Hence $S\,u_j=t_j\,u_j$: the coefficient of
$T^{\,c}w$ in $S\,u_j$ is the family join
$p_{c+1}(t_j)+p_{c-1}(t_j)=t_j\,p_c(t_j)$
(Definition~\ref{def:deck}), the recursion closing cyclically, its
two wrapped keys the reads $p_{L-1}(t_j)=p_1(t_j)$ and
$p_{L-2}(t_j)=p_2(t_j)$ against the seed $p_0=2$. The join is the
recursion's class read at the symbol: over the remainder lists at
$P_L$ (Definition~\ref{def:poly}) the column's coordinates are the
families' own classes, the chord multiplies as the free chord, and
the wrapped keys read the wrap identities, $p_L=2$ with
$p_{L-b}=p_b$, one read at every side at once, a located root's
reads the classes' evaluations, and a polynomial in the chord
reads the column at its own multiple, the membership iterated
along the Horner recursion (Definition~\ref{def:poly}). That seed against
the independent translates reads $u_j$ occupied. Its $A$-image is its
independent partner at the pairing reads: $\langle u_j,A\,u_j\rangle$
reads its own memberwise swap at $A^{\mathsf T}=\check A$, hence
equal members, and $\langle A\,u_j,A\,u_j\rangle$ reads
$\langle4\!:\!t_j^{\,2}\rangle\,\langle u_j,u_j\rangle$ on its upper
side at the strict interiority above, so $A\,u_j$ is occupied and
perpendicular to $u_j$, the two independent members of the root
block: the membership is the exchange's read, the datum and the
chord two words in the one translation at the product
$T^{\mathsf T}T=1$, so they exchange with every polynomial in the
chord across it, the datum maps each chord block to itself, and
the image row reads the moved display,
$S\,A\,u_j=A\,S\,u_j=t_j\,A\,u_j$, the symbol's read at the image
the member's own image. The independence reads over the remainder lists at every
side at once. The column's self-pairing collects at the product
families, $p_b^{\,2}=p_{2b}+2$, the doubled keys reading the key
list once (the even keys outright, the odd keys at the upper wrap),
and the key list's fold is the symbol's own multiple, the display
above, so the self-pairing reads $2L$; the
image's
self-pairing reads $\langle4\!:\!x^{2}\rangle$ against it at the
relation on the column, and the pair's Gram determinant reads
$4L^{2}\,\langle4\!:\!x^{2}\rangle$. At the band's two ends the
symbol divides at the linear factors: at the chord two the
remainder is $L$, the division display above, and at the partner

the even keys and its partner at the odd, so the remainder is the
unit at even depth and its partner at odd; the two cofactors'
product is the determinant's witness: the band pair is the two
linear factors' product's balance partner, each end's factored
read is its remainder's partner at the division display, and the
three partners compose, the cleared read $4L^{3}$ at the
balance partner of the depth's parity member, occupied at every side, so
the pair is independent over the remainder lists, one read per
side, the located roots' reads the evaluations; and the composed
witness reads the band at every stored factor besides: an
occupied-length factor of the symbol dividing the band pair
$\langle4\!:\!x^{2}\rangle$ divides the composed constant through
the identity, the division's descent at the monic factor against
the constant's occupied read, so the band symbol's remainder at
every stored factor is occupied, the equal-members case refused at
every stored root with the block pairing's lower side closing the
strict interiority above. The unit fiber reads one member of the orbit, its fixed read
$T\,v=v$ forcing equal coefficients along the orbit at the
elimination read (Definition~\ref{def:elim}), the orbit's own sum.
The orbit's count $1+2m=L$ squeezes the reads to equality: each root
block reads exactly two members of the orbit, the pair
$\big(u_j,A\,u_j\big)$, the block at doubled multiplicity, and per
fiber the classes are the free orbits at the \emph{pair base}, each
member against its $A$-image: the row structure the gradings below
read off this splitting. Over the residue the squeeze is one
splitting read at stated lists: the pair base enters as the
column's coefficient rows, one row per key below the residue's top
against its image under the doubling datum, and the orbit sum
joins them to an independent list at the orbit's own count. The
sum pairs every row at the sum's unit, against a coefficient row
at the key's coefficient of the key list's fold, the symbol's own
multiple at the residue, and against an image row through the
datum's memberwise-swap transpose at the sum's own image, the
fixed read's unit; a coefficient row pairs an image row at two

exchange and the wrap's reflection returning the exchange: equal

the residue's top, the families there their own below-top displays
at monic tops from the seed two, a dependency reading its
coefficients at the unit key by key up the tops; an image-row
dependency is the doubling datum's read at a coefficient-row
combination, the combination's two translation images at one
value, the orthogonality doubling the iterate's fix and the
member reading the translation through the wrap,
$T\,w=T^{\,2m+2}\,w=(T^2)^{\,m+1}\,w=w$ at $T^{L}=1$ over the
count,
so the combination sits in the unit fiber's kernel and joins the
sum's span while the sum pairs it at the sum's unit, its
self-pairing reading that unit with the positive pairing
withdrawing it; and a joined
dependency's sum part reads its own square at the count with each
block part its self-pairing against the whole, the positive
pairing withdrawing both. The joined list's count is the orbit's
own, $1+2m=L$, so every orbit vector reads through the splitting
at the elimination read, a root-datum kernel member through the
pairs alone, its sum coordinate clearing at the word's value $L$
off the unit, the division display's read.

The \emph{band congruence} takes the
block's list at pairs, each member against its $A$-image at the

crossings, mapping the block's
entries to polynomials in $t$ and $s^2$, substituting at the root's
identity, every count fixed (Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}) and the
gram's rescaled block $s^2G$ positive definite:
the fiber pencils' entries are one polynomial-pair list in

$\tau^2$
(Definition~\ref{def:pencil}): each fiber floor is
Lemma~\ref{lem:cellcount}'s count, a
cell function of $(t,\tau)$, constant off the root divisor of one explicit
determinant of ground data and persisting at the divisor's points beside a positive-semidefinite cell. At fixed content cutoff the entries are one polynomial-pair list in
$(t,\tau)$ over the residue at every $L$ beyond the locality band
(Lemma~\ref{lem:stableentries}), the window entering through its sampled
momenta and its separation range alone. The momenta are the
geometric word's symbol roots, the word identity above read at the
operator: the sampled momenta are the chord $2$, the unit fiber,
with $P_L$'s $m$ located
roots, one doubled block each, every root a designated bracket
datum at the
separation of Theorem~\ref{thm:windowsep}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[The fiber index is its grading, and the pencil is
bordered]\label{lem:grading}
Per fiber on the odd tori (Lemma~\ref{lem:fiberdec}) the configuration
classes read two counts off the index
(Proposition~\ref{prop:leastwindow}): the \emph{band components}, the
components of the class's support at adjacency within the locality band
(Lemma~\ref{lem:stableentries}), each with its content; and the
\emph{separation profile}, the band components' relative positions up
to translation. The grading is total, a class being its datum, and the
regions are the datum's own coordinate ranges: the \emph{head}, every
content below a head bound $\Lambda_h$ (a parameter of the
presentation, as a window's cutoff is) and every separation within the
band; the \emph{tower} direction, a band component's content beyond;
and the \emph{slab} direction, a separation beyond the locality band, the
repeating shape of Lemma~\ref{lem:greenprod}. The pencil's rows read
the grading in three shapes: the diagonal, the stencil rows, and the
border rows, and the tower rows repeat off the walls' collar:
\[
N^{\lambda+\nu}_{\theta\lambda}\;=\;\mathrm{mult}_\theta(\nu)
\qquad\text{at every }\lambda\ \text{whose consecutive gaps are at
least two,}
\]
one row, the adjoint's content list, the collar the labels with a
consecutive gap below two; at a member's table the row reads the
display at every dominant $\lambda$ whose simple coroot pairs sit
at or beyond the member's largest coroot magnitude, two
off $G_2$ and three at it, the contents the member's $\theta$ list
(Proposition~\ref{prop:row}), the collar the labels below that
clearance.
\begin{proof}
\emph{Totality} is the index's: a class's support decomposes into its
band components with contents and relative positions, counts off the
index (Proposition~\ref{prop:leastwindow}), and the regions are ranges
of that datum. A band component below content $C$ with $L$
occupied links reads $L\,\hat c_2(f)\le C$, one pair comparison
(Lemma~\ref{lem:casfloor}), and as many within-band gaps, so its
diameter is priced by $C$ and the head is a finite index
(Proposition~\ref{prop:windowfinite}).

\emph{The rows.} $E$ is diagonal at the content sum
(Definition~\ref{def:pencil}). A plaquette term meeting a band
component's neighborhood reads its changed edge there
(Lemma~\ref{lem:stableentries}), the target's content within the
fusion stencil (Proposition~\ref{prop:fusionfinite},
Proposition~\ref{prop:leastwindow}) and its support within the locality band
(Lemma~\ref{lem:stableentries}), band components joining or parting within
one locality-band step: a \emph{stencil row}. A plaquette term off every band
component's neighborhood factorizes (Lemma~\ref{lem:stableentries}),
creating its own loop class, one new band component, at one entry per
relative position beyond the locality band, a label-graph isomorphism
transporting it: a \emph{border row}, one repeated datum, the boundary coupling of
the walk's shape (\S\ref{sec:channel}). Every entry is one
polynomial-pair list in $(t,\tau)$ over the residue beyond the locality band
and the reach bound
(Lemma~\ref{lem:fiberdec}; Lemma~\ref{lem:rankstable}(iii)), at the
root coordinate's magnetic weight $\tau^2$.

\emph{The tower rows repeat.} At $\lambda$ with consecutive gaps at
least two, every permutation but the identity drops from the graded
sum (Corollary~\ref{cor:steinberg}): a contribution at $\sigma$ reads
$\nu_\sigma+\sigma(\lambda+u)=c+u$ at an adjoint content
$\nu_\sigma$, a
letter-pair move or the unit-monomial content
(Lemma~\ref{lem:adjchar}), $u$ the unit set's sorted display, the
matched-degree tie's one full column $\sigma$-fixed and absorbed
into the label class (Construction~\ref{con:labels};
Lemma~\ref{lem:channels}'s shift the unabsorbed spelling); at
gaps of
$\lambda+u$ at least three the content's move sits below the
entries' separations, so the join
$\sigma(\lambda+u)+\nu_\sigma$ at $\sigma$ off the identity keeps
a reversed pair, which the strictly decreasing $c+u$ misses at
every target. The
identity term reads
$\mathrm{mult}_\theta(\nu)$ at the tie $\lambda+\nu=c$, on the even
side, the display; every
target $\lambda+\nu$ is dominant there, the gaps absorbing one box.
The walk's own diagonal sits on the collar and differs, $2$ against
$r$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:row}): the collar is a named label set, its
labels' gap list the read.

\emph{The member rows.} At a member's table the deep exclusion
is the coroot pairing's: a contribution at $w$ off the identity
holds an
inversion list's member $\gamma$, $w\beta=\check\gamma$ at a positive
$\beta$, and the tie reads $w(\lambda+\rho)+\nu_w=\mu+\rho$ at
the target $\mu$ and an adjoint content $\nu_w$; pairing the tie
at $\gamma$'s coroot moves the image across the kept form,
$w^{-1}\gamma=\check\beta$ at the transport, and collects
\[
(\lambda+\rho)(\beta^\vee)+(\mu+\rho)(\gamma^\vee)
\;=\;\nu_w(\gamma^\vee)\ .
\]
The right side sits at the member's largest coroot magnitude, the
string's own cap (Lemma~\ref{lem:strings}); the left summands
read the simple pairs at the roots' natural coroot folds, so at
the stated clearance the first sits beyond the magnitude and the
second beyond the sum's unit, the target's pair the base's joined
to one content move: the join clears the cap by two, the tie
refused off the identity. The identity's
term reads the display, the deep pairs absorbing each move at
the stated clearance: the
member rows at simple pairs at or beyond it are the $\theta$
list's own, Proposition~\ref{prop:row}'s content data.
\end{proof}

\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[The separated sector fibers over relative momentum]
\label{lem:relfiber}
Per fiber on the odd tori (Lemma~\ref{lem:fiberdec}), at every $d\ge1$:

\emph{(i) Beyond the locality band the components factorize.} At a class of
$\varrho\ge2$ band components with every separation beyond the locality band,
the pencil's rows are per component: $E$ adds the contents, a plaquette
term meets at most one component's neighborhood
(Lemma~\ref{lem:stableentries}), a stencil
move moves one component within its own locality band with the others
spectators, and every entry is the moved component's own read at one
relative position (Lemma~\ref{lem:grading}). The $\varrho$-sector's pencil
therefore agrees, beyond the \emph{collar} (the classes with some
separation within the locality band, a finite border block of the head,
Proposition~\ref{prop:windowfinite}), with the components' tensor-sum
pencil extended over every relative position,
\[
H^{(\varrho)}\;=\;\sum_{a+b+1=\varrho}G^{\otimes a}\otimes H^{(1)}\otimes
G^{\otimes b}
\quad\text{against}\quad G^{\otimes \varrho} ,
\]
the fold over the factor splittings at the unit power the scalars'
line,
$H^{(1)}$ the single-component sector's intensive pencil at
Lemma~\ref{lem:grading}'s index, the sector's own pencil the join of
the vacuum diagonal with it, and the two pencils' site datum
supported on the
collar.

\emph{(ii) The relative fibering.} The extended pencil's entries read
the relative positions through translation differences alone, so the
relative translations act on it and it block-diagonalizes over their
chord operators by
Lemma~\ref{lem:fiberdec}'s chord splitting, run here at the relative
translations: their blocks
read at the chord coordinate (Lemma~\ref{lem:fiberdec}), and the
\emph{relative momenta}, the sampled chord values, are the chord $2$
with the geometric word's symbol roots, each
root a designated bracket datum at the separation of
Theorem~\ref{thm:windowsep}.

\emph{(iii) The separated bottom adds, and the sector clears the
bracket.} A single-component head pair positive semidefinite at
the level $\ell$ is the form floor $H^{(1)}\succeq\ell\,G$
(Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}), with its site datum $S_1$ at
$H^{(1)}=\ell\,G+S_1$,
and the tensor sum reads it $\varrho$ times:
\[
H^{(\varrho)}
\;=\;\varrho\,\ell\ G^{\otimes \varrho}
+\sum_{a+b+1=\varrho}G^{\otimes a}\otimes S_1
\otimes G^{\otimes b}\, ,
\]
each summand positive semidefinite against the positive-definite
tensor Gram: a product of two tensors reads factorwise, the product
index's fold collecting to the factors' folds' product at the ground
identities (Definition~\ref{def:ground}), so the gram's pivot
congruence at its positive pivots, the split at order-one blocks a
positive-definite datum reads off its leading minors of unequal
members (Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}'s minor sequence), and the site
datum's split compose
to the summand's own congruence, its image the split's blocks at the
pivots' positive scales, and the image's form at every vector is the
fold of the blocks' reads at the scales, on its upper side or of
equal members (Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}); the multiset symmetry and
the window compress it, a compression
keeping the split (Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}). So on a bracket
with top at or below $\varrho\,\ell$ the extended sector's pair is
positive semidefinite at every relative fiber, the collar is a head border, and the border rows
between component counts are the walk-shaped border chain:
the separated sector contributes its collar to the head and its jumps
sit beyond the bracket, the comparison's validity divisor the
single-component level's own, located per cell
(Lemma~\ref{lem:cellcount}). At $d\ge2$ the clearance is the slab direction's step, the
relative coordinates fibered and the comparison
Lemma~\ref{lem:cellcount}'s cell read, per relative fiber the
single-component level's own, so the direction count enters
through the fiber list alone (Lemma~\ref{lem:fiberdec}).

\end{lemma}

\begin{theorem}[The decimation: the counts live on the decimated head]
\label{thm:decimation}
Fix a fiber, its grading (Lemma~\ref{lem:grading}), a head bound
$\Lambda_h$, and a pair bracket $[\ell_1,\ell_2]$ of spectral
levels. The pencil decimates onto its head in three located
steps, each step a Schur complement
(Lemma~\ref{lem:greenprod}; Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}'s deflation),
and the decimation is the count's own presentation.

\emph{(i) The border direction is the walk's shape.} A pencil row moves
the band-component count within a named locality band, a border row creating one
component and a stencil move severing or joining at most its own
plaquette's arms (Lemma~\ref{lem:grading}), so over that count,
re-blocked along it, the pencil is a half-line chain of sectors
with the vacuum class at the boundary, and the count at every level
splits over the chain's pivot recursion,
$\operatorname{rev}=\sum_\kappa\operatorname{rev}(X_\kappa)$
(Lemma~\ref{lem:greenprod}(i),(ii)): the walk's shape at matrix blocks
(\S\ref{sec:channel}), the boundary pivot the vacuum's dressing. The
split is order-free: inertia adds at every nonsingular principal pivot
(Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}), so each slab ordering of the elimination reads
the one count, and an ordering is certificate data, its validity divisors
entering the emitted list as any boundary's do. The
ground jump is a divisor point of the decimated head: the level is the
distance of the first two jumps of one counting function, the walk's own
read, its boundary defect the vacuum's diagonal, the Kronecker delta
at two labels (Proposition~\ref{prop:row}).

\emph{(ii) The tower tails decimate on their dominance cells.} A class's
diagonal is intensive, the far plaquettes reading equal members on it
(Lemma~\ref{lem:stableentries}): the content sum against the local
magnetic stencil, the stencil rows one capped list
(Lemma~\ref{lem:grading}; Lemmas~\ref{lem:fpcap},~\ref{lem:loopcap}).
Beyond the head bound the tail block at the bracket reads its
\emph{dominance comparison}: per row, the off-row entries' magnitude
fold sits at or below the diagonal's entry, $f_i\le a_{ii}$ at $f_i$
the fold (Theorem~\ref{thm:windowsep}'s magnitude carrier), the
comparison's gaps $d_i$ at $f_i+d_i=a_{ii}$ its witnesses.  Wherever
it holds the peel telescopes on the comparison: at the symmetric
datum (Theorem~\ref{thm:pairpencil}) a vector's form peels at the
leading row, the leading square with the doubled cross fold against
the trailing block's form, and each cross term joins its magnitude's
squares at one square's multiple,
\[
2\,a\,xy+|a|\,(x^2+y^2)\;=\;|a|\,(x+y)^2\ \ \text{at $a$ its own
magnitude, and}\ \ |a|\,\langle x\!:\!y\rangle^2\ \text{at the swap,}
\]
at or beyond the sum's unit by its shape, so the leading row's
magnitudes withdraw from the trailing rows' folds into their gaps
and the descent closes on the order: the form sits at or beyond the
gaps' fold of squares $\sum_id_i\,x_i^2$ at every vector.  Every
split of the block then reads the upper side throughout, its lower
list at an occupied count naming a vector pricing the form strictly
below the sum's unit against the fold (Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}); at
occupied gaps the kernel block is vacant, a kernel column's image,
off the unit family at the congruence witness's clearing, reading
its form at equal members against the strict floor
(Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}), so the determinant sits off equal members
with located brackets and the adjugate its solve
(Definition~\ref{def:elim}); and the count telescopes over the pivot
recursion at the slab fold, the tail's summands each the sum's unit
(Lemma~\ref{lem:greenprod}(ii)): at the $\alpha{=}1$ representative the
comparison reads the content's growth against the stencil caps, the
caps' fold linear in the stencil's term count, at most the
per-vertex plaquette count's fold over the support's vertices
(Construction~\ref{con:lattice};
Lemmas~\ref{lem:fpcap},~\ref{lem:loopcap} the per-term caps), at the
$[\,\sigma:1\,]$ representative it is Lemma~\ref{lem:dualtrunc}'s read
toward the contact end, and each
comparison's validity is a located region of $(\tau,\ell,\Lambda_h)$
at the count's coordinate,
its divisor the comparison's own, the leading side in the count the
one linear read. The constant-coefficient instance is
the walk's boundary recursion, the deck quadratic's interior root
(Theorem~\ref{thm:channeldiv}).

\emph{(iii) The slabs decimate at the deck pivot, and the window enters
through its brackets alone.} Beyond the locality band the slab direction's bulk
datum repeats (Lemma~\ref{lem:grading}); its pivot is the
deck-interior solvent of the pivot identities
(Lemma~\ref{lem:spectator}(iii); Lemma~\ref{lem:deckfactor}), the
deck orbit's interior member at $m=1$,
Theorem~\ref{thm:channeldiv}'s read,
valid exactly off the bulk block's own band, the crossing locus of its
deck relation, and at a graph read of unequal members
(Lemma~\ref{lem:spectator}(iii)): the decimation's validity divisors there
are the tail's band and the graph read's side, located data
of the bulk block alone. At
$d\ge2$ the slab step is Lemma~\ref{lem:relfiber}'s clearance, the
relative coordinates fibered and the deck pivot the $d=1$ sharpening.
Two windows share every decimated datum up to their lengths, their reads
differing within the named geometric brackets
(Lemma~\ref{lem:spectator}(i),(ii)): off the unit fiber the decimated
symbol is window-free outright, and on it the window enters through
the brackets alone.

\emph{The decimated symbol.} On the bracket, within the validity cells of
(ii) and (iii), the count of the full fiber pencil is the decimated
head's: a finite symmetric pencil over the located stage
(Lemma~\ref{lem:stage}), entries pair data of the deck
coordinates and the located pivots, cell functions over $(t,\tau,r)$
(Lemma~\ref{lem:cellcount}; Lemma~\ref{lem:rankstable}(iii)). The
spectrum on the bracket is where the decimated determinant reads equal
members,
Theorem~\ref{thm:divisorid} per cell concrete; and the cells the
validity divisors cover are the decimation's own output, every boundary a
located root.

\emph{The divisor list, named.} The decimation's validity divisors are three
families: the \emph{dominance divisors}, the tower comparisons' own,
one per head bound and representative ((ii)); the \emph{deck divisors},
the bulk blocks' crossing loci with the graph read's side,
Lemma~\ref{lem:spectator}(iii)'s
counts ((iii)); and the \emph{jump divisors}, the decimated determinant's
roots at the bracket's levels (Lemma~\ref{lem:cellcount}). The list is
finite per stage, its cells partition the base, counts constant per
cell and persisting at a boundary beside a positive-semidefinite
cell (Lemma~\ref{lem:cellcount}); the emitted record, the located root
list with its per-cell counts and the binding read of which comparison
binds at each boundary, is the decimation's displayed object, every entry one
stage read of the crossing divisor (Theorem~\ref{thm:divisorid}).

\end{theorem}

\begin{lemma}[The charged cells]\label{lem:chargedcell}
Fix a fiber on the odd tori (Lemma~\ref{lem:fiberdec}). The
\emph{$d_f$-ality} of a label is its chain endpoint, the fundamental
or the unit label Lemma~\ref{lem:casfloor}'s descent ends at, one
value at every chain: a chain's moves fix the reduced degree and
each re-reduction withdraws one full column
(Construction~\ref{con:labels}), so a chain to the unit label reads
the label's degree at full columns outright, $|\lambda|=m\,d_f$, and
one to $\omega_j$ at the two-sided join
$|\lambda|+m'\,d_f=j+m\,d_f$ at naturals $m$, $m'$; two endpoints'
joins
against one $|\lambda|$ force one index by the sum's injectivity,
a positive gap of full columns reaching $d_f$ against the indices at
most $r$, and the fundamentals' degrees are their indices. Two
labels are one \emph{class} where their degrees join to full
columns, $|\lambda|+m\,d_f=|\mu|+m'\,d_f$ at naturals $m$, $m'$,
the endpoint the class's one fundamental or the unit; the dual's
endpoint is the complement at the join $a+a'=d_f$ at nonunit
endpoints and the unit at the unit's, the dual's
degree joining the label's to full columns,
$|\lambda^*|+|\lambda|=d_f\,\lambda_1$.

\emph{(i) Charge is a cut-free class, and the pencil grades by it.} At an
occupied configuration every touched vertex reads an invariant count

(Definition~\ref{def:carrier}): the unit label is a constituent, a

block's
contents sum to its degree, Construction~\ref{con:places};
Construction~\ref{con:labels}), and the incoming duals read across
at the join $|\lambda^*|+|\lambda|=d_f\,\lambda_1$: the \emph{vertex
law}, the outgoing against the incoming degree folds at the unit
class,
\[
\sum_{\mathrm{out}}|a_\ell|+m\,d_f
\;=\;\sum_{\mathrm{in}}|a_\ell|+m'\,d_f
\qquad\text{at naturals }m,\ m'.
\]
Summing the vertex laws over the region between two parallel
transverse cuts, an interior link's degree enters both folds once
and the sum's injectivity reads it off: the two cuts' pairs are one

endpoint the class's read. A cut transverse to direction $i$ is
crossed by direction-$i$ links alone
(Construction~\ref{con:lattice}'s direction reads), so the
directions' classes are
independent data, and the \emph{charge} $c$ is the occupancy family
over the directions at the flux endpoints, a direction occupied at a

table the law is class additivity: a block's occupied contents

label's to the unit class, so the vertex law reads at the

$\omega_5$ and $\omega_3$ with $\omega_6$, and two at $E_7$, the
key-two remainders occupied at $\omega_2$, $\omega_5$ and
$\omega_7$. At a
member whose class group is its unit alone every configuration

$E$ is diagonal; $M$ fuses one link by $\theta$, whose degree is $d_f$
(the reduced shape $(2,1^{\,u})$ at $u+2=d_f$), fixing every link's
$d_f$-ality; a
translation permutes the index at one charge, the relabeled

\emph{(ii) The winding floor, attained.} Every label reads

family

endpoints' floors tie exactly at the dual pair, the sum's

At a member's table the winding floor per class is the class's

The floor is attained by the \emph{winding strings}: one straight

\emph{(iii) At bounded excess the charged head is the dressed string,
and its pencil is one window-free head at a length shift.} Write the

winding floor with the excess joining to the content, the grading the charged decimation reads. The
exclusions are extensive, read per cut, each cut's price

table, (ii)'s floors with their subadditivity folds; the

reads below, one trichotomy fold over the member's displayed

below and beyond costs $2\,a_1a_2$
and $2\,g_1g_2$ at the complement gaps $a_i+g_i=d_f$, each at least $2$, the
parts nonunit, the drop case at the dropped pair's
own floors, $2\,w(a)$ at or beyond $2r$, and the unit conversion at
$\hat c_2(\omega_j)=[\,j\,g_j\,(r+2):2d_f^{\,2}\,]$ at $j+g_j=d_f$
(Lemma~\ref{lem:casfloor}), each at or above
$[\,r+2:d_f^{\,2}\,]$, the instance. A
cut crossed beyond its net class prices the excess at its extra
crossings' own floors: an extra crossing at the unit class and a

nonunit read, the chain keeping the class and ending at the unit
through a dominant root's content or on a unit-class end content
(Lemma~\ref{lem:casfloor}; (ii)): $\theta$'s read one at the
$A$-series, at $D_\ell$, and at $E_6$ and $E_7$ (the unit-class

to the unit class holds two members at their classes' floors, at
or above twice the least class floor, the $A$-series' fundamental

off every charged direction's cuts reads the member's floor
outright (Lemma~\ref{lem:casfloor}), the $A$-series'
$\hat c_2(f)$.

their one net crossing number at most
$L_0$, the least natural at $\varpi_0\,L_0\ge\Lambda_h$, one

threshold the head's classes are the \emph{dressed string}: the winding

within the bound the excess floors above set, each crossing one more

entry a finite stencil sum at translation powers: beyond $L_0$ and the
locality band (Lemma~\ref{lem:stableentries}) the off-diagonal entries and

residue, the label-graph isomorphism
of dressed neighborhoods transporting them along the string and across

at the $\alpha{=}1$ representative is the electric read alone,
\[
\varpi\;:=\;\sum_{i\ \mathrm{charged}}\hat c_2(\omega_{c_i})\ ,
\]
window-free and coupling-free, the charged head pencil is
\[
H_c\;=\;L\,\varpi\,G\;+\;H_{\mathrm{int}} ,
\]
$H_{\mathrm{int}}$ the window-free intensive head at the stencil
rows, and a charged count
at a level $\lambda$ is $H_{\mathrm{int}}$'s count at the shift

length enters the charged head through the shift, the
sampled momenta and the separation range alone. The magnetic datum
at a far cell is its \emph{dressing fold}, the tower decimation's

one adjugate solve per pivot (Theorem~\ref{thm:decimation}(ii)),
each atom read at the entry's square against its gap,
$[\,e^{2}:g\,]$, the pivot's cofactor at the coupling's two reads,
entering the dressed diagonal at the squared magnetic weight,
$[\,p^4:q^2\,]$ on the ray at $[\,q^2:p^2\,]$ and $\tau^4$ at the
$\alpha{=}1$ representative: its atoms are the stencil's
changed-edge entries (Proposition~\ref{prop:repring}) at their
content gaps, a link-sharing plaquette at the row targets $\mu$ of

(Lemma~\ref{lem:rankstable}(ii); Lemma~\ref{lem:serstable}(ii)
at the further series), at the content pair

monotone in its level, so the flat step's charged comparison rides the

\[
\Big\langle\,\varpi+\sum_{i\ \mathrm{charged}}D^{\mathrm{vac}}_i
\,:\,\sum_{i\ \mathrm{charged}}D^{\mathrm{str}}_i\,\Big\rangle ,
\]

and $D^{\mathrm{vac}}_i$ the unit class's at the matched slab, its

(Construction~\ref{con:xfusion}; Proposition~\ref{prop:E0}), the far

residue as a coordinate with its roots against the compared

(Construction~\ref{con:lattice}), both members linear in the term

and count.

\emph{(iv) The charged decimation, and the cell.} The grading and the decimation
run per charge: every pencil row preserves the charge ((i)), so each

one charge sector, read there; beyond $L_0$ the charged index is the

against the charged diagonal's floor (ii), the head bound a parameter of

\begin{theorem}[The cut is a flat step of the decimated counting
function]\label{thm:flatstep}
On a coupling cell, within the decimation's validity cells
(Theorem~\ref{thm:decimation}), the window cut of Definition~\ref{def:K} at level
$\kappa$ is reached by located jump comparisons on the decimated symbols:
per fiber and per charge (the $d_f$-ality class of
Lemma~\ref{lem:chargedcell}(i), $M$ of the unit charge class), the
counting
function's first jump sits at or above $\varepsilon_0+E_0\kappa$, and on the vacuum's
own sector its second jump does, the first the window's
$\varepsilon_0$, the boundary pivot's own root
(Theorem~\ref{thm:decimation}(i)). Each comparison is two counts at located
pairs: the jumps are count data on the decimated head, cell functions
of $(t,\tau,r)$ with located boundaries (Lemma~\ref{lem:cellcount};
Theorem~\ref{thm:decimation}), the momenta read cell by cell at one
bracket pair each (Lemma~\ref{lem:fiberdec}), the charged sectors' counts the reads
of Lemma~\ref{lem:chargedcell}, and the contact pair's value the
$X$-sector's read (Definition~\ref{def:contactpair};
Remark~\ref{rem:certtarget}).

\emph{The comparisons reach the cut.} They read
$\mathrm{spec}(H)\subset\{\varepsilon_0\}\cup
[\varepsilon_0+E_0\kappa,\infty)$ on the window, the cut at that
window at every ground (Lemma~\ref{lem:speccut}).
Window-freeness is the decimation's, off the
unit fiber outright and within the deck brackets on it
(Theorem~\ref{thm:decimation}(iii)). Per cell the verification is a finite
list of anchored jump brackets, one pair of counts each: the cells'
reads are the gap chain's data, each level naming its representative and
transported across the seam at the factors of
Lemma~\ref{lem:dualtrunc}'s identity.

\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[The ground reads are bracketed]\label{thm:groundreads}
Fix a window at a coupling with the ground bracket of
Theorem~\ref{thm:SO} and the slab split of
Lemma~\ref{lem:greenprod} along the decimation's direction
(Theorem~\ref{thm:decimation}), within the decimation's validity cells and at
the spectator certificates $\lambda_i$
(Lemma~\ref{lem:spectator}(ii)).

\emph{(i) A ground read is a stage read.} The ground $\psi$ has a
spanning list by elimination over the ground root's stage
(Lemma~\ref{lem:split}), and at an observable $A$ the read
enters as the pair of the two pairings,
$\langle\psi,A\,\psi\rangle$ against the gram's
$\langle\psi,G\,\psi\rangle$, its value the cofactor
(Definition~\ref{def:ground}),
Definition~\ref{def:pencil}'s $\omega$ at the unit gram; each
pairing is one stage read, its entries
Lemma~\ref{lem:stableentries}'s at the supports.

\emph{(ii) The tail rides the transfer factors.} At the ground root
the kernel rows of the slab split close from the far end,
$X_{i+1}\,\psi_{i+1}+B_i^{\mathsf T}\psi_i$ the sum's unit per row
(Lemma~\ref{lem:greenprod}(iii)'s row read), the tail pivots of
unequal-membered determinant on the validity cells, the ground's root the decimated
head's own (Theorem~\ref{thm:decimation}(i)); so a depth-$j$ component is
a $j$-fold transfer image and its weight is capped geometrically,
\[
\langle\psi_{i+1},\,G\,\psi_{i+1}\rangle\ \le\
\lambda_i^{\,2}\,\langle\psi_i,\,G\,\psi_i\rangle ,
\]
the certificates' product pricing the tail's whole weight beyond
any depth.

\emph{(iii) The ground transport, and the window sandwich.} The

\begin{theorem}[Euclidean restoration is a count]\label{thm:restoration}
Take the carrier at the lattice interface's fields and a region
mapped to itself by the hyperoctahedral group $B_d$ of signed
coordinate permutations (Construction~\ref{con:lattice}).

\emph{The symmetry passes to the fiber symbol.} $E=\sum_\ell\Delta_\ell$ and
$M=\sum_p\chi_\theta(U_{\partial p})$ run over all links and all plaquettes at
coefficient one (Definition~\ref{def:pencil}), and $B_d$ permutes those two
sets. A signed permutation reverses link orientations, which dualizes labels, so
$E$ is fixed because $C_2(\bar\lambda)=C_2(\lambda)$
(Definition~\ref{def:c2hat}) and $M$ is fixed because
$\chi_{\bar\theta}=\chi_\theta$ at the self-dual adjoint, a
reversed boundary reading the dual label's character. Hence
$H([\alpha\!:\!\beta])$ commutes with the action. A lattice symmetry
$g$ permutes the label index isometrically and maps each direction's
translation to the permuted direction's own, so it maps chord blocks
onto chord blocks (Lemma~\ref{lem:fiberdec}): the direction
permutation permutes the block index $(t_1,\dots,t_d)$, and an
orientation flip reads $T$ at $T^{\mathsf T}$, the chord operator
$S=T+T^{\mathsf T}$ fixed with it. So the $K$-fiber's pencil and the
$gK$-fiber's are congruent at the relabeling, the pencil's rows
and columns read along one permutation of the window index, an
orthogonal map;
the site-datum determinant reads one value across it, the
assignment fold reindexed along the relabeling: each assignment
pairs with its relabeled own at the entries' product kept, the
row and column exchanges composing at an even join
(Definition~\ref{def:elim}'s exchanged reads at
Construction~\ref{con:places}' descent), and their
characteristic polynomials agree, coefficient by coefficient:
\[
\mathcal S(gK;k,r)\;=\;\mathcal S(K;k,r)
\]
with $\mathcal S$ the fiber symbol of Theorem~\ref{thm:trigpencil}. Every
coefficient of $\mathcal S$ in $k$ is therefore one value at every
relabeling of the chord list: a flip fixes each chord at the
operator's own read, so $B_d$ reads the chord list through its
direction permutations alone, and the coefficients are the
permutation-invariant chord polynomials, one coefficient identity
per congruence; their momentum reading is even in each coordinate,
the deck relation's two roots exchanging at a flip with the chord
fixed (Definition~\ref{def:deck}). The derivation runs unchanged at every number of
arguments. A lattice symmetry permutes the label index and maps the
fibers isometrically, an orthogonal map commuting with $H$, so at a
window whose multiplicity reads $1$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:SO}) it reads the
ground line by a graded read the reads of $\omega$ are blind to:
$\omega$ is
$B_d$-invariant, and hence every read
$\omega(A_1\cdots A_m)$ is $B_d$-invariant, jointly in the positions and the
orientations $B_d$ permutes. The \emph{momentum transform} of the
read family is its translate family read at monomials: per
argument the translate key list $a^{(r)}$, one key per direction
below the side, the read
$\omega\big(T^{a^{(1)}}A_1\cdots T^{a^{(m)}}A_m\big)$ at the
composite shifts, and the transform the occupancy family of those
reads at the per-argument exponent lists, the translate keys'
own matched lists (Definition~\ref{def:ground}), the momenta
$K^{(1)},\dots,K^{(m)}$ the exponent coordinates, one list per
argument; a lattice symmetry moves a translate read to
the $g$-moved keys' own, the direction permutation permuting each
argument's keys and a flip reading a key to its balance partner
at the wrap (Lemma~\ref{lem:fiberdec}'s $T^L=1$), so the
transform is $B_d$-invariant with the reads, the moves the
coefficient identities.

\emph{What the continuum asks is $O(d)$, and the difference is a multiplicity.}
The momenta enter as formal coordinates at the transform's own
carrier, one matched list per argument, and two moves settle every
degree: a reflection, one coordinate $K_i$
read to its balance partner
across all $m$ momenta at once, sends every monomial at an odd count
of that coordinate to its balance partner, the coefficient's
members then equal, and a transposition of
coordinates equalizes what survives.
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em,itemsep=1pt]
\item \emph{Degree $2$: the rings coincide, at every $m$ and every $d$.} The
reflection leaves only $\sum_iK^{(r)}_iK^{(s)}_i$, and the transposition fixes its
coefficient, so the degree-$2$ $B_d$-invariants are spanned by the pairwise
inner products $K^{(r)}\!\cdot\!K^{(s)}$, each of which is $O(d)$-invariant
already. The leading term is isotropic because the invariant ring at that degree
is the inner products' span; the inclusion
$B_d\text{-inv}\subseteq O(d)\text{-inv}$ at that degree is the whole of what
the coincidence asks, and the two moves above exhibit it.
\item \emph{Degree $4$: breaking enters.} The \emph{isotropic
span} at a degree is the pairwise inner products' polynomial span,
item 1's ring at its products, and the breaking
\emph{multiplicity} is the independent invariants' count beyond
it, the classical $O(d)$-invariant ring the isotropic span's
reading in the dictionary alone: the derivations
read the two spans and their counts. The reflection leaves the
coordinate indices paired, so the space includes diagonal
quartics alongside the inner products' products, and the count
reads one of them, $p_4=\sum_i\big(K^{(1)}_i\big)^4$, every
coordinate total four. At $m=1$ every invariant is a cleared
combination of $p_2^2$ and $p_4$ over the balance pairs
(Definition~\ref{def:ground}), the clearing datum two at the
paired monomial's splitting count, $K_i^2K_j^2$ read at its two
factor orders, and the two are independent, a combination at the
sum's unit reading each coefficient at the quartic and the paired
monomial in turn: multiplicity $1$ at the join $1+1=2$, at every
$d\ge2$, with $p_4$ off the isotropic span at the paired
coefficient. At every $m$ the quartic $p_4$ is an invariant off
the isotropic span at $d\ge2$, its two reads at the first
argument's own monomials, so the multiplicity is positive there.
\item \emph{The rate is the gap between those degrees, uniformly in $m$.} An
odd degree's every invariant reads equal members at every monomial, the
coordinate totals joining to the degree with one member odd; so the
leading term sits at degree $2$ and the first breaking invariant at
degree $4$, the breaking suppressed by two degrees in the
momentum, the gap at $2+2=4$, at
every such family and at every $d$. What varies with $m$ is the multiplicity at
degree $4$; the rate stays that gap.
\end{enumerate}
Degree-$2$ isotropy therefore holds at every order and at every member, since the
statement is about which forms exist rather than about which are small,
and the ring at degree $2$ is the inner products' span. Restoration is a multiplicity read, and the degree-two rings'
coincidence is exactly what the continuum asks.

\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[The continuum reads]\label{thm:continuum}
Fix the member and the direction count. A \emph{continuum
datum} is a finite list of supports, separations and Euclidean
pairs stated against the corner scale at pair coordinates
(Lemma~\ref{lem:corner}; Theorem~\ref{thm:groundreads}(v)), its
observables the algebra's at those supports
(Definition~\ref{def:algebra}).

\emph{(i) The data are bracketed, and the spectral heights
read two-sided on the certificate's cell.} At
every scale of the corner cell the datum's read sits in the stated
brackets (Theorem~\ref{thm:groundreads}(i)--(vi)), the support
counts read as the scale counts against the corner scale
(Theorem~\ref{thm:groundreads}(vii)), and two scales' reads
sit in the scale sandwich's priced brackets
(Theorem~\ref{thm:groundreads}(vii)). On the corner disconjugacy
certificate's cell (Lemma~\ref{lem:cornerpivot}, the decimated
chains' at the block clauses) the floor
locates the clearance at every tail scale, the ground-level
sandwich sums to one cofactor read at the upper family's rate
(Lemma~\ref{lem:corner}), the heights read two-sided at the
certificate's rates, the site counts stabilizing with the floor,
and a datum's scale reads close on the moment folds' clearance
cell (Lemma~\ref{lem:momentfold}).

\emph{(ii) The bracketed reads cohere.} Every finite Gram of
bracketed reads is positive semidefinite within its brackets, the pairing
definitional at every scale (Definition~\ref{def:carrier};
Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}); reads at disjoint supports read one
value at either order, and an ordering difference beyond the reach
is the remainder's own read
(Theorem~\ref{thm:groundreads}(vi)); separated reads pair to their
product within the crossing certificates' brackets
(Theorem~\ref{thm:groundreads}(iv)); the chain's own heights are
two-sided located data at the cell's positive located floor,
two-sided at the
certificate's rates on its cell, so the spectral data sit at the
ground with the floor's clearance in the corner
scale there (Lemma~\ref{lem:corner}'s cell floor;
Lemma~\ref{lem:speccut}); and the momentum reads are isotropic at
degree two with the direction data two degrees down, the
multiplicities scale-free (Lemma~\ref{lem:cone};
Theorem~\ref{thm:restoration}).

\emph{(iii) The spacing is an output.} The corner height reads
two-sided at the certificate's rates on its cell, at the cell's
positive located floor, one output
bracket (Lemma~\ref{lem:corner}'s cell floor).
\end{theorem}

\section{The network constituents}

\begin{theorem}[Network reduction and the two-vertex coupling law]
\label{thm:network}
(i) A three-link path labeled $R$ is one edge
labeled $R$ (the convolution identity), so the
theta graph reduces to the three-edge $\Theta$-graph and the state
space is $\Theta(R_1,k,R_2;\alpha,\beta)$-networks with $E$
diagonal, eigenvalue $3C_2(R_1)+C_2(k)+3C_2(R_2)$.
(ii) Couplings between sectors differing on the shared link need
the label-index reads of Proposition~\ref{prop:repring} on the shared edge
alone: the
module--pair weights are
\[
\begin{gathered}
w_k\;=\;\big[\,m_k\,d_k:r^2(r+2)^2\,\big],\qquad
\sum_k m_kd_k=r^2(r+2)^2\\
\text{(the block count over
Lemma~\ref{lem:channels}'s list, Lemma~\ref{lem:blockcount}(ii))},
\end{gathered}
\]
with $d_k$ and $m_k$ the dimension and the multiplicity of
Proposition~\ref{prop:repring} at the channel $k$, so the sum is the
completeness of $\mathrm{adj}\otimes\mathrm{adj}$, whose dimension is
$r^2(r+2)^2$; the weights are the channel table's reads
(Lemma~\ref{lem:channels}),
\[
\begin{gathered}
w\;=\;\Big([\,1:r^2(r+2)^2\,],\ [\,c_1:r(r+2)\,],\
[\,(r+1)^2r(r+4):4r^2(r+2)^2\,],\\
[\,g\,(r+3):4r(r+2)\,]_{g+1=r},\
[\,g\,(r+3):4r(r+2)\,]_{g+1=r},\
[\,(r+1)^2(r+2)\,w':4r^2(r+2)^2\,]_{w'+2=r}\Big)
\end{gathered}
\]
over the channel list, the leading-term reads in the residue: the
first two weights' second members exceed in degree, and the four
channel weights read $[\,1:4\,]$ at the top
(Theorem~\ref{thm:windowsep}'s leading terms).
(iii) \emph{The weights are the data.} The slot-two self-energy assembled from
these weights and the network energies is
$\sum_kw_k\,[\,q_k:2q_k+p_k\,]$ at $\hat c_2(k)=[\,p_k:q_k\,]$, the
factor's gap the module pair's network eigenvalue against the
module count, $3+\hat c_2(k)+3$ against $E_0=4$ at
$\hat c_2(\mathrm{adj})=1$ ((i)'s eigenvalue;
Proposition~\ref{prop:E0}), the resolvent factor a constructed
pair. The recoupling constituent's content is the finite
tetrahedral list: the within-pair middle matrix
$\langle(\mathrm{adj},k',\mathrm{adj})|\chi_X(U_1)|
(\mathrm{adj},k,\mathrm{adj})\rangle$ at the middle index the
channel table's occupied Casimir values, the dual pair's shared
value one joint block at the summed weight, computable per entry
by the $E_{\mathrm{link}}$ interpolant, the projection onto one
Casimir value a polynomial in $E_{\mathrm{link}}$
(Construction~\ref{con:units}'s Lagrange read) entered cleared at
the node differences' product, one value across the clearing
scale (Definition~\ref{def:ground}'s homogeneity principle), at
the channel distances' occupied gaps (Theorem~\ref{thm:xdata}).
\end{theorem}

\section{The drift and the divisor}

\begin{theorem}[The uniform drift]\label{thm:drift}
At every label $R$ the adjoint row reads
$\sum_km_kd_k\hat c_2(k)=d_Rd_{\mathrm{adj}}(\hat c_2(R)+1)$, the
fold over the row's channels at the multiplicities against the
dimensions: \emph{the dimension-weighted fusion walk's Casimir
drift is exactly $+1$, uniformly in $R$}
($\sum_kw_k\hat c_2(k)=2$, the channel table's second closure
identity, Lemma~\ref{lem:channels}). With strictness (the unit
label is the one label of dimension one: a nonunit reduced shape
reads an occupied head row at its positive degree against the
withdrawn full column's vacant last row, the rows weakly
descending (Construction~\ref{con:places}), so a consecutive row
gap $h\ge1$ sits at some $\lambda_{i+1}+h=\lambda_i$, its gap
factor $h+1$ against the unit display's one with every further
factor at or beyond its left partner
(Corollary~\ref{cor:weyldim}'s display), so $d_\lambda\ge2$), the
closure datum is a \emph{constant} rather than a decaying one.
\begin{proof}
\emph{The member tier.} Trace the Casimir over
$W_R\otimes W_\theta$ at the trace read
(Lemma~\ref{lem:lowerspan}), one value at every orthogonal
spanning list. At an exhaustion the blocks' lists join to one
orthogonal spanning list, the blocks pairwise perpendicular
(Lemma~\ref{lem:blockcount}(i),(ii)), and the fold splits per
block at the cleared products, each block's diagonal pairings the
scalar's multiples of the self-pairings
(Lemma~\ref{lem:blockirr}(ii); Lemma~\ref{lem:casimir}): the
trace reads $\sum_km_kd_k\,C_2(k)$, one summand per channel at
its count. The tensor's operators read by the Leibniz rule and
the Casimir's fold collects at the factors
(Construction~\ref{con:gentable}),
\[
C_\otimes\;=\;C_R\otimes1+1\otimes C_\theta
+2\sum_\alpha[\,\langle\alpha,\alpha\rangle:2\,]\,
\big(E_\alpha\otimes F_\alpha+F_\alpha\otimes E_\alpha\big)
+Z\ ,
\]
each root term's two orders splitting at the four arrangements
with the one-sided pairs collecting to the factors' own folds,
and $Z$ the diagonal fold's cross read, the form's additivity at
the joint contents,
$q(\mu+\nu)=q(\mu)+q(\nu)+2\,\langle\mu,\nu\rangle$ per content
pair. The trace factorizes over the tensor list, two tensors
pairing at the factors' pairings' product
(Lemma~\ref{lem:blockcount}(iii)), so each term's trace is the
factor traces' product: the two one-sided terms read
$C_2(R)\,d_R\,d_\theta$ and $d_R\,C_2(\theta)\,d_\theta$; a root
term's factor trace is a fold of cross-content pairings, each of
equal members at the content sum's orthogonality
(Construction~\ref{con:gentable}), so the root terms' traces read
equal members; and $Z$'s trace is the double fold
$2\sum_{\mu,\nu}\mathrm{mult}_R(\mu)\,\mathrm{mult}_\theta(\nu)\,
\langle\mu,\nu\rangle$, the form's bilinearity collecting it to
$2\,\langle F_R,F_\theta\rangle$ at the content folds
$F_X:=\sum_\mu\mathrm{mult}_X(\mu)\,\mu$. The $\theta$-block's
contents are simple folds, the root list's members with the unit
content (Proposition~\ref{prop:row}'s content list) at each
root's fold over the simple list
(Construction~\ref{con:gentable}), so
the pairing collects at the halved products to $F_R$'s coroot
pairs, $2\,\langle F_R,\alpha_i\rangle
=F_R(\alpha_i^\vee)\,\langle\alpha_i,\alpha_i\rangle$, and each
reads equal members: the multiplicities are one value at every
reflection (Lemma~\ref{lem:strings}), a content joined to its
$s_i$-image reads the coroot pair with its balance partner, and a
fixed content's pair is its own partner. So
\[
\sum_km_kd_k\,C_2(k)\;=\;d_R\,d_\theta\,
\big(C_2(R)+C_2(\theta)\big)\ ,
\]
and the display clears at $C_2(\theta)=2\,(r+1)$
(Proposition~\ref{prop:anchor}), one value across the clearing
(Definition~\ref{def:ground}'s homogeneity principle):
$\sum_km_kd_k\hat c_2(k)=d_Rd_\theta(\hat c_2(R)+1)$.

\emph{The label calculus at the residue.} At the matrix units the
constant family holds every consecutive coroot pair at equal
members, so the content folds read the constant family and the
derivation runs at the alternant display's recursion
(Theorem~\ref{thm:weylchar}), the display at every content list
of the block's width with a key at the count's unit reading the
moved folds' one value. Sum the display over the recursion
family's keys: the moved folds reindex at the move's own round
trip, per place pair $a<b$ and per occupied target $\kappa$ the
sources at the shifts $1\le l\le\kappa_a$, one source per
shift at the reverse move, the second letter's weight reading the
target's own $\kappa_b+l$ and the shifted first letter's
$\kappa_a+l$; the two reindexed folds share the shift fold
$\sum_{l\le\kappa_a}l$, a summand on both members fixing the
value (Definition~\ref{def:ground}), and the summed display reads
\[
q(\lambda+u)\,d_\lambda
+2\sum_\kappa\mathrm{mult}(\kappa)\sum_{a<b}\kappa_a\kappa_b
\;=\;\sum_\kappa\mathrm{mult}(\kappa)\,q(\kappa+u)
+2\sum_\kappa\mathrm{mult}(\kappa)\sum_{a<b}\kappa_a^{\,2}\ .
\]
The swap symmetry (Lemma~\ref{lem:strings}'s read at the matrix
units) reads each place fold place-free, the first moment
$M_1$, the square fold $M_2$ and the product fold $M_{11}$ one
value per place and place pair with
$d_f\,M_1=k_\lambda\,d_\lambda$
at the contents' degree read, so the summed display collects to
$q(\lambda+u)\,d_\lambda+2\,p\,M_{11}
=d_f\,M_2+2\,U_1\,M_1+q(u)\,d_\lambda+2\,p\,M_2$ at $p$ the
place pairs' count and $U_1$ the unit display's total. The fused
carrier's family splits twice, over the channels at the
exhaustion's content refinement (Lemma~\ref{lem:blockcount}(ii))
and over the factors at the contents' addition
(Construction~\ref{con:places}), so each moment fold reads both
ways, the channels' counted fold against the factors' split at
the further factor's count,
\[
M_1(F)=d_\theta\,M_1(R)+d_R\,M_1(\theta)\ ,\qquad
M_2(F)=d_\theta\,M_2(R)+2\,M_1(R)\,M_1(\theta)+d_R\,M_2(\theta)\ ,
\]
with $M_{11}(F)$ at $M_2$'s shape, the cross read
$2\,M_1(R)\,M_1(\theta)$ shared: the collected display at the
fused carrier, read against the two factors' own collected
displays at the further factors' counts, withdraws the quadratic
folds (a summand on both members fixing the value) and leaves
\[
\sum_cN^c\,q(c+u)\,d_c+q(u)\,d_Rd_\theta
\;=\;\big(q(R+u)+q(\theta+u)\big)\,d_Rd_\theta
+2\,d_f\,M_1(R)\,M_1(\theta)\ ,
\]
whose $d_f$-multiple reads the moments at the degree folds,
$d_f\,M_1(R)=k_R\,d_R$ and $d_f\,M_1(\theta)=d_f\,d_\theta$ at
$\theta$'s degree $d_f$. The square identity
(Definition~\ref{def:c2hat}) with the row read
$\langle\lambda,2\rho\rangle+(d_f+1)\,k_\lambda
=2\sum_i\lambda_iu_i$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:weylchar}'s collection at
Lemma~\ref{lem:casimir}'s fold) reads
$d_f\,q(\mu+u)=d_fQ(\mu)+k_\mu^{\,2}+d_f(d_f+1)\,k_\mu
+d_f\,q(u)$ at every shape; every channel shares the degree
$k_c=k_R+d_f$, the contents adding over the factors at $\theta$'s
degree, so the dimension identity $\sum_cN^cd_c=d_Rd_\theta$
(Lemma~\ref{lem:blockcount}(iii)) withdraws the shared reads and
the display closes at $Q(\theta)=2d_f$
(Lemma~\ref{lem:casimir}):
\[
\sum_cN^c\,d_c\,d_fQ(c)\;=\;d_Rd_\theta\,
\big(d_fQ(R)+2d_f^{\,2}\big)\ ,
\]
the display's $\hat c_2$-cleared read at
$[\,d_fQ:2d_f^{\,2}\,]$ (Definition~\ref{def:c2hat}).
\end{proof}
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[The divisor identification, and the crossing divisor]
\label{thm:divisorid}
\emph{(i) The identification.} The spectrum is the root locus of
the exact fiber symbol (Theorem~\ref{thm:trigpencil}): on a window a
level is a pencil root exactly where the determinant reads equal
members, the
factorization read of Lemma~\ref{lem:split} at the gram's
determinant off the sum's unit, and the Feshbach
determinant reads equal members with it, one Schur factor of the whole
at a complement of unequal-membered determinant
(Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}), at every coupling
outright, each root a located divisor point.

\emph{(ii) The crossing divisor.} For a symmetric stage pencil of the
chain with $G\succ0$, a window compression or a fiber pencil, the
entries polynomial in the base coordinates over the stage's field
(Lemma~\ref{lem:fiberdec}'s band congruence), write $\chi(x)$ for the level
pair's site-datum determinant (Lemma~\ref{lem:split}'s $\chi$) and
take the squarefree split $\chi=c\prod_jS_j^{\,j}$ over the base's
polynomial pairs, the division descent's iterated greatest common
divisors, the equality test cross-multiplied
through the iterated division, each $S_j$ taken
primitive over the base's polynomial ring with the contents collected
into $c$, itself a base polynomial (Gauss): the split is an identity
of base polynomials and specializes at every stage point. The
\emph{crossing read} is
\[
D\;:=\;\prod_j\mathrm{Res}_x\big(S_j,S_j'\big)\ \cdot
\prod_{i<j}\mathrm{Res}_x\big(S_i,S_j\big)\ ,
\]
the resultants Definition~\ref{def:elim}'s at the variable $x$, a
base element off the sum's unit: a factor of equal members reads an
occupied cross pair on
a squarefree or coprime pair, against its B\'ezout read $up+vq=1$
(Definition~\ref{def:elim}). At
a base point off $D$'s root locus the specialized split keeps the generic
structure: the leading $x$-coefficients multiply to $\det G$ there, on its upper
side by the split, so every one reads unequal members and the degrees
hold; each $S_j$ stays squarefree and the pairs stay coprime, the
resultants' own reads there; so every root of $\chi$ at that point is
a root of exactly one $S_j$, simple in it, of multiplicity $j$ in
$\chi$, and distinct roots stay distinct: \emph{root collisions sit on
$D$'s located root locus alone}, the jump divisors of the
decimation's list; a decimated head reads its counts as the fiber
pencil's on its bracket, so its jumps inherit the confinement.

\emph{(iii) The ground's read.} On a cell with pairs $l<h$,
the pencil pair at $l$ positive semidefinite and the counting
function flat at $h$
across the cell, the ground multiplicity is
$\mathrm{count}(h)$, both reads cell functions read
once (Lemma~\ref{lem:cellcount}); by (ii) it moves across a located
boundary only where a ground collision sits on the boundary, the
boundary's own two-sided bracket counts. On the vacuum sector's
pencil at the free end's side it reads $1$: a level
bracket of pairs $(l,h)$ isolating the free ground, the unit's line
against content $4\hat c_2(f)$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:freeend}), reads
the pair at $l$ positive semidefinite and $\mathrm{count}(h)=1$
at the free end, both reads
constant over the attached cell (Lemma~\ref{lem:cellcount}), so the
least root sits in the bracket at multiplicity $1$ there.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[The scheme bridge is a count]\label{thm:bridge}
\[
\big[\,d_{\mathrm{adj}}\,C_2(\mathrm{adj})\;:\;d_f\,C_2(f)\,\big]
\;=\;2\,d_f
\]
exactly, the pair of two weight-$1$ objects, one value at every form
point
(Proposition~\ref{prop:form}), and the read is a count: the $f$-flux
bridge's component at the contact end is
$b_f^{\mathrm{equiv}}=2d_f\,b_X$, one
rescaling of the coupling coordinate.
\begin{proof}
$d_{\mathrm{adj}}=r\,(r+2)$ with $\hat c_2(\mathrm{adj})=1$
(Lemma~\ref{lem:adjchar}; Definition~\ref{def:c2hat}), and the fundamental
count $d_f$ with
$\hat c_2(f)=[\,r(r+2):2d_f^{\,2}\,]$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:casimir}), so
the pair reads the cross-multiplied natural identity
$r(r+2)\cdot2d_f^{\,2}=2\,d_f\cdot d_f\cdot r\,(r+2)$; the $r=2$
evaluation is $6$.
\end{proof}
\end{theorem}

\begin{definition}[The ruler excess]\label{def:excess}
At the residue $r$ the \emph{ruler excess} is the polynomial
\[
A(u)\;:=\;\sum_{j=1}^{r}[\,1:j\,]\,u^j
\]
(Definition~\ref{def:poly}), the one polynomial supported from
key one with $A'+u^r=1+u\,A'$, $r$ the pairing's residue.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[The walk residue]\label{thm:walkresidue}
The tower pairing is forced. At the residue $r$:
for the principal adjoint tower (the $k\theta$-string, $\theta$ the highest root, $\hat c_2(k)=[\,k(k+r):r+1\,]$), the Casimir $C_2(m\theta)=m\,(m+r)$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:anchor} splits into a square part and a row-weight part,
and the pairing is the second over the whole:
\[
\varphi_m=\big[\,m\,r:m\,(m+r)\,\big]=[\,r:m+r\,],
\qquad
\varphi_m+\psi_m=1\quad\text{at}\quad\psi_m=[\,m:m+r\,]\ ,
\]
two weight-$1$ objects read as one pair, one value at every form
point
(Proposition~\ref{prop:form}), every datum forced, $\psi_m$ the
pairing's complement, the one-value read. Its generating object is
\[
\Psi(u):=\sum_{m\ge1}[\,1:m\,]\,\psi_m\,u^m
=\sum_{m\ge1}[\,1:m+r\,]\,u^m\ ,
\]
and the shifted series $F(u):=u^{r}\Psi(u)=\sum_{j\ge r+1}[\,1:j\,]\,u^j$ satisfies
the recursion
$F'=u^{r}+u\,F'$, coefficientwise one read per degree: its
divisor pair reads the least occupied degree $r$ and the pole order
$1$ at $u{=}1$, both naturals. Against the harmonic ruler
$R(u):=\sum_{m\ge1}[\,1:m\,]\,u^m$, the ruler
at the recursion $R'=1+u\,R'$, its pole order $1$ as well, the pole
excess is
one polynomial, coefficientwise exact and finitely supported:
\begin{gather*}
R(u)\;=\;F(u)+A(u)\ ,\qquad A(u)\;=\;\sum_{j=1}^{r}[\,1:j\,]\,u^j
\quad(\text{Definition~\ref{def:excess}})\ ,\\
A'+u^r=1+u\,A',\quad\text{the support from key one}\ ,
\end{gather*}
the one polynomial with the geometric derivative, closing
$F'+A'=R'$ term by term, a
divisor-characterized object. The value's second coordinate is its
read at $u=1$, the polynomial evaluation
\[
A(1)\;=\;H_r=\sum_{j=1}^{r}[\,1:j\,]\ ,
\]
one pair per residue, the ruler's excess over the walk; and the coefficientwise record at the
$\Psi$-level is the finite telescope, exact at every $M$,
\[
\sum_{m=1}^{M}[\,r:m(m+r)\,]
+\sum_{j=M+1}^{M+r}[\,1:j\,]
\;=\;H_r\ ,
\qquad
[\,1:m\,]=[\,1:m+r\,]+[\,r:m(m+r)\,]\ ,
\]
the tail itself an $r$-term expression. One object, one pole, one
read: $\varphi$ fixes $\Psi$, $\Psi$'s divisor fixes $F'$, and the
ruler's excess is the polynomial $A$, the construction settling the
coordinate outright. The exponent is an integer at $r=1$ alone; the pairing's own
pole residue $r$ and the weight-$1$ tower sum $[\,r+1:r\,]H_r$ agree with it
there and separate from $r=2$, so the residue-one instance leaves them
together, and the construction rather than the instance is what selects.

\end{theorem}

\begin{definition}[The contact pair]\label{def:contactpair}
At the contact end the decimated head's counts are the walk's
(Lemma~\ref{lem:contactcell}: the contact end's reads are the fusion
matrix's own), and two divisors read them: the channel's, the boundary
resolvent $G_0$ with its $\iota$-symmetrized weight
(Theorem~\ref{thm:channeldiv}), and the tower's, the generating object
$\Psi$ against the harmonic ruler (Theorem~\ref{thm:walkresidue}). The
\emph{contact pair} is the two divisors' reads: the first coordinate is
the channel's order count at the base $c_1$
(Definition~\ref{def:channelreads}); the second is
the ruler's excess over the walk read at one, $A(1)$
(Definition~\ref{def:excess}).
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[The constant's assembly]\label{thm:closing}
The contact pair of Definition~\ref{def:contactpair} reads
factor by factor, and there are two: the walk
exponent $H_r$ on the ruler's side, the polynomial excess $A(1)$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:walkresidue}) and coupling-free; and the channel's bit count,
read off the divisor of $G_0$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:channeldiv}). The pencil's other
degree-one read is the module count $E_0$ at the free end against the level at
the contact end, and $\mathcal K$ is the pair of those two reads
(Definition~\ref{def:K}, Theorem~\ref{thm:unitschain}). What makes the two
divisor factors below a read of it is Theorem~\ref{thm:divisorid}: the
Feshbach determinant reads equal members exactly on the spectrum, so the level
Definition~\ref{def:K}'s cut fixes is a point of the fiber symbol's divisor, and
the coordinates below are that point's. That level reads two-sidedly
at the walk's own divisor: at the contact end the head's counts are
the fusion matrix's, the walk's divisor data
(Lemma~\ref{lem:contactcell}; Proposition~\ref{prop:row}), a window
cut the chain's compression at that window and the sector's within
it, a compression of a compression (Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}); the
truncation pair's two counts bracket the chain's at every level
below the seed floor at every cutoff with an agreeing pair a
located count of the chain itself, the scale key's cutoff clearing
every window level below the seed floor at once
(Lemma~\ref{lem:corner}), so the window cuts' edges sit at or
beyond the chain's and the bordered pencils' at or below, counts
monotone under the comparisons (Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}), the level
the mass point's own located read between them; the mass weight is
the pair $[\,\langle c_1^{\,2}\!:\!1\rangle:c_1^{\,2}\,]$ against
its complement $[\,1:c_1^{\,2}\,]$, the two summing to one, its
side the upper at base two and equal members at base one, the mass
point at the crossing there, the divisor count one value throughout
(Theorem~\ref{thm:channeldiv}); and each window cut's edge, where
one is named, is the compressed pair's least root, read at a word
image with the two moments at equality and the first positive, the
state the word sector's own
(Lemma~\ref{lem:attained}(i),(ii); Lemma~\ref{lem:statespace}).
So the pair's two coordinates read:
\[
\big(3,\ H_r\big) ,
\]
and both are divisor reads. The second is the walk's pole
excess against the harmonic ruler, the polynomial read $A(1)=H_r$
on the ruler's side, off
$F'$'s divisor pair, the least occupied degree $r$ and the pole order
$1$ at $u{=}1$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:walkresidue}). The first is the channel's order count: the
boundary entry at its renewal witness $G_0=z+c_1zG_0$ reads its
divisor as the witness's
own, the mass point at $c_1z_*=1$ a simple pole, order $1$, and the
$\iota$-symmetrized weight reading the base at order $2$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:channeldiv}); the two orders against the base give
$c_1^{\,1}\cdot c_1^{\,2}=c_1^{\,3}$.

The pair $(3,H_r)$ is what the theory determines: two orders, both
naturals,
summing to $3$, and an exact pair, the first coordinate read at the base
$c_1$, a fusion multiplicity moving with the residue
(Construction~\ref{con:xfusion}). The two reads are of two divisors, the channel's ($G_0$, Theorem~\ref{thm:channeldiv}) and the tower's
($\Psi$, Theorem~\ref{thm:walkresidue}), and the coordinates read
them alone.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[The scaling form, and the constant as a pair of reads]
\label{thm:unitschain}
The pencil is degree-one homogeneous in the end weights,
$H([\,u\alpha\!:\!u\beta\,])=u\,H([\alpha\!:\!\beta])$, so a rescaling
multiplies every spectral read of the family by one $u$; the pair
of two degree-one reads therefore reads one value at that scale, at
whichever
points of the base the two are taken. The overall scale of $H$ is the one
freedom the
base is already taken up to (Proposition~\ref{prop:segment}), so the
statement is
written on the end-weight pair alone: \emph{the ray is the only variable}.

\emph{Each end has its read, and the invariant is their pair.} At the free
end, where the pencil is $E$, the module is an eigenvector with the link count
$E_0$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:E0}); at the contact end the value is the level.
Both are degree-one, so by the paragraph above the pair descends, and
that pair is $\mathcal K$ (Definition~\ref{def:K}). It descends jointly:
the two reads enter symmetrically, each a read rather than the
other's measuring scale. Both are weight-free, one value at every form point
(Proposition~\ref{prop:form}), and $\mathcal K$ is a count against a count.

\emph{Consequences.} (1) $\mathcal K$ is
dimension-free, both of its coordinates divisor reads: the pole
excess built from the Casimir, the order count from the channel
divisor, each read $d$-free.
(2) The scheme bridge $b_f^{\mathrm{equiv}}=2d_f\,b_X$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:bridge}) rescales the coupling coordinate, acting on
the base by $[\alpha\!:\!\beta]\mapsto[\,\alpha\!:\!\lam\beta\,]$, whose fixed points are
\emph{exactly} the two ends, so $\mathcal K$ is invariant under it: the
bridge locates a coupling in the fundamental theory
(Remark~\ref{rem:certtarget}).

\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[The decomposition]\label{thm:decomp}
The chain's named outputs factor. The invariant's two
coordinates are residue-side divisor reads, one value at every
direction count and every family scale: the order count off the
channel's divisor and the pole excess off the tower's
(Theorem~\ref{thm:closing}; Theorem~\ref{thm:unitschain}), the
per-member table the pair $(3,H_r)$ at the member's derived
residue with the base the $\theta$-support count
(Proposition~\ref{prop:anchor}; Proposition~\ref{prop:row}),
two at the $A$-series at $r\ge2$, one at its first member and
at every further member. The
corner outputs, the extent, the floor and the spacing, hold the
whole direction and scheme dependence: each is a located datum
per member and direction count
(Lemma~\ref{lem:corner}), moving with the family's scale as the
level does, while the invariant reads one value across the
directions and the scales, the dimension-freeness
Theorem~\ref{thm:unitschain}'s first consequence.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[The two-action comparison]\label{thm:twoaction}
At the fundamental-character family, the pencil at the magnetic
member $\sum_p\big(\chi_f(U_{\partial p})
+\chi_{\bar f}(U_{\partial p})\big)$, the chain's corner
machinery runs at the swapped member: the sum is symmetric at
the dual pair, each term capped as a form at the fundamental's
own square (Lemma~\ref{lem:loopcap}); the fibering, the
truncation caps and the momentum reads are the derivations' own
instances there, while the member moves the ality by one, so the
charged grading reads at its own class data and the comparison
runs at the outputs both families define, the extent, the floor
and the spacing at the unit-class head, located data per
member and direction count. The invariant is fixed under the
bridge, the rescaling $b_f^{\mathrm{equiv}}=2d_f\,b_X$ of the
coupling coordinate with the two ends its fixed points
(Theorem~\ref{thm:bridge}; Theorem~\ref{thm:unitschain}), so the
two families' invariants read one pair, and each corner output's
comparison is one bracket at the two families' certificates,
finite and decidable.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Truncation control]\label{thm:truncation}
At the coupling $[\alpha\!:\!\beta]$ the truncation at Casimir cutoff
$\Lambda_C$ is a projection $\Pi$, the statement homogeneous on the ray,
and the off-block coupling is the magnetic form's cap. $M$ is
multiplication by
$\sum_p\chi_\theta(U_{\partial p})$ (Definition~\ref{def:pencil}), each
plaquette term capped as a form, two-sided at the adjoint
dimension (Lemma~\ref{lem:loopcap}, the member cap; the
$A$-series' shifted square at unit width below and $r\,(r+2)$
above), so the sum is capped two-sidedly and with
it the pencil's magnetic part (Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}),
\[
\beta M\ \preceq\ W\;:=\;\beta\,\#p\,d_\theta
\qquad\text{with}\qquad
W+\beta M\ \ \text{positive semidefinite},
\]
the adjoint dimension times the region's plaquette count at the
magnetic weight, $d_\theta=r\,(r+2)$ the $A$-series read
(Proposition~\ref{prop:row}'s dimension fold),
independent of the cutoff; $E$ being diagonal reads its off-block
part at the sum's unit ($\Pi E\,\Pi'$ at the complement projection
$\Pi'$ at $\Pi+\Pi'=1$), so the off-block coupling of $H$ is the magnetic one
alone, under the one form cap $W$. The cap's compression
(Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}) bounds the magnetic part inside the removed
block, whose electric diagonal sits at or above $\alpha\Lambda_C$, so that
block's floor $f$ reads the cross-added comparison
$W+f\ge\alpha\Lambda_C$;
writing $E_{\mathrm{low}}$ for the higher of the two window levels the window cut of
Definition~\ref{def:K} reads plus the cap $W$, an admissible cutoff
reads $E_{\mathrm{low}}<\alpha\Lambda_C$, and at its witness gap
$\varsigma$,
\[
E_{\mathrm{low}}+\varsigma\;=\;\alpha\Lambda_C,
\]
the removed block's floor sits at least $\varsigma$ above each level.
A compression lowers each count below a line (Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}),
raising each low level; and the cap's two splits, read at the vector
$Wx+\varsigma y$ and at its partner $Wx+\varsigma\,\check y$, $\check
y$ the memberwise swap of $y$, $x$ in the head and $y$ in the
removed block, collect to the polarization:
the sum $2\langle x,\,\beta M\,y\rangle+c_W\langle
x,x\rangle+\varsigma\langle y,y\rangle$ reads on its upper side or of equal
members, at the pair weights $W$,
$\varsigma$ and the \emph{shift cap} $c_W$, the cofactor at
$\varsigma\,c_W=W^2$ (Definition~\ref{def:ground}, $\varsigma$
the admissible cutoffs' own gap), so the shifted split reads
$H+c_W\!\oplus\!\varsigma\ \succeq\ H_{11}\!\oplus\!H_{22}$
with the removed block's floor above every level, counts are monotone
under $\succeq$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}), and each level's downward
shift is at most $c_W$; the level
$E_0\mathcal K$ is the two jump reads' gap, so the two cuts' values
bracket each other at the priced width,
\[
E_0\,\varsigma\,\mathcal K\ \le\ E_0\,\varsigma\,\mathcal K_\Lambda
+\beta^2\,\#p^2\,d_\theta^{\,2} ,
\qquad
E_0\,\varsigma\,\mathcal K_\Lambda\ \le\ E_0\,\varsigma\,\mathcal K
+\beta^2\,\#p^2\,d_\theta^{\,2} ,
\]
degree matched on the ray as the level is, the two reads' bracket
priced by one pair comparison each way at every $\alpha$, the
member's adjoint dimension ($r^2(r+2)^2$ at the $A$-series) and
the region's plaquette count its data: the truncation is certifiable at every window size with exact
atoms, and the window cuts approach $\mathcal K$ with a modulus rather than
along a subsequence. The gap chain reads the modulus at the $\alpha{=}1$ representative, and the
display is homogeneous on the ray (Proposition~\ref{prop:segment}), every
representative's read one rescaling of it.

\end{theorem}

\begin{lemma}[The dual truncation]\label{lem:dualtrunc}
At the coupling $[\,\sigma:1\,]$ at a pair $\sigma$, the cap, the
floor and the compression of
Theorem~\ref{thm:truncation} read with one substitution, the
two terms' roles exchanged in the bound's inputs: the off-block coupling
and the removed block's magnetic part are capped as forms by a $W$ of the
fusion data (the fusion form's two-sided dimension cap on a label
index,
Lemma~\ref{lem:fpcap}; per plaquette term over its stencil at
the member's $d_\theta$,
Lemma~\ref{lem:loopcap} with Lemma~\ref{lem:stableentries}), $W$
linear in the stencil's term count, at most the per-vertex
plaquette count's fold over the support
(Construction~\ref{con:lattice}), the removed
block's electric diagonal sits at or above $\sigma\Lambda_C$, and each
level shifts downward under the compression by at most the
shift cap $c_W$, the cofactor at $\varsigma\,c_W=W^2$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:truncation}), at the order
$\lambda+W<\sigma\Lambda_C$'s witness gap $\varsigma$,
$\lambda+W+\varsigma=\sigma\Lambda_C$. A count at level $\lambda$ is
therefore pinned on the finite head whenever the bracket
$[\lambda,\ \lambda+c_W]$ is clear of the head's divisor
(Lemma~\ref{lem:cellcount}, the level as the parameter): every count of
the dual pencil, the pair $(\sigma E\!:\!M)$'s site datum
$H([\,\sigma:1\,])$, lives on a finite head
pencil, at every cutoff with
\[
\sigma\,\Lambda_C\ >\ \lambda+W+c_\vartheta
\qquad\text{at}\qquad \vartheta\,c_\vartheta=W^2 ,
\]
one pair comparison at a clearance pair $\vartheta$, the admissible set
an output whose floor rises toward the contact end. The transfer is one-sided: the compression lowers counts and raises
levels, each level's downward shift capped at $c_W$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:truncation}; Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}), so between two
head jumps the lower comparison reads at its equality, the head ground at
or above the full one, and the clearance rides the upper jump alone: the
admissible levels are those beyond the ground bracket's top whose
sum with $c_W$ sits below the second
jump's bottom, the measured gap spent on one side.

\emph{The count beyond every cutoff.} The display defines the dual
pencil's count at $\lambda$ over the unbounded content range: at a
level whose bracket $[\lambda,\ \lambda+c_W]$ is clear of
the head's divisor, it is the head count at any cutoff beyond the
display's bound, and two admissible cutoffs read one integer: the
smaller head is the larger head's own truncation (the projections
nest), the display runs on the larger head at the one cap $W$ (a
compression of a cap is a cap, Lemma~\ref{lem:inertia}) with the
removed block's floor at least $\varsigma$, and the clear bracket
pins the count both times.

\emph{The seam identity.} The ray at end weights $[\,q^2:p^2\,]$
